# Brand World GP - Founder Source Pack (Markdown mirror)

Mirrored from the Founder-supplied `Brand World GP.zip` on 1 October 2026 for Claude Code via the `agent-handoff` branch.

**Authority:** these sections reproduce the Markdown source files from the ZIP verbatim. The ZIP also contains PDF visual/reference artefacts, prototype HTML/CSS/JS and font binaries. Those binary/visual files are not embedded in this Markdown mirror; visual/layout decisions must continue to defer to the original files supplied by the Founder.

<!-- SOURCE FILE: GL-2026-07-26-01-RATIFICATION-RECORD.md | SHA256: e604dd128c6410e65de18ac7514963468903510998b5cbafda19d79964ad1b9b -->
# SOURCE FILE: `GL-2026-07-26-01-RATIFICATION-RECORD.md`

# GOVERNANCE LOG ENTRY GL-2026-07-26-01
## Ratification of Brand World v2.1 · Retirement of Brand Bible v1.1

**Date:** 26 July 2026 · **Author:** CR (Founder) · **Class:** A (standing) · **Status:** Recorded, append-only
**Authority:** Charter Art. IV.1, IV.2 (reserved matters) · Art. V.2 (errata) · Art. I.13 (preservation)

---

## 1. The decision recorded

The Founder, having reviewed Brand World v2.1, the Brand Bible Migration Report v1.0 and the associated constitutional findings, formally approved the completed migration.

**Effective immediately:**
1. **Brand World v2.1 is ratified** as the sole constitutional authority governing GetPromptly's institutional identity and expression.
2. **Brand Bible v1.1 is retired** as a governing document and reclassified as a **Historical Founding Source Document**.
3. **Brand Bible v1.1 remains preserved** as part of institutional memory and is no longer treated as the authoritative source for identity decisions.

**The Founder further confirmed** that the Migration Report accurately records the constitutional disposition of the migrated material; that the omitted provisions were correctly excluded on constitutional grounds; and that the retained, revised, merged and archived material faithfully preserves the intent of Brand Bible v1.1 while aligning it with the Constitutional Charter and the Unified Operating System.

**Architecture affirmed by the Founder:** Constitutional Charter → Unified Operating System → Brand World, as the constitutional, operational and institutional-expression instruments of GetPromptly.

---

## 2. Founder Action recorded

**Subject:** the inherited outreach template at Brand World §26.13 and §23.2.
**Action:** the wording shall be reviewed before implementation to ensure that vendor communications cannot reasonably be interpreted as offering a pathway to a higher score or a favourable review outcome.
**Classification:** implementation refinement. **It does not affect ratification.**
**Owner:** CR, under Charter Art. I.2 and Operating System §1.4.
**Operational consequence recorded in the instrument:** until the review is complete and recorded, the template is not used.

---

## 3. Consequential errata applied

Applied under Charter Art. V.2 and Art. III.3. Each corrects a reference of fact; none alters meaning, and none required an amendment.

| Ref | Instrument | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Erratum 01a | Constitutional Charter, Schedule A | Class (d) now names Brand World v2.1; class (e) now names the amendment register and programme documents; a historical row records Brand Bible v1.1 as retired and non-authoritative. Schedule A is erratum-updatable by its own terms. |
| Erratum 01b | Unified Operating System §0.2 | The class (d) instrument is named as Brand World v2.1; the Brand Bible's retirement to historical status is noted. |
| Erratum 01c | Brand World v2.1 | Status changed from pending to ratified and in force; §0.2 states the instrument *is* the identity constitution; §26.1 marked discharged with numbering preserved so existing references remain valid; §26.13 carries the Founder Action; the ratification block records the decision. |

**Note on effect.** Where an instrument receiving an erratum is not itself yet in force, the erratum takes effect with that instrument.

---

## 4. Register of what is now in force

| Class | Instrument | Status |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Charter | Constitutional Charter v1.0 | Complete; ratification signature outstanding (§5.1) |
| (a-i) Originating authority | Founder Decision Record v1.0 | Ratified |
| (a-ii) Interpretive authority | Declaration of Belief v1.0 | Founder Approved |
| (b) Governance | Unified Operating System v2.0 | Complete; ratification signature outstanding (§5.1) |
| (c) Methodology | Score standard and assessment model v2.2 | In force |
| (d) Identity constitution | **Brand World v2.1** | **Ratified and in force, 26 July 2026** |
| (e) Strategic / programme | Consolidated Amendment Register Rev A | Unapplied |
| Historical | Brand Bible v1.1 | **Retired 26 July 2026**; preserved, not authoritative |

---

## 5. Records matters outstanding

*Recorded because Charter Art. IV provides that approvals are dated and recorded, and that silence approves nothing.*

**5.1 — Ratification signatures for the Charter and the Operating System.** The Founder's decision expressly ratifies Brand World v2.1 and affirms the three-document architecture. The Charter and the Operating System each carry their own ratification block, which remains unsigned, and each states its own preconditions: the Charter requires confirmation of the Article I enumeration and of Schedule A; the Operating System requires the Schedule A reconciliation of any v1.3.1 provision not visible to the drafter. Brand World v2.1 draws its authority from the Charter, so the Charter's own ratification should not lag the instrument that depends on it. A single short confirmation would discharge both. This is a records-completeness matter, not a challenge to the decision recorded above, and it does not affect Brand World's ratification.

**5.2 — The commercial instrument.** Commercial operations remain constrained by the Charter and unauthorised by any ratified instrument (Operating System §24.1). The Founder Action at §2 sits adjacent to this gap.

**5.3 — Succession.** Charter Art. VI.3 requires provision for succession of the guardianship; none exists operationally (Operating System §24.7).

**5.4 — Thirteen open matters** at Brand World §§26.2–26.14, and the ten existing plus thirteen proposed decisions in the Operating System's ledger, all unchanged by this entry.

---

## 6. Preservation

Brand Bible v1.1 is preserved intact and marked historic under Charter Art. I.13. It is not edited, not deleted, and remains readable as the founding source from which the identity constitution derives. Its Manifesto, its version history, its mark construction record and the reasoning behind each omitted provision are retained in the archive and in the Migration Report.

*Entry closed. Append-only: any correction to this entry is made by a further entry, never by alteration.*

# SOURCE FILE: `GetPromptly-Logo-Territory-Jury.md`

# GetPromptly Logo Territory Jury
**Baseline:** GetPromptly — The Distinctive Brand World **v1.1** (canonical, 25 July 2026), §9 as applied (R9–R17), with the Symbol Distinctiveness Loop and Handoff as canonical sources.
**Scope discipline:** findings, failures, exact anchored amendments (LJ1–LJ5, proposed, none applied), and open decisions. No open founder decision is converted; D1–D14, TR1–TR11 and AS1–AS12 remain unapplied, and conditional stacks are labelled.
**Jury integrity note:** no artwork exists, so this is a desk jury, and it says so. Every rating below carries its basis — **[E]** evidence-cited · **[S]** structural inference from the written specifications · **[P]** prototype-dependent prediction that only the battery can decide. Ratings are **S** Strong · **A** Adequate · **W** Weak · **R** At-risk. No numerals: the house rule is that figures must be itemisable, and a jury without prototypes has no measurements — the battery produces those.

---

## Part 1 — The jury matrix

| Criterion | 1 Audit Stamp | 2 Five-Pillar Seal | 3 Marked-Paper Monogram | 4 Evidence Aperture | 5 Verdict Compass |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Strategic meaning | S [E] | S [S] | S [S] | A [S] | W [S] |
| GetPromptly ownership | S [S] | S [S] | S [E] | W [S] | W [S] |
| Five-second recognition | A [P] | A [P] | A [P] | A [P] | W [E] |
| 16px performance | S [S] | R [S] | A [S] | R [S] | S [S] |
| Monochrome performance | S [S] | A [S] | A [S] | A [S] | S [S] |
| Print performance | S [S] | S [S] | W [S] | A [S] | A [S] |
| Pillar Card compatibility | S [S] | S [S] | A [S] | W [S] | W [S] |
| Methodology Mark compatibility | S [S] | A [S] | W [S] | W [S] | W [S] |
| Social-avatar performance | R [P] | S [S] | A [S] | R [S] | S [S] |
| Accessibility | S [S] | A [S] | S [S] | A [S] | S [S] |
| Category distinctiveness | S [E] | S [E] | S [E] | A [E] | R [E] |
| Certification-implication risk | R [S] — managed | R [S] — managed | S [S] | S [S] | S [S] |
| Governmental-look risk | R [S] — managed | A [S] | S [S] | S [S] | S [S] |
| Generic-AI resemblance risk | S [E] | A [S] | S [E] | A [S] | R [E] |
| Long-term durability | S [S] | R [S] — new finding | A [S] | R [S] | W [S] |

Reading the matrix honestly: the execution criteria (rows 3–6, 9, 10) are predictions the battery will overrule where it disagrees; the structural criteria (rows 1, 2, 7, 8, 11–15) are decidable now and decide the jury. On structural criteria the Stamp wins eight of ten, loses none outright, and carries its two At-risk entries as *managed* risks with named mitigations and a named dependency (Part 2, F-notes). The Compass wins only the criteria any simple mark wins.

**Per-territory jury notes (what the matrix compresses):**

**1 · Audit Stamp.** Strongest on every criterion that ties the mark to the operating system: it formalises the Methodology Mark already in production, houses the receipt header and reviewer stamp, and is the only territory the canonical audit found that can function as both logo and everyday instrument. Its two risks are cultural, not formal — stamps *mean* approval in the public mind, and heavy frames read municipal — and both already carry mitigations in §9 (records-never-approves grammar; editorial line-weights; rejection criteria (a)–(c)). The jury's addition: the first risk is not merely mitigated by the stamp doctrine, it is **dependent** on it — see F3 and LJ3. Avatar clipping is the known formal hazard; the test exists.

**2 · Five-Pillar Seal.** The deepest IP tie and the strongest avatar, with the canonical distinctiveness case on its side (no comparator uses a five-part seal bound to a named methodology). Two jury findings sharpen it. First, language: "seal of approval" is the English idiom — the internal territory name collides head-on with §10.8's most-guarded prohibitions, so the mark must never be publicly *called* a seal (F2, LJ2). Second, durability: the Seal hard-codes the number five into the master mark, in a system whose methodology has a public amendment pipeline. If the pillar count can ever change, the logo inherits methodology-versioning risk the Stamp does not carry; whether the count is constitutionally fixed is a question for the methodology owner, not this jury (F1, LJ2, open decision). Its 16px thesis test ("five-ness legible or the territory falsifies itself") stands as written.

**3 · Marked-Paper Monogram.** The canonical audit's "highest conceptual departure from every comparator located", clean on all three imitation risks, and the humane member of the family — but structurally a secondary mark: underpowered on report covers, no relation to the Methodology Mark, redundancy beside the wordmark. The jury's use for it is precisely that shape: the **reserve** (Part 3, LJ1) — a genuinely distinctive route held at zero cost, activating only on double failure, which is what a reserve is for.

**4 · Evidence Aperture.** Retained fourth per the canonical hierarchy and this jury does not relitigate R13: the canonical rating is medium-high, the expectation of failure remains labelled interpretation, and the named tests (lens/aperture line-up in privacy and photography branding; surveillance-read panel) decide it if it is ever reached. The jury adds one durability observation already implicit in v1.1: the surveillance read is a *worsening* liability for a safeguarding brand as monitoring-in-schools debates sharpen — a reason to leave it unfunded in round one, not a reason to pre-judge it.

**5 · Verdict Compass.** The matrix shows its true shape: it passes exactly the criteria any simple needle passes (small, mono, avatar) and fails the criteria the retirement exists to protect — meaning, ownership, distinctiveness, AI-adjacency. Its sole strategic virtue is continuity with a mark retired for genericity, and §9 already contains its own sentence: a viewer calling it "the old logo, tidied" violates the commissioning instruction by definition. The jury's finding is structural: as the designated last-resort fallback it is not neutral insurance — it is a standing pull back toward the starburst, the exact sunk-cost risk §15 names. With the Monogram available as a distinctive reserve, the Compass's insurance function is better served by a route that doesn't undo the retirement. Elimination proposed — as an approval item, because it departs from the canonical fallback designation (LJ1, Part 4).

---

## Part 2 — Failures

**F1 — The Seal is never tested against the methodology's own future.** v1.1 tests the Seal against flowers, spinners and favicons but never against the system's amendment pipeline: "the methodology is the mark" cuts both ways, and a master mark that hard-codes five is a versioning liability unless the pillar count is constitutionally fixed — a fact this jury does not know and will not assert (→ LJ2; open decision for the methodology owner).

**F2 — The challenger's name is the banned idiom.** "Seal of approval" is what English speakers say; §10.8 bans every approved/certified construction; nothing in v1.1 stops the territory's internal name leaking into public copy (→ LJ2).

**F3 — The Stamp's key mitigation is an unadopted decision.** §9's "records, never approves" grammar is the load-bearing answer to the certification read, and it is §19.6 — open. v1.1 treats the doctrine as ambient; the jury makes the dependency explicit: the Stamp should not pass Gate 4 while its central risk mitigation is unratified (→ LJ3).

**F4 — Gate 4's reader criterion has no protocol.** "Any test reader describes it as approved/certified" is a rejection criterion with no defined reader, sample, or method — as written it is untestable and therefore unenforceable (→ LJ4, LJ5).

**F5 — Five-second recognition is unmeasurable for an unlaunched mark, and v1.1 never says what stands in for it.** No one recognises a mark that doesn't exist yet; the canonical five-second finding assessed the *current* starburst. The testable proxies must be named: category-read (evidence institution v AI tool v approval body) and single-exposure attribution (→ LJ4).

---

## Part 3 — Verdicts

**Prototype first: Territory 1, the Audit Stamp.** Consistent with the canonical sequencing and confirmed, not inherited: it wins the structural criteria, requires the least invention, and is the only route that doubles as the instrument the Defensibility work keeps pointing at — with the explicit condition that its doctrine dependency (F3) is resolved before Gate 4, not after.

**Fallback: Territory 2, the Five-Pillar Seal** — exactly as v1.1 already stages it: the head-to-head challenger in the same battery round, becoming primary if the Stamp fails any rejection criterion. The jury adds its two conditions (naming rule; pillar-count question answered) to the head-to-head, changing nothing else in §15's plan.

**Eliminate immediately: Territory 5, the Verdict Compass** — proposed by LJ1, requiring founder approval because it departs from the canonical fallback designation. On adoption, the reserve function passes to the Monogram (Territory 3), which activates only if both Stamp and Seal fail. If declined, the canonical structure stands unchanged and this verdict is recorded as jury opinion only.

**Unfunded but retained: Territory 4, the Evidence Aperture** — fourth, no round-one budget, canonical rating intact, its named tests waiting if ever reached.

---

## Part 4 — Required amendments (exact text; anchors verbatim from v1.1)

**LJ1 — §9, Territory 5: elimination and reserve reassignment.** *Requires founder approval — departs from the Symbol Distinctiveness Loop's fallback designation.* *Replace* "**Prototype requirements:** the canonical fallback condition — only if both Territory 1 (Audit Stamp) and Territory 2 (Five-Pillar Seal) fail testing; then the universal battery + explicit differencing from the retired starburst (if a viewer calls it "the old logo, tidied", it has violated the commissioning instruction by definition)." *with* →
> **Prototype requirements:** none — eliminated by the Logo Territory Jury (approval recorded [date]): a fallback that fails the commissioning instruction by construction is not insurance but a standing pull back toward the retired mark, the sunk-cost risk §15 names. The reserve function passes to Territory 3 (Marked-Paper Monogram), which activates only if both Territory 1 and Territory 2 fail their batteries. If this elimination is declined, the canonical fallback condition stands and this entry reverts.

**LJ2 — §9, Territory 2: naming rule and durability question.** *Insert after* "Its stated rating, moderate small-size risk, is operationalised by the five-notch ≤24px test above." →
> Jury additions: (a) Naming rule — the mark is never publicly called a seal; "seal of approval" is the English idiom, and §10.8 bans the family. Public vocabulary on adoption: "the Pillar Mark" or the plain "our mark", decided at Gate 4. (b) Durability question, referred to the methodology owner before the head-to-head concludes: is the pillar count constitutionally fixed? A master mark that hard-codes five inherits methodology-versioning risk if it is not — a liability the Stamp does not carry, and a fact this brief does not assert either way.

**LJ3 — §9, Territory 1: doctrine dependency.** *Insert after* "**Rejection criteria:** fails if (a) at 16px it is indistinguishable from a generic app-container icon; (b) any test reader describes it as "approved/certified"; (c) the family derivations require more than one new rule each." →
> Jury condition: criterion (b)'s mitigation — the records-never-approves grammar — is the open §19.6 doctrine. The Stamp does not pass Gate 4 while that doctrine is unratified; adopt it first or the territory's central risk stands unmanaged.

**LJ4 — §9, universal battery: reader-panel protocol and recognition proxies.** *Insert after* "The Loop's five-second finding frames the whole battery: current recognition is wordmark-dependent, not icon-dependent, so the icon-only items above are the decisive ones." →
> Reader-panel protocol (defines Gate 4's reader criterion): [X] readers, role-mixed — teacher, safeguarding lead, parent, school leader, IT — each given one five-second, icon-only exposure and asked, unprompted, what the organisation is and does. Fail terms, recorded verbatim: approved, certified, official, government, app, AI, camera, loading, spinner. Recognition proxies for an unlaunched mark (no one recognises what doesn't exist yet): category-read — evidence/record institution beats AI tool beats approval body — and single-exposure attribution on second sight. Panel results are battery evidence, filed with the Gate 4 record.

**LJ5 — §14, Gate 4 inputs.** *Replace* "Territory prototypes + full battery results + confusion line-up + formal design-similarity re-test report" *with* →
> Territory prototypes + full battery results + confusion line-up + reader-panel report (§9 protocol) + formal design-similarity re-test report

---

## Part 5 — The designer testing plan (no polished artwork)

**Phase 0 — Briefing pack (Day 0).** The designer receives: v1.1 §9 in full (as applied); the Symbol Distinctiveness Loop verbatim; this jury document; the constraints list — container ban (standing), wordmark lockup at current proportions, no ticks/approved glyphs/crests/gradients/spark geometry, and (conditional on D9, unapplied) no dummy data in record slots — templates ship empty or real. Explicit commission: structural exploration, not artwork.

**Phase 1 — Structural sketches (Weeks 1–2, per §15's days 31–60 window, unchanged).** Greyscale only; colour earns entry by passing, it doesn't decorate failure. Round one funds two territories: **Stamp ×3 constructions** (rule-structure variants: double-rule, interrupted-rule, slot-forward) and **Seal ×3 constructions** (segment-end and safeguarding-weight variants), each delivered at 16px, 24px, 120px lockup, 8mm print and 400px circular crop. **Monogram: zero spend** — reserve status. **Aperture and Compass: zero spend** (the latter pending LJ1's decision either way — its canonical trigger requires a double failure that hasn't happened).

**Phase 2 — The battery (Weeks 3–4).** The §9 universal battery verbatim, plus: the reader panel per LJ4 ([X] readers, fail terms recorded); the confusion line-up as amended (Gemini's spark the closest conceptual neighbour, Perplexity's asterisk the most strategically uncomfortable, Claude-family marks, three stock starbursts); the Seal's spinner line-up and five-notch favicon count; the Stamp's circular-crop clip test and photocopy pass; the formal design-similarity tool re-test on every surviving construction (Gate 4 input, already applied); the Seal naming-rule check in all test materials (never "seal" in anything a reader sees).

**Phase 3 — Decision artefacts (Week 5).** Per construction: a one-page battery report against the §9 rejection criteria, pass/fail per criterion, reader-panel verbatims attached. Per territory: a recommendation memo in the house register — verdict first, evidence itemised. Then the Gate 4 sitting per §14 as amended (LJ5), with the §19.6 doctrine question resolved beforehand (LJ3) or the sitting postponed. Explicitly excluded from all phases: polished artwork, colourways beyond mono plus one lime-accent test, brand applications, motion beyond a single stamp-down sketch (itself still awaiting §7 approval).

---

## Part 6 — Decisions that remain unresolved

Existing and untouched: **§19.5** (prototyping budget and sequence — this plan is the jury's input; the spend is the founder's); **§19.6** (the stamp doctrine — now a named precondition of the Stamp's own gate, LJ3, which raises its urgency without deciding it); the Gate 4 decision itself remains the §15 days 61–90 gate. New, awaiting the founder: **LJ1** (Compass elimination and reserve reassignment — declining it restores canon exactly); the **Seal's public name** if it wins (LJ2a, decided at Gate 4); the **pillar-count constitutional question** (LJ2b — the methodology owner's answer, not the brand document's); the **reader-panel size** [X] (a budget-and-rigour trade the founder sets). Conditional stacks: the dummy-data constraint in Phase 0 rides on D9 (unapplied); nothing here touches §19.1–19.4 or 19.7–19.10, and D1–D14, TR1–TR11, AS1–AS12 all remain proposed.

*Logo Territory Jury · 25 July 2026 · run against v1.1 · amendments LJ1–LJ5 proposed, none applied · — for CR, GetPromptly*

<!-- SOURCE FILE: GetPromptly-Unified-Operating-System-v1 2.md | SHA256: 1c43c11493f36a4e3c5690175029c0fbbebcee00cde36d0a05a4071b1d888bac -->
# SOURCE FILE: `GetPromptly-Unified-Operating-System-v1 2.md`

# GetPromptly — Unified Operating System v1.2

**Membership Strategy (v1.2) × 8-Week Activation Roadmap (v1.1), merged into one plan. Supersedes Unified OS v1.1.**
**Methodology v2.1 · 5-Pillar Trust Framework v1.1 · Brand Bible v1.1 · British English · Reviewed against KCSIE 2025, never “compliant.”**
**Date:** 11 June 2026 · **Owners:** CR + CD · **Review:** monthly at the Monday review · **Status:** execution-ready

-----

## 1. Executive Summary

GetPromptly is live and the destination has changed. The reviews are no longer the product; they are the evidence base underneath the product. What schools will buy is **defensibility** — the ability to adopt AI and prove to governors, DPOs and inspectors that they did it properly. The strategic system (v1.2) sets that destination: school and MAT membership revenue from January 2027, with the **Validator as the near-term bridge** that funds the build, and **affiliate as petrol money** — useful, never load-bearing.

The next eight weeks are Phase 1 of that strategy, executed with the discipline of the activation roadmap. The work is to build the trust layer in public, land the re-graded scores cleanly, stand up the artefact layer (receipts, Pillar Cards, living pages) that the membership will sell, grow the Compliance Calendar audience that the membership will convert, and prepare Pilot Zero — all behind the Donna gate, all on confirmed consent, and none of it touching a verdict.

**The re-grade is the hinge of the eight weeks, and it is two things at once.** Operationally, it is the highest-stakes event in the plan: roughly 220 downgrades, gated by an approved communication plan and the registry→site re-sync, published once and cleanly, every downgrade signed off by CR + CD. Strategically, it is the founding credibility asset of the membership: the public, dated proof that scores are not for sale, which will lead every sales conversation from January onwards. Same facts, both framings — the caution of the activation roadmap, the ambition of the membership strategy. Neither half is optional.

Membership groundwork leads from Week 1: tier names, founding prices and the VAT position decided in thirty minutes; the Pilot Zero agreement drafted; the library cadence and founder caps set before the first asset is drafted. Affiliate keeps its hygiene (the registry, the gates, the fail-closed wiring) but loses its prominence — applications wait until Week 3, links until Week 5, and both run as background tasks earning petrol money while the membership work takes the attention.

Week 1 remains deliberately light and exact, and everything external passes Donna first. **The discipline is the product.** The plan’s enemy is impatience in months 7–9 when school revenue looks thin. The calendar and the Validator bridge say hold, so hold.

### How to use this document

Read **§2 once** and revisit it monthly at the Monday review — it is the destination, and it changes rarely. Work weekly from **§§3–5**: the snapshot, the week-by-week plan, and the workstream detail. **§6 is the law**: the sequencing rules, the Donna checklist and the founder-time caps are stated once there and cited by number everywhere else — where a table entry says “through the §5.6 gate” or “rule 4 satisfied”, the full text lives in §§5.6 and 6.1 and is deliberately not repeated.

**§§8–9 are the daily surface**: tick the checklists, and treat *Do Not Do Yet* as binding. Where this document and the Brand Bible v1.1 diverge, the Brand Bible wins.

-----

## 2. Strategic Direction (condensed from v1.2)

### 2.1 Positioning

The evolution: from “independent AI review site” to **“the AI compliance and adoption membership for UK schools and trusts.”**

**Core promise:** *Adopt AI with a paper trail.* Every tool reviewed against KCSIE 2025, every decision documented, every update tracked — so a school can move forward and defend every step.

|vs                      |Their weakness                                             |Our edge                                                                                                  |
|------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|The Key                 |Broad statutory coverage; AI thinly covered, annual cadence|Depth and currency on the one topic moving too fast for them; living scores, not annual PDFs              |
|Generic AI directories  |No methodology, no UK lens, no compliance artefacts        |Published methodology, the re-grade record, receipts a DPO can file                                       |
|Vendor “trust centres”  |Marking their own homework                                 |Independence — scores are not for sale, and we have publicly lowered scores on household names to prove it|
|LA/MAT internal guidance|Slow, inconsistent, unfunded                               |Updated weekly by agents, consistent nationally, cheaper than one INSET day                               |

### 2.2 The independence architecture

- **Schools pay** for access and artefacts (the library, receipts, briefs, Calendar Pro). Their money buys convenience, never influence.
- **Vendors pay** for methodology engagement (the Validator) — structurally separated from public scores per Brand Bible §21, Donna-enforced: delivered as a private report, never published, never moving the public Promptly Score, badge worded “Methodology Engagement” without the score.
- **The standing line** on every paid surface: *no paid placements; scores are not for sale.* The re-grade is the proof, and it leads every sales conversation.
- **What stays out of paid tiers, permanently:** scores, reviews, and the methodology. The moment a score sits behind a paywall, the independence story dies.

### 2.3 Pricing architecture

**Principle:** stay under the procurement-committee threshold. Price for a credit-card-or-single-invoice decision, not a tender. Confirm the ex/inc VAT position before any launch copy is written.

|Tier               |Price (yr 1 founding → standard)                           |What’s included                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|-------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|**Free**           |£0                                                         |Full public directory + scores, Compliance Calendar weekly email, one demo receipt, public methodology                                                                                                   |
|**School**         |£295 → £395/yr                                             |Everything free, plus: full template and policy library, unlimited Audit Receipts (school-branded, filed history), Luna Procurement Briefs on demand, Calendar Pro, webinar library, priority Luna access|
|**MAT**            |£1,250 → £1,950/yr (to ~10 schools, then per-school add-on)|Everything School ×N, plus trust-level dashboard, central DPO receipt archive, one bespoke comparison brief per term, onboarding call                                                                    |
|**Enterprise / LA**|£4k+, demand-led                                           |API access to scores, white-label receipts, co-branded CPD. Build only when someone asks twice                                                                                                           |

**Vendor side (separated):** Validator Tiers 1–3 per the existing plan. Validator insights are never a school-tier feature; school data is never a vendor-tier feature.

**Activation metric:** track **time-to-first-receipt** for new School-tier members — the strongest leading indicator of renewal.

### 2.4 Phased roadmap to revenue

Anchored to the school calendar: pilots in autumn term 2026, paid launch January 2027 to catch spring budget-setting, material revenue from April 2027 (maintained-school year) and September 2027 (academy year).

|Phase                        |Window           |Headline                                                                                                                                                                         |This document covers                                 |
|-----------------------------|-----------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|
|**1 — Foundation & Audience**|Jun–Aug 2026     |Build, don’t sell. Calendar to 800–1,200 subscribers; re-grade story published; receipts live; Pilot Zero signed; membership MVP functional; first Validator conversations booked|**Yes — the 8-week plan below is the Phase 1 engine**|
|2 — Pilot Cohort & Library   |Sep–Nov 2026     |5–10 Founding Schools; library to ~40 assets (max 2/week, founder-gated); first webinar; first 3–5 Validator deals (£5–12k cum.); Luna Briefs for members                        |Prepared in Weeks 6–8                                |
|3 — Paid Launch              |Dec 2026–Feb 2027|January launch at founding rates; 10–25 paying schools by end Feb; churn machinery built before the first renewal needs it; MAT conversations open                               |Out of scope here                                    |
|4 — Compounding              |Mar–Jun 2027     |40–80 paying schools, 1–3 MAT deals, Validator at 10–20 vendors; the renewal moment executed as a value demonstration                                                            |Out of scope here                                    |

### 2.5 Revenue bridge and the honest maths

|Stream                    |M0–3       |M4–6       |M7–9        |M10–12      |Role                                              |
|--------------------------|-----------|-----------|------------|------------|--------------------------------------------------|
|Affiliate                 |£100–400/mo|£300–800/mo|£500–1k/mo  |Similar     |Petrol money; funds API costs and ads             |
|**Validator (the bridge)**|£0         |£5–12k cum.|£15–30k cum.|£25–50k cum.|**Funds the entire build phase**                  |
|School subs               |£0         |£0         |£3–8k ARR   |£12–30k ARR |**The destination** — meaningful, not yet material|
|MAT                       |£0         |£0         |£0–2k       |£2–8k ARR   |Real money lands FY27/28                          |

Free list → paid runs 1–3% in this market over a year. 3,000 Calendar subscribers supports 30–90 paying schools by late 2027. That is the honest maths, and it is enough.

### 2.6 Kill / slow criteria (both sides)

- **Vendor side:** if the Validator sits below ~£10k cumulative by month 6, slow the membership build. School conversion lag is expected; vendor reluctance is diagnostic.
- **School side:** if fewer than half the pilot schools are generating at least one receipt a month by December, the product has an activation problem — fix it before launching paid, even if that moves the launch to spring.

### 2.7 Pilot Zero — used properly, and only this way

Real school, real staffroom, structured feedback, named case study agreement. The teacher voice becomes a recurring Calendar feature (“From the classroom”). Warm intros yield 1–4 pilot leads, not a channel. **Limit:** one school’s perspective, and she has a day job — the plan never depends on her for more than this. The wider pilot cohort is recruited from the Calendar list, not the network.

## 4. The 8-Week Activation Plan

The original eight weeks, re-pointed at the membership destination. Three changes of emphasis, none of discipline:

1. **Membership groundwork leads.** Tier decisions, Pilot Zero, the Calendar audience, the artefact layer and the library now occupy the front of each week.
1. **The re-grade carries its dual role** (§1): Week 3 delivers both halves — the cleanly sequenced operational publish, then its repackaging as the credibility story.
1. **Affiliate runs in the background.** Registry hygiene stays in Week 1 (cheap, prevents accidents); applications begin Week 3; first links Week 5 — only ever through the §5.6 gate.

### 4.2 Week 8 exit criteria (Phase 1 milestones)

- [ ] Compliance Calendar at or tracking towards 800–1,200 subscribers
- [ ] Re-grade published once, cleanly, with the methodology-update note — and repackaged as the credibility story
- [ ] Audit Receipts + Living Pages live on re-graded scores; demo receipt downloadable
- [ ] Pilot Zero agreement signed; “From the classroom” running; feedback call #1 booked
- [ ] Membership MVP quiet-live (auth + Stripe + invoice/PO + gated library + receipt history), both payment paths tested
- [ ] Webinar #1 built and scheduled for late September
- [ ] 10–12 library assets approved (the 2/week cap held, not exceeded)
- [ ] Founding Schools email drafted, Donna-passed, held for its send window (§5.1)
- [ ] First Validator conversations booked (vendor revenue starts here, not school revenue)
- [ ] Oliver stable at ramped volume on clean deliverability; Donna v3.1 and legacy v1 retired
- [ ] Affiliate: registry gated, first links live only on published reviews — earning quietly, demanding no attention

### 4.3 Top 20 Review Prioritisation Method

Score each candidate 1–5 on six factors, weight, rank. This decides **scoring order** first; affiliate application order is a by-product, never a driver.

|Factor                        |Weight|What a high score means                                       |
|------------------------------|------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
|Teacher demand                |25%   |Frequently asked about; real classroom pull                   |
|UK education relevance        |20%   |Fits UK curriculum, KCSIE context, school workflows           |
|Evidence availability         |20%   |DPA, privacy, safeguarding, accessibility docs accessible     |
|Affiliate/partner availability|15%   |`affiliate_status = found` or willing vendor exists           |
|Risk level *(inverse)*        |10%   |Lower risk scores higher; pupil-facing/data-heavy scores lower|
|Search/social potential       |10%   |Likely to draw traffic and Score Reveal interest              |

**Rule:** evidence availability and risk decide scoring order; affiliate availability never promotes a tool up the queue. A high commission rate cannot move a tool above a better-evidenced one. Bot-walled vendors (OpenAI, likely Google/Microsoft) need human-supplied policy text — factor that into evidence availability honestly. Weight a glance towards tools the pilot cohort will actually adopt — a scored tool a Founding School uses generates receipts, and receipts are the activation metric.

**Founder-time check (Weeks 6–8):** everything above is agent-built and founder-approved. The founder touch is: asset approvals (≤ 2 hrs/week, shared CR/CD), the webinar approval (≤ 1 hr, once), the recruitment email approval (CR, minutes), the flagship plan review (30 min), and booking — not yet holding — the Pilot Zero call. The §6.3 caps hold.

**Founding Schools send window — the timing rule:** late August / early September, flexible within that window, but gated on two proofs: **the re-grade has published** and **receipts are live**. *Rationale:* v1.2 placed this in the 30–60 day window, and leaders do read email in August — but this email converts on evidence, not timing. A recruitment ask without a downloadable receipt and the published re-grade behind it burns the list’s best moment. Early September also lands beside the KCSIE update, when AI policy is on every SLT agenda, and catches teachers back at work. If both proofs are live early, the leader segment may receive it in late August; the teacher segment waits for term.

### 5.2 Warm Lead Nurture → Compliance Calendar (the sales team)

The honest funnel: **Calendar email → webinar/template download → pilot or trial → paid.** Schools buy from brands they have received useful free things from for months. There is no shortcut; the Calendar is the sales team, and the 5,000 Brevo leads are its seed audience.

Value-first, verdict-led. No affiliate links in early emails. **Nothing sends until consent/provenance is confirmed AND the leads are confirmed in Brevo** (§6.1 rule 1).

|Email      |Angle                                                                          |CTA                          |Timing                              |
|-----------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|------------------------------------|
|**E1**     |One real verdict a head could quote at SLT; invitation onto the weekly Calendar|*Read the methodology →*     |Week 2, post-consent                |
|**E2**     |Show the working: a Score Reveal or changelog                                  |*See the Pillar Card →*      |Week 5 (after the re-grade — rule 4)|
|**E3**     |Role-specific: DPO data note / SENCO caveat / rescreen alert                   |*Ask Luna →*                 |Week 5                              |
|**Ongoing**|Compliance Calendar, weekly through summer — leaders read email in August      |British, direct subject lines|Weekly from Week 3                  |

**Draft — Nurture Email 1 (Donna-ready, held until the list is clean):**

> **Subject:** A tool that saves a primary teacher about forty minutes on a Friday
> 
> Hi [First name],
> 
> Most AI tools promise the earth. We test them against five published pillars — safeguarding, data privacy, age suitability, transparency, and accessibility — and tell you plainly what holds up.
> 
> Here’s one verdict to start with: a genuinely useful planning aid for primary staff, with one important caveat on pupil data. We’ve reviewed it against KCSIE 2025, named the reviewer, and dated the score. You can read exactly how we reached it.
> 
> No paid placements. Affiliate income never affects a score. Every score is earned.
> 
> Read the methodology → [link]
> 
> *Reviewed independently · KCSIE 2025 · Methodology v2.1. You’re receiving this because you signed up for GetPromptly. One-click unsubscribe below.*

### 5.3 Oliver Vendor Outreach (evidence first, Validator later)

**Posture:** calm, independent, methodology-led. **5/day Week 1**, **10/day Week 2**, toward 50/day Week 7 only on clean deliverability (pause on bounce > 5% or complaints > 0.1%). Two polite follow-ups, then quiet. Donna gates every send — on **v3.2** from Week 1 Day 4.

**Dual purpose, strict sequence:** Email 1 leads with review participation and evidence — never commission, never a score preview, **never the Validator** (rules 7–8). The Validator enters only in later conversation with vendors who engaged, from Week 5, with founder involvement on Tier 1 calls. Oliver prepares; founders close. Vendor revenue is the bridge that funds the membership build, so Oliver’s ramp rules carry strategic weight, not just operational weight.

**Prerequisites before the first real send:** Brevo API key → n8n credential → send node migrated SMTP→Brevo → gated path re-tested end to end → outbound gate repointed v3.1→v3.2 → TEST_MODE off. The **AI Outreach Generator stays contained** — ungated, and not to be reactivated as part of go-live (rule 13).

**Draft — Oliver’s first vendor email (Donna-ready):**

> **Subject:** Reviewing [Vendor] against our five-pillar methodology
> 
> Hi [Name],
> 
> I run vendor partnerships at GetPromptly, the UK independent review platform for AI in education, and [Vendor] is on our list to review against our published five-pillar methodology. To assess it fairly for UK schools, I’d value sight of your current data-protection, safeguarding, and accessibility documentation — a DPA, privacy policy, under-18 position, and accessibility statement cover most of it.
> 
> There’s no cost and no obligation. Our methodology is public and our scores are not for sale. You are welcome to send factual corrections or missing evidence before publication, but scores are not previewed, negotiated or sold.
> 
> Yours sincerely,
> Oliver — Vendor Partnerships, GetPromptly
> 
> *METHODOLOGY V2.1 · [PARTNERSHIPS@GETPROMPTLY.CO.UK](mailto:PARTNERSHIPS@GETPROMPTLY.CO.UK) · GetPromptly is independent. Scores are not for sale and never have been. · One-click unsubscribe below.*

### 5.4 Social — Live and Building in Public

Build trust, not sales. Dark Pillar-Card template, methodology mark at the foot, nothing animated. Frame is **live and building in public**, never “coming soon.” Anchor lines: *“GetPromptly is live — and we’re building the UK education AI trust layer in public,” “Follow the scores as they land,”* and *“Before you prompt, check Promptly.”*

**Hard rules:** no tool promoted as a recommendation until its review page exists (rule 5). No Score Reveal until the re-grade has published and the registry→site re-sync is done (rule 4) — a Reveal screenshot must never contradict the live site.

**First 10 post ideas:**

1. “GetPromptly is live — and we’re building the UK education AI trust layer in public.”
1. “Before you prompt, check Promptly.”
1. “Popular is not the same as suitable.” — with the five pillar names.
1. “Follow the scores as they land.” — a build-in-public update cadence.
1. “Are you Promptly ready?” — three questions to ask before any AI tool reaches a classroom.
1. The five pillars, one card each (a short series).
1. “No paid placements. Scores are not for sale. Every score is earned.”
1. “Reviewed against KCSIE 2025 — not ‘compliant’, not ‘approved’. Here’s the difference.”
1. Meet Luna: “Tell her your role and what you’re trying to do.”
1. “Stop guessing with AI. Check Promptly.”

**The re-grade note is the flagship building-in-public asset and the membership’s founding story:** “We strengthened our methodology — here’s what changed and why some scores moved.” That post does more for trust than any Reveal, and it is the proof point every future sales conversation leads with.

**Workflow:** *Notion brief → Claude draft → Donna gate → Canva visual → Buffer schedule → Analytics review.* Nothing reaches Buffer without a Donna pass.

### 5.5 Paid Ads (small, evidential, Calendar-first)

**Budget:** £5–10/day, 7-day test (£35–70 total), Meta, from Week 3. **Goal:** learn which audience responds — then ongoing spend buys **Calendar signups only**. Do not buy subscription leads; the cycle is too long.

|Ad set|Audience                         |Destination               |
|------|---------------------------------|--------------------------|
|A     |Teachers / school staff (UK, 25+)|Methodology page          |
|B     |Teachers / school staff (UK, 25+)|Luna chat                 |
|C     |School leaders / staff (UK, 25+) |Compliance Calendar signup|

**Rules:** never target under-18s; never send paid traffic to an affiliate link or any monetised page; on-brand copy only; measure cost per methodology read, per Luna chat, per Calendar signup. Scale only after evidence, and scale into ad set C.

### 5.6 Affiliate Registry & Gate (supporting stream — setup runs quietly, promotion waits for the verdict)

Affiliate exists to pay the petrol — API costs and the ad test — while the membership is built. It earns in the background and is never allowed to set priorities: review order is decided by evidence and risk (§4.3), and a link exists only because a verdict already does.

The registry is the **existing Tools tab** (slug-keyed, columns M–Q live, backed up). Extend it — do not create a second source of truth.

|Column                                                         |Purpose                                                                                                         |
|---------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|partner_name / product_name / category / slug                  |Identity (slug is the key)                                                                                      |
|raw_url / tracked_url                                          |Source + held tracked link                                                                                      |
|commission_rate / cookie_window / dashboard_url / api_available|Terms (for the record, never for ranking)                                                                       |
|affiliate_status                                               |found / partnership_candidate / not_applicable / needs_review (from discovery)                                  |
|**status**                                                     |Not contacted / Applied / Accepted / Rejected / Link Ready / Published                                          |
|**Review Status**                                              |Not reviewed / Under review / Published / Re-review due                                                         |
|**Public Link Allowed**                                        |Yes / No                                                                                                        |
|is_affiliate                                                   |Stays FALSE until human enrolment confirms — `affiliate_link_injection` fails closed on every slug until flipped|
|linked_review_url / disclosure_present                         |Publication wiring                                                                                              |
|last_checked / notes                                           |Maintenance                                                                                                     |

**The gate, plainly:** `Public Link Allowed = Yes` **only when** Review Status = Published **and** disclosure_present = Y **and** linked_review_url exists **and** is_affiliate = TRUE. Until then, every link sits “Link Ready” and dark. “Accepted” never means “live.”

**Background sequence:** W1 gate columns added, all rows “No” → W3 confirm each programme allows editorial/review promotion with disclosure; apply to the first 10, review-first, networks carrying several tools prioritised → W3–4 record terms; accepted → tracked URLs → “Link Ready” (held; enrolment is a **human** step — no agent flips `is_affiliate`) → W4 map links to review URLs; Disclosure block templated → W5 onwards, live only through the gate. **W5–6:** inject a single contextual “Next step in this workflow” product card into each of the top 20 prompt pages — only where the matched product has a Published review, through this same gate, one card per page with the Disclosure block inline. Park programmes for unscored tools — no application that pressures a score.

**Disclosure block (once links exist):** *“DISCLOSURE: tracked link · scores never affected · methodology public.”*

## 6. Key Operating Rules & Gates

### 6.1 Sequencing rules (numbered, non-negotiable — cited by number throughout)

1. **Nothing sends** until the Brevo credential chain is proven and consent/provenance is confirmed per segment. Nurture E1 is drafted and held until both conditions pass.
1. **Pupils never receive marketing.** Quarantined, Donna hard-blocked, excluded entirely if genuinely under 18.
1. **No score publishes** before the registry→site re-sync and the approved re-grade communication. Hold-and-publish-once; CR + CD sign off every downgrade.
1. **No Score Reveal** before the re-grade has published and the site is in sync. A Reveal must never contradict the live site.
- **Rule 4b.** No score artefact leaves the platform without a dated stamp and a live-score link. Badges must be served live from the GetPromptly endpoint only — static badge images are never issued to vendors. Every downloaded receipt must state “Score as at [date] · Check current score: [live link]”.
1. **No social recommendation** of any tool without a live review page. Locked.
1. **No public affiliate link** before a published Promptly Score, a live review page, the Disclosure block, and the full registry gate (§5.6). Setup runs ahead; promotion waits for the verdict.
1. **No Validator mention in a first email.** Review participation and evidence first; commercial conversation only with engaged vendors, founder-led on Tier 1.
1. **No score preview to any vendor, ever.** Factual corrections before publication, yes; previews, negotiation, or sale, no.
1. **Review order is decided by evidence and risk**, never by commission. Affiliate availability cannot move a tool up the queue.
1. **Founder approval caps are hard caps:** 2 library assets/week, 2 hrs/week. Slow the cadence rather than lower the gate.
1. **Scores, reviews, and the methodology stay free forever** — stated on the pricing page. Schools pay for artefacts and workflow, never for verdicts.
1. **Validator/membership firewall:** Validator insights are never a school-tier feature; school data is never a vendor-tier feature. If the three §21 separations ever blur, the Validator pauses immediately.
1. **No ungated sender is reactivated** (AI Outreach Generator et al.) without a clone-first Donna gate.
1. **Scope control:** MAT dashboard month 7+ and only after a MAT asks; Enterprise tier only when someone asks twice.
1. **Founding Schools recruitment sends only after the re-grade has published and receipts are live** — the §5.1 timing rule.

### 6.2 Donna Pre-Send Checklist (v3.2 on all paths once the outbound repoint is done)

Pass/fail first, reasoning second, failed rule cited. Any fail → held, owner notified, reason logged. No “this one time” exceptions.

- [ ] One-click unsubscribe present (PECR)
- [ ] Recipient not on Do-Not-Contact; not in the quarantined pupils segment
- [ ] Subject line accurate, not misleading; no clickbait
- [ ] No exclamation marks anywhere
- [ ] No proscribed words (revolutionary, game-changing, seamless, empower, leverage, unlock, AI-powered, intelligent, transformative, best-in-class…)
- [ ] No “approved”, “certified”, “safest”, “trusted by schools”, “official”, or “KCSIE compliant” — “reviewed against KCSIE 2025” only
- [ ] No “no sponsors” phrasing — use “no paid placements / scores are not for sale”
- [ ] No earnings or commission guarantees
- [ ] No score preview offered to any vendor; no implication of paid placement
- [ ] No affiliate link unless Review Status = Published **and** disclosure present **and** Public Link Allowed = Yes **and** is_affiliate = TRUE
- [ ] No social recommendation of a tool without a live review page
- [ ] No Score Reveal before the re-grade has published and the site is in sync with the registry
- [ ] **No score artefact (badge or receipt) issued without a dated stamp and a live-score link** — badges served live from the GetPromptly endpoint only, never as static images to vendors *(Rule 4b)*
- [ ] **No paid-tier copy that implies paying improves a school’s safety, standing, or score** — sell time and artefacts, never approval *(membership)*
- [ ] **No Validator copy that displays or implies the Promptly Score; badge wording is “Methodology Engagement” only** *(membership)*
- [ ] **Co-marketing with Validator vendors says “independently reviewed”, never “endorsed”** — encoded now, before the first vendor asks *(membership)*
- [ ] Methodology mark present on public-facing output
- [ ] Recommendation relevant to recipient’s role (legitimate interest justified)
- [ ] Data minimisation respected; no pupil data in scope (else human review) — the Luna rule extends to the membership
- [ ] SEND / safeguarding caveats present where applicable
- [ ] British English throughout

### 6.3 Operations cadence and founder-time caps

|Function                                     |Agent(s)                                     |Cadence                                |Founder touch (hard cap)                                        |
|---------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
|Calendar + nurture                           |Newsletter + Content, Donna-gated            |Weekly                                 |30 min review                                                   |
|Library drafting                             |Content + Research → Donna → founder approval|Max 2 assets/week                      |**Max 2 hrs/week**                                              |
|Re-scoring + re-grade                        |Research → CR + CD dual sign-off             |Batches of ~25                         |Sign-off only                                                   |
|Membership build (MVP, webinar, profile spec)|Technical + Content → Donna                  |Per §5.1 schedule                      |Approvals only — webinar ≤ 1 hr once; email approvals in minutes|
|Vendor outreach + Validator prep             |Oliver + Donna                               |5 → 10 → 50/day on clean deliverability|Tier 1 calls only                                               |
|Affiliate registry                           |Research + Technical                         |Background from Week 3                 |Human enrolment step only                                       |
|Social                                       |Social + Donna → Canva → Buffer              |Per plan                               |Spot checks                                                     |
|Pilot Zero / pilot comms                     |Brevo sequences + Oliver-style templates     |Automated                              |Monthly calls (non-delegable)                                   |
|Quality watch                                |Sentinel Weekly vs Brand Bible               |Weekly                                 |15 min                                                          |

**Post-launch founder load target:** 6–8 hrs/week total. **Freshness rule:** every library asset carries a methodology-mark-style stamp (version · last verified · reviewer) and a re-review date; Sentinel flags stale assets; a verification past its cadence reverts to Partial until re-checked.

### 6.4 Success metrics (watch weekly)

|(Post-launch) Time-to-first-receipt      |Days, not weeks                                |Strongest renewal predictor                                                             |

## 8. First 30 Days

### Week 1 checklist (days 1–7)

- [ ] Brevo API key → n8n credential → migrate Oliver send node SMTP→Brevo → re-test gated path end to end *(Day 1)*
- [ ] Confirm the 5,000 leads are in Brevo; import from Sheets if not *(Day 1)*
- [ ] Publish Google OAuth consent screen Testing→Production; reconnect *(Day 1 — token expires ~15 Jun)*
- [ ] Segment the 5,000 by role + consent basis; record source/provenance *(Day 2)*
- [ ] Quarantine pupils; Donna hard-block; document the decision *(Day 2)*
- [ ] Extend the Tools tab with Review Status + Public Link Allowed; all rows = No *(Day 2)*
- [ ] Build the Top 20 Review Priority List *(Day 3)*
- [ ] Confirm consent/provenance per segment; PECR position recorded *(Day 3)*
- [ ] **Decide + draft the re-grade communication** (CR + CD; open methodology-update note; hold-and-publish-once) *(Day 3)*
- [ ] **Decide tier names + founding prices + VAT position** (30 min — unblocks all copy; CR + CD) *(Day 3)*
- [ ] **Draft the Pilot Zero agreement** — one page, plain terms (CD) *(Day 4)*
- [ ] Extend + test the Donna checklist (incl. the three membership rules); repoint Oliver outbound v3.1→v3.2 *(Day 4)*
- [ ] Donna-pass Oliver’s first vendor email; pilot 5 evidence-led sends *(Day 5)*
- [ ] Donna-pass Nurture Email 1; **hold** until consent + Brevo confirmed *(Day 5)*
- [ ] Post the building-in-public set (3 posts) *(Day 5)*
- [ ] Close the 3 pre-alert Luna escalations; name the escalation queue owner + response window *(Days 6–7)*
- [ ] Build the ad test; hold the spend *(Days 6–7)*

*No nurture send in Week 1. No affiliate applications in Week 1 — the registry hygiene is enough.*

### Days 8–30

- [ ] Pilot Zero agreement signed; “From the classroom” feature agreed
- [ ] First 10 library assets listed and drafting; approvals at max 2/week
- [ ] Luna school-intent question live (budget holder or not)
- [ ] Pillar enrichment complete; tier-column fix; 243 re-scoring run done in batches (CR + CD sign-off)
- [ ] Registry→site re-sync; methodology-update note published; **re-grades publish once, cleanly**
- [ ] Re-grade story repackaged: LinkedIn series + one press pitch (agents draft, 1 hr approval)
- [ ] First review pages under v2.1; Score Reveals begin post-re-grade
- [ ] Demo Audit Receipt downloadable; Living Pages live
- [ ] Membership MVP scope locked; build begins
- [ ] £5–10/day Meta test run and read; scale decision into Calendar signups only
- [ ] *Background: apply to first 10 affiliate programmes (review-first, networks prioritised); accepted → Link Ready (held)*

-----

## 9. Do Now / Do Next / Do Not Do Yet

**Do now (Week 1):** Brevo credential chain; confirm leads into Brevo; publish OAuth; quarantine pupils; confirm consent; extend the registry + Top 20; decide the re-grade comms; **decide tier names, founding prices and the VAT position; draft the Pilot Zero agreement**; repoint Oliver to v3.2 and pilot at 5/day; building-in-public posts; draft and hold Nurture E1; extend Donna with the membership rules; own the escalation queue.

**Do next (Weeks 2–5):** confirm consent → first nurture send and weekly Calendar; sign Pilot Zero; begin library drafting at 2/week; Oliver to 10/day; pillar enrichment + tier-column fix; full 243 re-scoring run (output-only, CR + CD sign-off); registry→site re-sync; publish the methodology-update note + re-grades once; repackage the re-grade story for LinkedIn and one press pitch; lock the MVP scope and begin the build; open Validator conversations with engaged vendors; Score Reveals after the re-grade is live; £5–10/day Meta test; *then, in the background:* affiliate applications (first 10, review-first), tracked links to Link Ready, first links live only through the §5.6 gate.

**Do not do yet:** no public affiliate links before a published score + review page + disclosure; no Score Reveal before the re-grade publishes and the site is in sync; no social recommendation without a review page; no Validator pitch in a first email; no score previews; no marketing to pupils; no reactivating any ungated sender; no Oliver ramp to 50/day until deliverability holds; no paid traffic to monetised content; no scaling ad spend before evidence (and then into Calendar signups only); no Trends Scout activation (~1 month deferral stands); **no Founding Schools recruitment send before the re-grade is published and receipts are live (window: late August / early September, §5.1); no paid tier before January 2027 and before both kill criteria are reviewed; no MAT dashboard before a MAT asks; no Enterprise tier until someone asks twice; no buying subscription leads with paid spend.**

-----

**One-line verdict:** the strategy sells the only thing schools reliably pay for — defensibility and time — funded by the only counterparty with money right now (vendors), supported quietly by affiliate petrol money, and held together by the independence record the re-grade is about to pay for in public. The next eight weeks build the audience, the artefacts and the proof; the discipline is the product. Hold your nerve in months 7–9; the calendar says the revenue arrives late, and it is right.

-----

## Merge decisions (v1.0) — abridged

1. Membership strategy (v1.2) set as destination; the 8-week roadmap became the Phase 1 engine with explicit exit criteria and a Phase 2 handover.
1. Affiliate demoted from headline to background — applications Week 1→3, links Week 4→5 — with every gate and fail-closed mechanism preserved unchanged.
1. Membership groundwork inserted into the freed space: tier/pricing/VAT and Pilot Zero in Week 1; library drafting (capped) from Week 2; artefact layer in Week 4; webinar, profile and recruitment prep in Weeks 6–8.
1. The re-grade reframed as the membership’s founding asset, with every sequencing rule kept and a Week 3 repackaging deliverable added.
1. Nurture re-pointed at the Compliance Calendar as the top of the funnel; E2 timing tied to the re-grade rule.
1. Oliver given a dual mandate with a strict firewall: evidence outreach from Week 1; Validator only from Week 5, never in a first email, founder-led on Tier 1.
1. Donna extended with three membership rules (no paying-for-safety implication; Validator badge wording; “independently reviewed”, never “endorsed”).
1. Risk registers merged — operational and strategic in one table; both kill/slow criteria carried in.
1. Ads aligned to the channel ranking: test survives; scaling buys Calendar signups only, never subscription leads.
1. Where Weeks 1–4 hours were contested, the merge consistently chose actions serving both the live platform and the membership.

## Changes in v1.1

1. **Founding Schools timing corrected (§5.1, rule 15).** The recruitment send moves from “late August, Week 7” to a flexible **late August / early September window, gated on two proofs: the re-grade published and receipts live** — restoring v1.2’s 30–60 day intent. Drafting and Donna-passing stay in Week 7 so the email is ready the moment the proofs are. A short rationale note now sits with the rule: the email converts on evidence, not timing, and early September lands beside the KCSIE update with teachers back at work.
1. **Membership build expanded with milestones and owners (§5.1, Weeks 6–8 of §4.1).** Concrete additions: webinar deck + registration page (Content → Donna, CD approval ≤ 1 hr); “Tools we use” profile spec (Technical) with its data shape named; MVP staged as scope-locked (W4) → functional in staging with both payment paths tested (W7) → quiet-live with no public launch (W8); demo receipt + library walkthrough recorded; pricing-page copy with the “free forever” statement; Pilot Zero call #1 booked. A founder-time check confirms the §6.3 caps hold — everything new is agent-built, founder-approved.
1. **Scannability improved.** A “How to use this document” section added after the Executive Summary; the §4.1 week table tightened, with background affiliate tasks italicised by convention; rules now stated once in §6.1 and cited by number elsewhere (the risk register and workstreams reference “rule 4”, “§5.6 gate” rather than restating them); the v1.0 merge notes abridged to one-liners.
1. **Membership now visibly leads.** §5 reordered: Membership Build is §5.1 and the lead workstream; affiliate moves to §5.6, retitled “Affiliate Registry & Gate”, and opens by stating its job — pay the petrol, never set priorities. Residual affiliate-first phrasing in the snapshot, week table and checklists reframed as background tasks. All operational rules intact.
1. **The re-grade’s dual role made explicit.** A dedicated Executive Summary paragraph names both halves — highest-stakes operational event and founding credibility asset — and Week 3 is restructured as *operational half / credibility half* so neither can be skipped. The snapshot and risk register cross-reference the framing.
1. **Rule 15 added** to make the Founding Schools proof-gate a numbered sequencing rule rather than a note, and the §4.2 exit criteria updated to match (webinar built, MVP quiet-live, recruitment email held for its window).

## Changes in v1.2

1. **Procurement template named (§5.1).** One of the first 10 library assets is now explicitly the static “AI Procurement Checklist & RFP Template — KCSIE 2025 aligned”, set as the free flagship download behind Calendar signup with the fuller pack held in the paid tier; the Week 2 library line updated and a dedicated §5.1 row added.
1. **Prompt-page product cards added as background affiliate yield (§5.6, §6.4).** Weeks 5–6 inject a single “Next step in this workflow” card into the top 20 prompt pages — Published reviews only, through the existing §5.6 gate — with a tracking row added to §6.4. No new workstream, no founder time beyond spot checks.
1. **Rule 4b added (§6.1, §6.2, §5.1, §4.1).** No score artefact leaves the platform without a dated stamp and a live-score link — badges served live from the GetPromptly endpoint only, never static to vendors; receipts carry an “as at” date and a live-score link. Mirrored in the Donna checklist (§6.2) and referenced at §5.1 Week 4 (receipts) and §4.1 Week 6 (badges).
