# SOURCE FILE: `PROMPTLY-BRAND-WORLD-v2.2.md`

# The Brand World of Promptly

**Version 2.2 · Identity constitution · 28 July 2026**

**Status:** Harmonised Draft for Founder Ratification.

**Supersedes:** Brand World v2.1 (Migration Edition), ratified by the Founder on 26 July 2026 (Governance Log GL-2026-07-26-01) and in force until this version is ratified — then retained intact and marked historic. Earlier: Brand World v1.1 and v2.0 (historic); Brand Bible v1.1, retired as a governing document on 26 July 2026 and reclassified a Historical Founding Source Document, preserved as institutional memory under Charter Art. I.13 and not authoritative for identity decisions.

**Version Note.** This version harmonises the identity constitution with the Constitutional Charter v1.1 and the Unified Operating System v2.1. The principal changes are: **institutional naming alignment** — the public institution is Promptly, the public wordmark reads Promptly, and GetPromptly Ltd appears only in legal and colophon lines; **foundational-language alignment** — the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath are recorded at §2 as the source of the expression doctrine; **beneficiary-language alignment**, so that safeguarding and consent provisions continue to name children and young people; **status clarity** — §0.8 separates settled institutional language, current design direction and genuinely open production matters; **cross-reference correction** following the renumbering of Charter Articles III and V; **terminology harmonisation** and removal of duplication; and the referral of one inherited master phrase that depends on the retired public wordmark. No visual-design decision has been reopened, no specification has been invented, and no matter listed at §26 has been decided.

---

## 0. Standing, sources and method

**0.1 — What this document is.** A design manual and, on ratification, the institution's identity constitution. It states how Promptly is experienced, recognised and understood. It expresses an institution that already exists; it does not define one.

**0.2 — Position in the hierarchy.** This is the class (d) identity constitution under Charter Art. III.2. It yields to the Constitutional Charter and to the constitutional instruments at Charter Art. III.1, to the Unified Operating System, and to the methodology in force. It prevails over strategic, programme and operational instruments.

**0.3 — What this document does not do.** It creates no constitutional principle, no governance power, no approval right and no methodology (Charter Art. III.4). Every gate, check, threshold, cadence and approval is cited to the Operating System, not restated here. Where expression touches process, the process governs.

**0.4 — Traceability convention.** Each section states its authority: **Charter** Article, **UOS** section, **FD** decision. Migrated provisions additionally cite their origin as **Bible §nn**, referring to Brand Bible v1.1 as a historical source. A principle that cannot state an authority appears at §26 for Founder review.

**0.5 — The migration, as recorded.** Brand Bible v1.1 was read in full and dispositioned section by section under the Founder Decision of 26 July 2026. Eight sections were retained, seven revised, four merged, four items archived, and four provisions omitted with their constitutional conflict stated. The reasoning is at the Migration Report v1.0, and the index is at Schedule C. Nothing was copied automatically. That disposition stands and is not reopened by this version.

**0.6 — The amendment register.** The Consolidated Amendment Register Rev A — thirty-nine entries, six requiring Founder decision — remains **unapplied**. This version applies none of it. Where a provision is touched by a pending amendment, the provision stands in its current form and the pending matter is listed at §26.

**0.7 — Working principle.** *Bible, How to Read.* If a future decision could be made consistently by a single named gate, a single named editor, or a single visual rule in this document, it should be. Identity integrity is checklist-shaped, not atmosphere-shaped.

**0.8 — Three kinds of statement.** This document contains three kinds of statement, and they are not interchangeable.

- **Settled institutional language.** Names, terms, prohibitions and doctrines that follow from the Charter or from a recorded Founder decision. These are applied without interpretation.
- **Current design direction.** The system in service — typefaces, tokens, layouts, devices and components as currently specified. These may be refined through the change process at §25.4 and are not constitutional.
- **Open production matters.** Decisions not yet taken, listed at §26. Nothing here is applied as though decided, and nothing at §26 may be inferred from the surrounding text.

Where a passage does not say which it is, the surrounding authority line governs, and a reader in doubt treats the matter as open and asks.

---

## 1. Institutional identity

**Charter:** Art. I.3, I.9, I.14 · **UOS:** §19.4 · **FD:** 03, 06 · **Bible:** §02, §11

**1.1 — What the institution is.** A reviewer of record for artificial intelligence in education. It marks, dates and publishes; it does not promote, oppose, certify or approve.

**1.2 — Reference class.** Closer in kind to *Which?*, *The Economist* and *Wirecutter* than to any tool, management system or marketplace. The product is the judgement. The asset is the methodology.

**1.3 — What the identity must say before anything else.** Three things, in order: this is a record; it is dated; a named person stands behind it. Every identity decision is judged by whether it makes those three legible faster.

**1.4 — The name.** *Settled institutional language.*

- The public institution is **Promptly**. The name is used for the institution, its public identity, its work, its methodology, its judgements and its public presence, on every surface and in every register.
- The public wordmark reads **Promptly**.
- **GetPromptly Ltd** is the legal entity. It appears only in legal-entity references, ownership and contractual statements, statutory notices, copyright and colophon lines, and formal company details. It is never used as a public wordmark, masthead or institutional byline.
- The forms *Get Promptly*, *GP* as a public abbreviation, and any mixture of Promptly and GetPromptly in public-facing material are not used.
- Historical records, retired instruments, past document titles and archived publications retain the wording they bore when made, and are identified as historical. They are not rewritten to match present naming (Charter Art. I.6, I.13).
- The name is a reserved matter under Charter Art. IV.4. Its formal adoption is effected by the Founder's ratification of the harmonised document family; this document asserts no earlier adoption.

**1.5 — Wordmark production.** *Current design direction, with an open production matter.* The wordmark is set in Satoshi Bold, as the incumbent identity system specifies. The single-word wordmark has not been through a production specification of its own: its weights, spacing, optical sizing, small-size behaviour and lockup construction are unresolved and are listed at §26.16. No specification is invented here. Until one is recorded, the wordmark is set from the supplied master and is not redrawn.

**1.6 — The mark.** *Open.* The identifying mark is **not settled**. The incumbent starburst mark remains in service until a successor is adopted; its construction record is archived at Schedule D and remains available while it serves. Adoption follows FD-06: criteria recorded in advance — recognition, comprehension, accessibility, distinctiveness, legal clearance, operational suitability — structured evaluation against them, evidence filed, decision reserved to the Founder (Charter Art. IV.4). Where evidence does not support adoption, nothing is adopted. No territory is selected by this document.

**1.7 — Mark rules that survive any outcome.** *Bible §11.* Minimum sizes: 120px digital and 35mm print for the full lockup; 24px digital and 8mm print for the icon alone; 400×400px for a social profile canvas; 16×16px for a favicon. Sub-brand lockups set the sub-brand name in Satoshi Bold at the wordmark's x-height, separated by a centred middle dot; co-branding uses a centred multiplication sign between marks of equal height; no more than two sub-brand levels deep. Misuse: no stretching or distortion; no placement on mid-tone grounds without a reversal treatment; no shadows, outlines, gradients or effects; no hand-redrawing — designers work from the supplied master. The icon alone serves profile pictures, favicons and app icons.

**1.8 — Identity is not personality.** The institution is recognised by conduct made visible: dated judgements, named reviewers, published corrections, refusals on the record. Not by character, mascot or campaign. Warmth, where it appears, is evidence of human labour, never a substitute for it.

**1.9 — Audience by role.** *Bible §02.* Teachers — what actually helps me teach. School leaders — what can I defend at senior team and to governors. SENCOs — will this stretch every learner without putting any child at risk. Parents — is this safe for my child. Students — how do I use this to learn, not to cheat. Admin and IT — will procurement approve it. Each role has its own route and register. *Pending:* a proposed seventh route for safeguarding leads (§26.10).

---

## 2. Expression philosophy

**Charter:** Foundations F.1, F.2; Art. I.15 · **FD:** 04 · **Bible:** §08, §18

**2.1 — What expression serves.** The institution's public presentation exists to make one commitment legible:

> Every learner deserves decisions worthy of the future they will inherit.

and to make a second commitment unmistakable in conduct rather than in claims:

> Promptly shall place the interests of learners and future generations above commercial interests.

Nothing in this document may be applied in a way that would make either harder to see.
**2.2 — The doctrine.** Public presentation favours clarity, restraint, documentary readability and institutional memory rather than technological novelty. Editorial surfaces communicate evidence, permanence and institutional trust. Visual expression supports understanding rather than decoration.

**2.3 — The governing question.** For any expressive decision: *does this make the record easier to read, check or trust?* If not, it is decoration, and decoration is declined.

**2.4 — Show, do not assert.** Trust is earned by conduct rather than claims. The surface displays the evidence rather than describing the institution's trustworthiness. A page that argues it is trustworthy is weaker than a page that resolves to a record.

**2.5 — Three operating principles.** *Bible §08.* **Editorial over ambient** — annotation marks, stamps, monograms, redaction bars and quietly visible methodology, never floating particles or ambient glow. The atmosphere is that someone has done the work and left their marks on the page. **Disciplined accent** — the accent is the brand axis and the safeguarding pillar; it is used sparingly and structurally, never as background, never as decoration. If it is on the page, it is doing a job. **Calm density** — substantial pages, earned by writing well and arranging clearly. Whitespace is not authority; clarity is.

**2.6 — Restraint as a defence.** Novelty dates; documents do not. Every element chosen for present appeal is a maintenance liability in five years, and this institution intends to be read by people who are not yet born.

**2.7 — We are not.** *Bible §18.* Not Linear — too tech-startup, design-as-product-marketing. Not Notion — too clean-neutral, too productivity-SaaS. Not Khan Academy — too cheerful-primary, too consumer. Not Canva for Education — too template-shaped; we are reference, not output. Not Duolingo — too gamified, too persona-driven; we are not your friend, we are your reviewer. Not any breathless venture-backed AI brand. Not any SaaS with an ambient-particle hero. Not an influencer's link-in-bio; we earn revenue, we are not a monetisation surface.

**2.8 — We are closer to.** *Bible §18.* *The Economist* — serif gravitas, dry register, disciplined density. *Which?* — verdicts real people quote in real decisions. *Wirecutter* — reviews backed by methodology, named reviewers, timestamped re-tests. *The Athletic* — premium editorial restraint. *Pitchfork* — scoring as cultural artefact. *London Review of Books* — British editorial sensibility and respect for the reader. *Stripe Press* — a technology-adjacent organisation that chose to look like a publisher.

**2.9 — The single-sentence test.** *Bible §18.* Before publishing any new surface: could this appear in *The Economist*, in *Which?*, or in *Wirecutter* without anyone noticing? If yes, publish it. If no, identify which anti-reference it has slipped into, and rework it.

---

## 3. Editorial philosophy

**Charter:** Art. I.3, I.4, I.8 · **UOS:** §8, §11 · **Bible:** §07

**3.1 — Verdict first.** The reader is given the finding before the apparatus. Evidence follows the judgement; it never delays it.

**3.2 — The verdict-paragraph rule.** *Bible §07.* Every review opens with a single-sentence plain verdict a school leader could quote in a senior-team meeting. If the opening cannot be quoted clean in a meeting, rewrite it. *Good:* "Genuinely useful for primary planning, with one important caveat on pupil-data handling." *Bad:* "In this review, we'll explore whether this tool lives up to its promise for educators." No hedges, no preamble.

**3.3 — Uncertainty is displayed, not confessed.** Insufficient data, "Not Yet", provisional and historic states are presented with the same composure as a settled finding.

**3.4 — Neither promotion nor opposition.** Editorial serves the decision, not its outcome. Copy never celebrates a high score or laments a low one.

**3.5 — Correction is ordinary.** Corrections are published in the same register as findings: not apologetic, not buried, not dramatised.

**3.6 — The reader is a professional.** Address assumes competence and scarce time. Nothing is simplified to the point of inaccuracy; nothing is complicated to signal rigour.

---

## 4. Visual language and the evidence devices

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.5, I.6 · **UOS:** §7, §9, §17 · **FD:** 02 · **Bible:** §12

**4.1 — The source of the grammar.** British institutional paperwork — marked scripts, examination documentation, inspection records, published corrections. Chosen because it is the visual language of the work itself.

**4.2 — The six devices.** *Bible §12, retained in full.*

- **The Methodology Mark** — a short monospace stamp on every public judgement, PDF, embed and significant editorial output. It telegraphs that someone did the work, named themselves and dated it: the highest credibility per pixel in the system.
- **The Reviewer Monogram** — a two-letter monogram in monospace medium at the end of every review and on every card its reviewer authored. Reviews have humans behind them.
- **The Audit-Receipt Motif** — a faint perforated edge on receipts, procurement briefs, calendar issues and the annual publication. It references the receipt, the audit log and the chain of custody.
- **The Margin Annotation** — a bracket, underline or check in the margin of long-form reviews; accent stroke, 1.5px, slightly imperfect. It says a human edited this.
- **The Change-Stamp** — a small horizontal lockup showing how a score has moved, with date and reason. Visible change history is itself a trust signal.
- **The Redaction Bar** — a solid bar in two contexts: a withdrawn score, and a contested or unverifiable vendor claim. Most organisations hide uncertainty; this one displays it.

**4.3 — The binding rule.** A device may appear only where the record it signifies exists and can be reached (Charter Art. I.5, FD-02). An evidence device without its record is decoration, and this institution's own definition of failure. The check is operational and sits at UOS §9.2.

**4.4 — Retired devices.** *Bible §12.* The dot scatter and the ambient orb trio are retired permanently as generic clichés and are never reintroduced under any name. The diamond badge is kept but reframed: it is the trust-trio lockup, a stamp and not an ornament.

**4.5 — Structural hierarchy of assets.** *Constitutional* — wordmark, identifying mark when adopted, stamp. *Evidential* — pillar card, score presentation, methodology mark, receipt, change stamp, redaction bar. *Editorial* — monogram, margin annotation, verdict typography. *Systemic* — grid, colour law, type system. Assets are never promoted between classes by usage; promotion is an amendment.

**4.6 — Symmetry and ornament.** Symmetry is avoided where it flattens meaning: where five things are named, the form shows five unequal things. Ornament carrying no information is removed rather than refined.

---

## 5. Surface grammar

**Charter:** Art. I.15 · **UOS:** §9.7 · **FD:** 04 · **Bible:** §08, §17

**5.1 — Two grounds.** A paper ground for process, teaching and record; a dark ground for verdict events. Their proportion — which is the resting state — **is an open decision** (§26.2). This document states the principle, not the ratio: the rarer ground carries the greater weight, and whichever is chosen as resting state must read as a document.

**5.2 — Calm density.** Surfaces are dense with information and quiet in treatment. Institutional documents are not sparse; they are orderly.

**5.3 — Consistency across surfaces.** A record looks like itself wherever it appears. Surface parity is operational (UOS §9.7); its expressive consequence is that no surface may present a record more favourably than another.

**5.4 — The five-second test.** Any surface bearing a judgement communicates verdict, state and date within five seconds, without a legend. A surface that requires explanation of its own notation is redesigned, not annotated.

---

## 6. Typography

**Charter:** Art. I.15 · **FD:** 04 · **Bible:** §10

**6.1 — The three voices.** **Display — Fraunces:** hero headlines, section titles, pull-quotes, plain-verdict sentences, tool names. Editorial authority; variable optical sizing gives real character at large sizes. **Functional — Satoshi:** body, interface, navigation, buttons, captions, score numerals, automated-surface responses. **Methodological — JetBrains Mono:** methodology marks, timestamps, reviewer monograms, change-stamps, system identifiers, receipt serials, gate outputs.

**6.2 — Why the monospace is narrowly scoped.** It does one job: it signals *this is methodology, not marketing*. Marketing copy does not use it; body copy does not use it. The narrow scope is what makes it read as a stamp rather than a fashion.

**6.3 — Type scale.** *Bible §10.* Display hero — Fraunces Regular or Italic, 56–80px, 1.1× leading. Display section — Fraunces Regular, 36–44px, 1.15×. Plain verdict — Fraunces Italic, 24–28px, 1.3×. Heading 1 — Fraunces Regular, 28–32px, 1.2×. Heading 2 — Satoshi Bold, 20–24px, 1.3×. Heading 3 — Satoshi SemiBold, 16–18px, 1.4×. Body large — Satoshi Regular, 16–18px, 1.6×. Body — Satoshi Regular, 14–16px, 1.6×. Label and interface — Satoshi Medium, 12–13px, 1.3×. Caption — Satoshi Regular, 11–12px, 1.4×. Methodology — JetBrains Mono Regular, 10–12px, 1.3×. Methodology emphasis — JetBrains Mono Medium, 10–12px, 1.3×.

**6.4 — Rules.** Buttons and calls to action are Satoshi Medium, never bold — pill buttons are refined, not heavy. Fraunces Italic is reserved for two uses only: the master phrase and its formula variants, and the plain verdict on reviews. Fraunces is not used below 18px; below that, Satoshi Bold. JetBrains Mono is not used above 14px; above that, Satoshi. There is no third sans-serif, ever.

**6.5 — Licensing and production.** Every production environment holds licensed files for every family in use. A missing licensed file is a production defect, not an occasion for substitution.

**6.6 — Future type.** Commissioned type remains an open decision (§26.5), judged under FD-06 criteria if pursued. Any change to the wordmark's typeface is an identity decision under FD-06 and Charter Art. IV.4, not a typographic refinement (§26.16).

---

## 7. Colour system

**Charter:** Art. I.15 · **FD:** 04 · **Bible:** §03, §09

**7.1 — Primary tokens.** Accent `#C8E44A` — calls to action, links, score numerals, active filters, the safeguarding pillar; the brand axis. Accent hover `#A8C228`. Accent bright `#DCFA60` — highlight only, never a fill larger than a pull-quote rule. Accent pale `#F0F8C8` — tinted surfaces, very rare. Ground black `#1E1E1E` — dark base for hero, automated surfaces, receipts. Surface dark `#2A2A2A` — cards on dark. Oat `#F5F2EC` — light base, the default reading surface. Oat deep `#8C7A52` — hero text on oat, and the age-suitability pillar.

**7.2 — The pillar palette, structural.** Data Privacy `#6A8CAF` — depth and secure storage. Safeguarding `#C8E44A` — the brand axis; safeguarding is the central pillar and visually the anchor. Age Suitability `#8C7A52` — warm, human, grounded. Transparency `#4A4F5C` — structural, clear, undecorated. Accessibility `#D97757` — warm and inclusive. These are not decorative tones: they convert the methodology into a visual vocabulary, so that readers who see the five colours together read the assessment in seconds.

**7.3 — Editorial greys.** Fog `#9C9C8A` — secondary text, captions, methodology marks. Rule `#E8E4DC` — card borders and section rules. Redaction `#1E1E1E` — the solid bar.

**7.4 — Usage rules.** *Bible §09, retained.* The accent sits on dark or oat only — never on white, never on mid-tone, never on a saturated ground. Text on the accent is always `#1A1A0E`. The five pillar colours are reserved to pillar visualisation and are used for nothing else. The redaction bar is solid `#1E1E1E` on light contexts and solid `#C8E44A` on dark, always with a one-line monospace caption explaining the redaction. **Gradients are forbidden anywhere. Flat colour only.**

**7.5 — Print specification.** Pantone 388 U uncoated, 388 C coated; CMYK C20 M0 Y80 K0; RGB 200 228 74. The accent shifts warm in CMYK at small sizes: use spot colour for any printed element below 30mm and always produce a printed proof.

**7.6 — Internal name.** The accent is known internally as "Promptly Lime 9.1" — a deliberate reference to a high but not-quite-perfect score, and to the institution's commitment to being openly imperfect and audited. Externally it is "Promptly Lime".

**7.7 — Scarcity.** The accent marks; it does not fill. Its function is to direct attention to evidence-bearing elements. **The numeric scarcity rule remains an open decision** (§26.3); the principle does not depend on it.

**7.8 — Colour carries no defensibility.** Colour carries recognition and nothing more. Nothing in the institution's credibility rests on it; credibility rests on the record and on the controls that produce it.

---

## 8. Layout principles

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.15 · **UOS:** §9

**8.1 — Reading order is meaning.** Verdict, state, date, evidence, method, what to do next. This order holds on every surface carrying a judgement.

**8.2 — The record block.** Wherever a judgement appears, a record block appears with it: date, methodology version, reviewer attribution, state, and the means of resolution. Standard, so readers learn it once.

**8.3 — Margins are functional.** Margin space carries annotation, correction marks and record fields. It is not decorative and is not compressed to fit more content.

**8.4 — Print discipline in digital layout.** Columns, rules and fixed positions are preferred to floating elements. A page should be capable of being printed, filed and cited without loss.

**8.5 — Pages end.** Records are finite objects with beginnings and ends, because they are cited.

---

## 9. The Pillar Card and score presentation

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.5, I.8 · **UOS:** §8 · **FD:** 01, 02 · **Bible:** §04

**9.1 — The signature artefact.** The Pillar Card converts the methodology into something glanceable. Outer ring: five segments of 72°. Twelve o'clock is Data Privacy; clockwise: Safeguarding, Age Suitability, Transparency, Accessibility. The centre disc carries the composite score in Satoshi Bold. Reference size 240px.

**9.2 — Card states.** *Bible §04, extended for constitutional alignment. State names are those held by the platform (UOS §0.7) and are never paraphrased.*

- **Active** — full opacity, full colour fills, current methodology mark. Default.
- **Provisional** — outer ring stippled rather than solid, centre number in italic, mark reads PROVISIONAL. Mid-review or awaiting response.
- **Updated** — active card with a change-stamp below. Score changed within the last 30 days.
- **Withdrawn** — desaturated to greyscale, redaction bar through the centre score, mark reads WITHDRAWN — SEE NOTES.
- **Historic** — active card at 60% opacity with an archive timestamp.
- **Not Yet** *(FD-01)* — outer ring complete but unfilled on the pillars whose material requirements are not currently satisfied, centre disc carrying the state name rather than a number, mark reads NOT YET — REASSESSMENT PATHWAY STATED. The card never resembles a provisional pass and no treatment implies eventual approval.
- **Insufficient data** *(UOS §8.4)* — outer ring drawn in rule weight without fills, centre disc reading INSUFFICIENT DATA. The absence is the finding and is presented with the same composure as a score.

**9.3 — Where the card appears.** Review pages at 240px; directory tiles at 96px; social templates at 480px; vendor embeds in the 200×80px lockup; receipt PDFs at 144px in the header; enquiry-surface responses inline at 96px; procurement PDFs at 144px on the cover; calendar issues at 48px alongside the tool name.

**9.4 — The legacy score pill.** The 28px accent-on-black pill survives for dense contexts — comparison tables, search rows, preview cards, signatures. In these contexts the card is implied: **every pill is tappable to its card, and no pill is ever published without a path to it.** This rule predates FD-02 and is its earliest statement in the institution's record.

**9.5 — Rules that protect the card.** *Bible §04, retained in full.* Never used decoratively — it is a judgement, not an ornament. Never published without a methodology version mark. Never published without a last-verified date reachable within one click. Never reordered — top-clockwise, always; consistency is comprehension. Never placed on a vendor page or vendor email without current embed permission and a current score.

---

## 10. Iconography

**Charter:** Art. I.9, I.15 · **FD:** 03 · **Bible:** §13

**10.1 — Purpose.** Icons label record functions: methodology, evidence, change, correction, currency, resolution. They do not illustrate concepts.

**10.2 — Specification.** Consistent outlined set, 1.5–2px stroke, rounded caps, in muted text colour unless serving as an active indicator, in which case they take the accent. The library in use is an instance, replaceable without amendment.

**10.3 — Prohibitions.** No ticks, seals of approval, badges, shields, crests or rosettes — each implies approval the institution does not give (Charter Art. I.9). No spark, orb, brain, circuit or robot forms — each misdescribes the work as technology rather than judgement.

**10.4 — Construction.** Single weight, flat, legible at the smallest size in use before any decorative consideration. An icon requiring a simplified variant to survive small sizes is the wrong icon.

**10.5 — Meaning is fixed.** An icon means one thing across the system, permanently. Reassignment is an amendment.

---

## 11. Photography and illustration

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.10, I.15 · **Bible:** §13

**11.1 — Photography direction.** Documentary, as though photographing a series about working educators. Real environments — actual classrooms and staffrooms, where the slight mess of a working room is authentic rather than a problem. Available light. Genuine moments rather than staged smiles. Diverse representation across every role, age, ability and school type, without tokenism. Technology in context: devices are tools within human situations, never the hero; the educator is always primary.

**11.2 — Colour treatment.** Subtle warm grade: lifted shadows rather than crushed, muted warm mids, slightly desaturated greens and reds. No cold filters, no high-contrast social styles, no accent overlays on photographs.

**11.3 — Interim fallback.** Until commissioned photography exists: hand-curated, paid-licence editorial photography from a UK library, selected by one person. Forbidden: generic office-team stock, child-at-laptop stock, teacher-pointing-at-whiteboard stock, and anything visibly American. **The test:** if the photograph could appear on a competitor's site without anyone noticing, it is wrong.

**11.4 — Illustration.** Minimal line-art with selective accent fills. Functional, never decorative: each illustration must explain something photography cannot. Reference: editorial infographic. Not cartoon, not three-dimensional render, not generated.

**11.5 — Forbidden imagery.** Robot, brain and circuit-board illustration. Floating particles, ambient orbs and abstract energy graphics. **Generated photography or illustration of any kind, anywhere, for any reason** — the institution's work is the opposite of generated content, and using it would be indefensible. Posed stock. Accent overlays on photographs.

**11.6 — Children, young people and consent.** Consent is obtained and recorded wherever a person is identifiable. Where children or young people appear, safeguarding takes precedence over the image, and the image is not used if any doubt remains (Charter Art. I.10). No image is used that could identify a pupil in connection with a tool under review.

**11.7 — Provenance.** Every image carries its licence or consent record. An image whose provenance cannot be established is not published — the rule the institution applies to evidence applies to its pictures.

**11.8 — Sector clichés.** A named negative list for education photography stands as a proposed amendment and is not yet in force (§26.6).

---

## 12. Motion principles

**Charter:** Art. I.15 · **FD:** 04 · **Bible:** §17, §20

**12.1 — Position.** Motion is rare, brief and functional. No ambient motion, no loops, no particles, no parallax, no decorative transition. **No dot animation. No ambient orbs.**

**12.2 — Permitted.** A methodology-mark fade; a margin-annotation draw; a state change settling once. Nothing else moves.

**12.3 — Reduced motion.** Reduced-motion preferences are honoured completely. Any meaning carried by motion is also carried without it.

**12.4 — No persuasion.** Motion is never used to create urgency, delight or momentum. Those are techniques of promotion, and the institution does not promote.

---

## 13. Accessibility

**Charter:** Art. I.15 · **UOS:** §21 · **FD:** 06 · **Bible:** §20

**13.1 — Standing.** WCAG 2.2 AA across every surface, including embedded, syndicated and print-derived material. Accessibility is a criterion of adoption under FD-06, not a refinement stage: a design that fails it is not finished.

**13.2 — Conformance record.** *Bible §20.* Text contrast 4.5:1 body and 3:1 large — accent on ground black measures 8.1:1, oat on ground black 7.4:1. The accent on white fails and is therefore never used on white. Focus indicators 3:1 against adjacent colours — a 2px accent outline on all interactive elements. Motion respects reduced-motion preferences. Body text floor 16px. Colour is never the sole differentiator: the card uses segment position as well as colour, the pill uses a number as well as colour, badges use text as well as colour. Methodology-mark legibility is verified against its background at 10–12px, switching to oat in darker contexts.

**13.3 — Meaning never rests on one channel.** Every state is named in text as well as shown.

**13.4 — Alternative text.** Every image, card and device carries alternative text stating what it records, not what it looks like.

**13.5 — Plain language.** Every state and record term carries a fixed plain-English gloss for readers outside the profession. The gloss set awaits ratification by the methodology owner (§26.7); until then plain explanation is given in line and consistently.

**13.6 — The standard applied to ourselves.** *Bible §20.* Accessibility is pillar five, scored on every tool reviewed. The same standard applies to Promptly. **We do not score what we do not practise.**

---

## 14. Tone of voice

**Charter:** Art. I.3, I.4, I.8, I.15 · **UOS:** §9.2 · **Bible:** §02, §14

**14.1 — The register anchor.** *Bible §02, revised from personality to register.* The voice is that of a knowledgeable senior teacher who has actually done the marking, has reservations about most things, and tells you the truth. Confident, warm, rigorous, dry, British. Not arrogant, not casual, not dry-as-dust, not chaotic, not glib. This describes how the institution writes, not who it is: the institution is recognised by its conduct, not its character (§1.8).

**14.2 — Why the register matters.** *Bible §14, revised.* A British editorial register is difficult to imitate convincingly and is genuinely the institution's own. It is not, on its own, a defence — the institution's defensibility is operational (UOS §§2, 9, 17). It is written this way because it is accurate and because the reader deserves it.

**14.3 — The three registers.**

- **The Plain Verdict** — direct, specific, unornamented; used for verdicts, scores, headline copy and recommendations. *"This is a good tool with a bad data policy. Use the free tier; do not enter pupil data."*
- **The Dry Aside** — wry, parenthetical, never punching down; used for editorial moments and occasional pull-quotes. *"The interface is, to be charitable, inherited from 2014."*
- **The Honest Caveat** — admits limits, names trade-offs; used wherever the answer is yes-but, which is most of the time in schools. *"Useful for KS2 reading comprehension. Less useful below Year 3. Not appropriate for SEND reading without an adult."*

**Choosing:** stating a verdict, plain verdict; acknowledging an imperfection, dry aside; recommending with conditions, honest caveat. Most pages use all three in succession.

**14.4 — Rhythmic patterns.** *Bible §14.* **The triplet** — three things in rising or falling order, the third sharpest. **Verdict-then-evidence** — verdict in the first sentence, evidence in the second, never the reverse. **Plain Anglo-Saxon over the Latinate** — use over utilise, buy over procure, help over facilitate, find over identify.

**14.5 — Words we use.** Honest · earned · asked · plain · the work · ours · in the best interests of children and young people · useful · careful · sensible · considered · we tested · we read · we asked · we re-scored · worth the money · not worth the money · not yet · not for this year group.

**14.6 — The proscribed list.** Revolutionary · game-changing · disruptive · empower · empowering · leverage · best-in-class · seamless · cutting-edge · future-proof · solutions · transformative · unlock · ecosystem · paradigm · journey · excited to announce · thrilled to share · AI-powered · intelligent · smart · passionate about · trusted leader · compliance engine. If any appear in copy, the pre-publication gate fails the check (UOS §9.2).

**14.7 — Phrases that are ours.** "KCSIE-aware, not KCSIE-approved." · "We did the marking." · "Methodology v[X.Y] · last verified [date]." · "Reviewed by [initials], [role]." · "Useful, careful, considered." *The inherited master phrase turned on the retired public wordmark and cannot stand under the Promptly-only identity; it is referred for decision at §26.15 and is not used in the interim. One further phrase in the Bible's list is subject to a pending amendment and stands unchanged pending decision (§26.8). The headline formula at §15.8 is unaffected.*

**14.8 — British without parochial.** *Bible §14.* British English for British educators. We use Ofsted, MAT, SLT, SENCO, key stages, KCSIE, Pupil Premium, EHCP, SEND, DSL, GDPR. We do not use principal, grade, faculty, or schoolwork. We do not perform Britishness, do not write marketing exclamations, and do not pretend to be in a sitcom. The register is dry, considered and slightly sceptical. That is enough.

**14.9 — Prohibitions.** No promotional language. No urgency. No claims of trust, leadership or transformation — the institution does not describe itself as trusted; it publishes the record and lets the reader decide. No language implying approval, certification or endorsement (Charter Art. I.9). No figure that cannot be itemised (Charter Art. I.4). Dryness is permitted; jokes are not, and nothing is at the expense of a school, a reviewer, a vendor, a learner or a child.

---

## 15. Writing standards and messaging

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.5, I.6 · **UOS:** §7.7, §8.6, §9.2 · **FD:** 01, 02 · **Bible:** §15

**15.1 — Every figure is itemisable.** A number appears only where the reader can reach the list behind it.

**15.2 — Every judgement cites.** No pillar judgement or headline verdict publishes without its evidence citation.

**15.3 — Every claim resolves.** Evidence-bearing statements carry the means of reaching their record, in the form appropriate to the surface (FD-02).

**15.4 — States are named exactly.** Published states are used with their exact names, never paraphrased (UOS §0.7).

**15.5 — "Not Yet".** Where the verdict is used, the writing states the material requirements not currently satisfied, cites the evidence, and states the reassessment pathway. No wording implies eventual approval; the verdict is never presented as a provisional pass (FD-01).

**15.6 — Dates and versions are visible.** Every published judgement carries its date and methodology version in text, not in metadata alone.

**15.7 — Corrections.** A correction states what was wrong, what is now held, and when it changed. The root-cause record is operational (UOS §11.4).

**15.8 — Headline formula.** *Bible §15.* *Stop [unhelpful state]. Start [helpful state with our verb].* The formula is used sparingly and in narrow contexts. Its master expression is open pending §26.15.

**15.9 — Audience messages.** *Bible §15.* Teachers — tools that actually help you teach. School leaders — lead AI adoption with confidence, and a methodology you can quote. SENCOs — AI that stretches every learner, with the caveats they deserve. Parents — know what AI your child is using, and why it is safe. Students — AI tools to help you learn better, not do it for you. Admin and IT — practical AI that procurement can defend.

**15.10 — Calls to action.** Short, role-first, forward arrow, no urgency. "Start with your role →" · "Read the methodology →" · "See the Pillar Card →" · "Ask Luna →". No "get started for free", no "sign up today". **Calls to action are doors, not sales pitches.**

**15.11 — Microcopy.** Three truths, no verbs, middle-dot separated: *Free to use · No account required · UK-focused.* *Reviewed independently · KCSIE [edition] · Methodology v[X.Y].* *Last verified · Reviewer named · Re-scored quarterly.* *The framework edition year is subject to a pending edition rule (§26.9).*

---

## 16. Evidence presentation

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.5, I.8 · **UOS:** §7, §8 · **FD:** 02 · **Bible:** §03, §16

**16.1 — The Methodology Mark.** *Bible §16.* Long form, for review pages and PDFs: `METHODOLOGY v[X.Y] · VERIFIED [date] · REVIEWER [initials]`. Short form, for thumbnails, social and embeds: `v[X.Y] · [date]`. Compact form, for favicons and small embeds: `v[X.Y]`. Always JetBrains Mono Regular, 10–12px, fog colour, kerning slightly opened. Never decorative. **Always carries a verifiable date.**

**16.2 — Public-facing language rules.** *Bible §03, retained in full and enforced at the gate.* Say KCSIE-aware, KCSIE-aligned checklist, or reviewed against KCSIE [edition] — **never KCSIE-compliant about a third-party tool.** Say independently reviewed only where the review is not paid placement and the methodology is consistent. Say per our methodology v[X.Y] wherever a score is shown publicly; versions are public and so is the changelog. Include a professional-assessment caveat for SEND-sensitive products and assistive equipment. Include the supervision, age-suitability and school-policy reminder for student-facing artificial intelligence, always.

**16.3 — Provenance is shown, not implied.** Where evidence supports a published claim its provenance mode is stated — attested, documentary, tested, corroborated — as a description of how a thing is known, never as a ranking.

**16.4 — No composite confidence.** Provenance is never collapsed into a single confidence figure or star rating.

**16.5 — Redaction.** Where a claim could not be verified the redaction device is used, with a one-line monospace caption and the record of what was asked and what was received. A redaction without that record is not published.

**16.6 — The Disclosure Voice.** *Bible §16, retained.* Required wherever a tracked link, commission or commercial connection exists. **Sits above the affiliate call to action, never buried below.** Canonical block: *This page contains links that may earn Promptly a commission if you buy through them. Affiliate income never affects scores, rankings, or whether a tool is recommended. Our methodology and our record of score changes are public.* JetBrains Mono Regular 11px, one monospace rule above and below. Variants: in the enquiry surface, a plain note that the link may earn commission and that this does not change what is recommended; in outreach, the standing one-line statement that the institution is independent and judgements are not for sale; in social, the shortened form. **Disclosure done well is a trust amplifier, not a footnote** — it is published with the same calm authority as the verdict.

**16.7 — The Score Integrity Record.** *Bible §16, retained.* The standing statement: that Promptly has never raised a score in response to vendor pressure, payment, threat or commercial inducement; that the record of every score change is public; that the methodology version is public; that the reviewer is named on every review; and that if any of this ever changes, it will be said on the front page. Set in monospace on a near-black ground with a single accent rule. A deliberately plain page, and one of the most quietly powerful the institution publishes.

**16.8 — Currency.** Every record shows its assessment date, the edition assessed and the methodology version. Historic records are marked historic wherever they appear.

---

## 17. The Audit Stamp and the trust trio

**Charter:** Art. I.9 · **UOS:** §9.2 · **FD:** 03 · **Bible:** §12

**17.1 — Meaning, fixed by decision.** The stamp signifies institutional custody of a published record: provenance, publication authority, and the existence of an accountable review record. It does not represent approval, endorsement, accreditation or certification, and no usage may suggest otherwise.

**17.2 — The trust trio.** UK Education · KCSIE [edition] · Independent, in the diamond-stamp lockup, at the top of review pages and on key trust surfaces. The diamond is a stamp, not an ornament. *The hard-coded edition year is subject to a pending edition rule (§26.9).*

**17.3 — What the stamp may mark.** Published reviews and records; audit receipts; institutional publications; correction notices. The institution's own records only.

**17.4 — What it may never mark.** A reviewed product, a school, a vendor's material, a partner's material, or any surface where it could read as a badge conferred on the thing beside it.

**17.5 — Record fields carry real data.** Templates ship empty. A stamp bearing invented data on any public surface is a defect, and the institution may not be the counterexample to its own rule.

**17.6 — Third-party display.** *Bible §05, expression requirements retained.* Any licensed display is live, auto-updating, revocable, carries the wordmark and the methodology mark, and links to the live review. Static screenshots, edited numbers and removed marks are forbidden, because a static copy cannot show a change. The eligibility threshold and revocation conditions are operational (UOS §9); licensing of the stamp is a reserved matter (Charter Art. IV.5).

**17.7 — Relationship to the mark.** FD-03 fixes what the stamp means. It does not determine the identifying mark, which remains open under FD-06 (§1.6).

---

## 18. Review presentation

**Charter:** Art. I.4, I.7, I.8 · **UOS:** §5.4, §8, §9 · **FD:** 01, 02, 05A · **Bible:** §07

**18.1 — Page anatomy, top to bottom.** *Bible §07, extended for FD-01 and FD-02.* (1) Trust-trio strip in the diamond lockup. (2) Tool name, Fraunces Regular 56–64px, no taglines. (3) One-line plain verdict, Fraunces Italic 24–28px. (4) **Published state**, named in text. (5) Pillar Card at 240px, right-aligned on desktop, stacked on mobile. (6) Methodology mark at the card's top-right. (7) What we tested for — five to eight plain-language items, no jargon. (8) Pillar breakdowns — five short paragraphs in pillar order, each headed by the pillar name in monospace with its sub-score. (9) **What remains uncertain**, named. (10) Pros and cons — plain, specific, three to five each, never balanced for the sake of balance. (11) Disclosure block in the Disclosure Voice, above the affiliate call to action. (12) Similar tools — three alternates with their cards. (13) Change-history strip — the last twelve months of score moves, with reasons. (14) Reviewer attribution — monogram, role, date, resolving to the Reviewer Register. (15) **Means of resolution** for the record. (16) Last-verified stamp at the foot.

**18.2 — The card is the front door.** Readable in five seconds without a legend (§5.4). Its states display exactly as the platform holds them; the card never shows a state the record does not have.

**18.3 — Reviewer attribution.** Every review shows its accountable reviewer and resolves to the register entry (FD-05A). Attribution appears with the judgement, not in a footer.

**18.4 — Directory tile.** 96px card, tool name in Fraunces 20px, one-line plain verdict in Satoshi 14px, methodology mark in monospace 10px. **No tile shows a score pill without the card being one tap away.**

**18.5 — Citation.** Reviews are designed to be cited in minutes, policies and procurement records: a stable reference, a date, a version, and an address that will still work.

**18.6 — Scope statement.** Review surfaces state plainly what the review does and does not do. Reviews inform a school's duties; they do not discharge them (Charter Art. I.9, UOS §12.4).

**18.7 — Forbidden around the score.** *Bible §05, retained in full.* Never describe a score as approved by Promptly — it is a judgement, not an endorsement. Never build a marketing claim around the score; marketing claims sit with vendors, judgements sit with us. Never comment publicly on individual vendor relationships, paid or unpaid — the methodology is public, the conversations are not. **Never offer a preview of a score to a vendor before publication. Vendors see scores when readers do.**

---

## 19. Publication standards

**Charter:** Art. I.11 · **UOS:** §9

**19.1 — Expression never sets timing.** Nothing publishes because a design is ready or a moment is opportune. Publication is governed operationally and expression waits.

**19.2 — No preview.** No design, mock-up or draft carrying an unpublished judgement is shown to a reviewed party.

**19.3 — Nothing publishes without its record.** A surface may not display a judgement whose record is not live.

**19.4 — Gated programmes.** Where publication is gated, expression prepares templates and nothing else. Populated examples use clearly notional data, marked as such.

**19.5 — Parity.** A published change is expressed on every surface carrying the record, or it is not complete (UOS §9.7).

**19.6 — The score-publication format.** *Bible §05.* Title pattern: [tool] is now Promptly-Scored: [score] · [one-line verdict]. Hero: the card at 240px. Lead: the plain verdict, one sentence, no throat-clearing. Body: one paragraph per pillar, in pillar order. Close: reviewer monogram, methodology mark, change-stamp where relevant. *The format's name is subject to a pending amendment (§26.8).*

---

## 20. Digital experience

**Charter:** Art. I.5, I.15 · **UOS:** §9.7, §15.5, §20 · **Bible:** §17

**20.1 — The front door.** The public entry point leads with the current record — what has been marked, and when — rather than with a description of the institution.

**20.2 — Dark hero.** *Bible §17.* Ground black. **No dot scatter. No ambient orbs.** A single calm composition: Fraunces italic headline with an accent rule, trust-trio stamp top-left, role cards to the right, methodology mark bottom-left in monospace. Calm density; let the headline do the work. *Whether the dark ground is the resting state is an open decision (§5.1, §26.2).*

**20.3 — Light content.** Oat ground. The accent appears only as accent: active filter pills, trust badges, card centres, score numerals, buttons, review links. Body text `#1A1A14`. Cards white on oat with a rule-weight border.

**20.4 — Registers by role.** Distinct routes for each professional audience (§1.9), each written in that reader's register.

**20.5 — The record is the interface.** Navigation is organised around records and their states rather than marketing sections.

**20.6 — The enquiry surface.** *Bible §06, revised.* Answers from published records, states currency, resolves to the record, and does not generate judgements (UOS §15.5). Expressed as an enquiry desk within the page, never a floating assistant. Standing behaviours: asks for role and context before recommending; **surfaces the Pillar Card with every recommendation — the score is never naked**; escalates to a human where a question crosses into safeguarding, clinical advice, legal interpretation or a complaint; **no more than three recommendations in a turn**; British register throughout. Never claims to be human or denies being an artificial-intelligence system when asked; never collects pupil data; never collects personal details unless follow-up has been requested; never says great question, absolutely, as an AI, AI-powered or intelligent; never recommends without the card; never promises school approval, legal compliance or clinical fit.

**20.7 — Product surfaces.** Any product surface displaying institutional records displays them under these standards; embedded displays are live and revocable (§17.6).

**20.8 — Performance and durability.** Pages are lightweight, print correctly, degrade legibly and remain readable without scripts. A record that cannot be read on a school network is not published to that reader.

---

## 21. Print, PDF and email

**Charter:** Art. I.13, I.15 · **UOS:** §17 · **FD:** 05B · **Bible:** §17

**21.1 — Why print exists.** Printed records are filed by third parties and outlive systems. Print is an instrument of institutional memory, not a marketing channel.

**21.2 — The audit-receipt PDF.** Perforated-edge motif top and bottom, serial number top-right in monospace, Pillar Card at 144px on the cover, pillar breakdowns on page two, methodology mark and reviewer monogram in every page footer. **This is the single most important PDF the institution produces; treat it with care.**

**21.3 — Presentations and reports.** Dark cover with accent title in Fraunces and oat body; subsequent pages light with accent header rules; tables use an accent header row; methodology mark in every footer. The full dark-and-accent system is never run throughout a document — the cover alone carries it.

**21.4 — Print production.** White stock, spot colour where possible, Fraunces headings, Satoshi body, monospace methodology marks. Avoid process-printing the accent at small sizes; always produce a proof (§7.5). Specifications favour longevity and legibility over finish; specialist stock is permitted only where it aids handling or filing, never as a signal of prestige.

**21.5 — Email and the calendar issue.** *Bible §17.* White ground, 8px accent header bar, Fraunces heading, Satoshi body, monospace methodology mark in the footer, accent pill button, dark footer. Subject lines follow the British register — direct, specific, no clickbait. *Passing:* "[Tool] just moved to [score] — here's why." · "This week's KCSIE update: three tools you may need to rescreen." · "Two new SEND tools worth a look. One we'd avoid." *Failing:* emoji and "you won't believe what just got scored"; "the future of education is here"; "don't miss this exclusive review."

**21.6 — Requirements common to all printed records.** Date, methodology version, reviewer attribution, unique reference, and the means of resolution — print is where a durable reference matters most, because the reader cannot follow a link.

**21.7 — Wording discipline.** Printed material for schools states plainly that a record concerns the tool reviewed, not the school holding it, and confers nothing on the holder (Charter Art. I.9).

**21.8 — Colophon.** Printed and PDF records carry a colophon line naming the legal entity, the version and the date. Standard form: *Published by Promptly. GetPromptly Ltd. [Instrument] v[X.Y] · [date].* This is the only routine context in which the legal name appears on a reader-facing surface.

---

## 22. Social media expression

**Charter:** Art. I.3, I.4, I.5 · **UOS:** §7.7, §9.2 · **Bible:** §17

**22.1 — Position.** Social surfaces are distribution, not publication. Nothing originates there; every post points to a record that already exists.

**22.2 — The template.** *Bible §17.* 1080×1080, dark ground, Pillar Card at 480px centred, tool name in Fraunces below, one-line plain verdict in Fraunces italic beneath, methodology mark in monospace at the foot, accent rule along the top edge, trust-trio stamp top-left. Stories: 1080×1920, full-bleed, card at 600px in the upper third, headline lower third, accent swipe pill. Stories re-cut published events; they do not originate claims.

**22.3 — Formats.** A fixed set of recurring formats, each tied to a real institutional event — a publication, a re-mark, a correction, a redaction, a methodology change, a periodic report. Recognisable so readers learn them; not campaigns. *Format naming is subject to pending amendment (§26.8).*

**22.4 — Prohibitions.** No post without an evidence device. No figure that cannot be itemised. No score without its record line. No teasers, countdowns or withholding mechanics — a judgement is a record, not a release. No motion beyond §12.2.

**22.5 — Resolution on non-linking surfaces.** Where a platform cannot carry a working link, that surface's resolution method is recorded before publication on it (UOS §7.7). The institution-wide standard is pending (§26.11).

**22.6 — Cadence.** The institution posts when it has published something. Silence is preferable to filler and is consistent with the register.

---

## 23. Outreach and marketing expression

**Charter:** Foundations F.2; Art. I.2, I.3, I.9 · **UOS:** §1.6, §24.1 · **Bible:** §06, §15

**23.1 — The constraint.** Marketing communicates what the institution does and what its records show. It does not persuade, promise outcomes, or characterise the institution's trustworthiness.

**23.2 — Outreach voice standards.** *Bible §06, retained as instrument voice.* Subject lines state the purpose plainly — no clickbait, no curiosity gaps, no emoji. The first line names a specific reason for contact. Opening messages run to no more than three sentences. **No exclamation marks anywhere.** Every message carries the standing sign-off, the methodology mark in the footer, a one-click opt-out, and the disclosure line where relevant. Never sent without a pre-send gate pass; never to anyone on the do-not-contact list; never beyond the standing rate limit. Never claims a tool has been approved or certified. No earnings claims, commission promises or exclusivity suggestions. No urgency tactics. No apology for following up: two polite follow-ups, then quiet.

**23.3 — Disclosure.** Any commercial relationship is disclosed plainly wherever relevant, in the Disclosure Voice (§16.6), never in a footnote or euphemism.

**23.4 — Separation.** Commercial material never adopts the visual grammar of the record. Evidence devices, stamps, receipts and record blocks are not used to dress commercial propositions.

**23.5 — Standing limitation.** Commercial operations are constrained by the Charter but not yet authorised by a ratified commercial instrument (UOS §24.1). Until it exists, marketing expression is conservative by default and new arrangements are reserved matters. **One inherited outreach template is referred for decision (§26.13).**

**23.6 — The separated vendor service.** Where a paid methodology engagement is offered, its badge reads as a methodology engagement and **never displays the Promptly Score**; the engagement is delivered privately and never moves a public judgement. If these separations blur, the service pauses immediately: the integrity of the judgement is worth more than the revenue (Charter Foundations F.2).

---

## 24. Internal communication

**Charter:** Art. VI.1 · **UOS:** §16, §22

**24.1 — Same register.** Internal documents use the institutional register. An organisation that writes differently in private eventually writes differently in public.

**24.2 — Traceability.** Internal instruments cite the authority under which they act, as this document does.

**24.3 — Templates.** Standard forms exist for governance-log entries, review records, correction notices and decision records, so the record is complete by default rather than by diligence.

**24.4 — Nothing written to be unpublishable.** Internal material is written in the expectation that it may one day be read publicly, because the institution publishes its corrections and its audits.

---

## 25. Practitioner notes and future evolution

**Charter:** Art. V, VI.4 · **UOS:** §19.4, §23.5 · **FD:** 06 · **Bible:** §19, §22

**25.1 — Standing pitfalls.** *Bible §19, retained as a collected note.* The accent shifts warm in CMYK at small sizes — proof print and use spot colour. The monospace is tempting to overuse; resist, because the narrow scope is what gives it meaning. The Pillar Card looks busy at thumbnail scale; use the pill and link to the card. Fraunces italic loses its softness above Regular weight; use Regular Italic only. Designers will want to add a new accent colour; they are wrong — refer them to this section.

**25.2 — What may change.** Typefaces for editorial use, layout systems, photographic direction, iconography, platform technologies, surface treatments, named component libraries.

**25.3 — What may not change without amendment.** The institutional name and the wordmark it carries; the prohibition on implying approval; the requirement that devices resolve to records; the reserved meaning of pillar colours; the ban on generated imagery; the accessibility floors; the ban on gradients; the display of dates, versions, attribution and uncertainty; the retirement of the ambient devices.

**25.4 — How change is decided.** Identity changes follow FD-06: criteria first, structured evaluation second, evidence filed, decision reserved to the Founder (Charter Art. IV.4). Preference is not a reason; evidence is. Review of this document is scheduled with the periodic audit (UOS §21.2), and amendment follows Charter Art. V. Editorial harmonisation across the document family is disclosed in the Version Note and is not an amendment (Charter Art. V.4).

**25.5 — Occasions for review.** *Bible §22, revised.* New product surfaces; a new audience segment; a change in accessibility standards; a material methodology change requiring visual reflection; evidence from recognition or comprehension testing that a specific element needs refinement.

**25.6 — The test of any future direction.** Would a reader still see, in five seconds, that this is a record, that it is dated, and that a named person stands behind it? If not, the direction is declined however well it performs on other measures.

**25.7 — Guidance to successors.** Read the Constitutional Charter, then the Operating System, then this document. The Founding Charter explains why any of it matters. Where this document is silent, express less rather than inventing more. Where it conflicts with the instruments above it, they govern and this document is wrong.

---

## 26. Matters for Founder decision

*Listed rather than assumed. None is decided by this document. Numbering is preserved from v2.1 so that existing references remain valid; new matters are added at the end.*

**26.1 — Ratification of the Bible migration.** **Discharged 26 July 2026** (GL-2026-07-26-01).
**26.2 — The resting ground and its ratio** (§5.1, §20.2), and consequent contrast re-testing.
**26.3 — The accent scarcity rule** (§7.7).
**26.4 — The identifying mark** — prototyping, evaluation and adoption under FD-06 (§1.6).
**26.5 — Commissioned type** (§6.6).
**26.6 — The education photography negative list** (§11.8).
**26.7 — The plain-language gloss set**, awaiting methodology-owner ratification (§13.5).
**26.8 — One phrase in §14.7** and **the score-publication format name** (§19.6, §22.3), both subject to unapplied amendments.
**26.9 — The framework edition rule** for the trust trio and microcopy (§15.11, §17.2).
**26.10 — The proposed safeguarding-lead audience route** (§1.9).
**26.11 — The institution-wide resolver standard** (§16, §22.5), pending under FD-02.
**26.12 — The commercial instrument** on which outreach and marketing authority depends (§23.5).
**26.13 — The inherited outreach template** that offers a vendor the item holding their score below a threshold (§23.2). **Founder Action, 26 July 2026:** the wording shall be reviewed before implementation to ensure that vendor communications cannot reasonably be interpreted as offering a pathway to a higher score or a favourable review outcome. Recorded as an implementation refinement. Until the review is complete and recorded, the template is not used.
**26.14 — The Consolidated Amendment Register Rev A**, thirty-nine entries, none applied.
**26.15 — The master phrase.** The inherited master phrase depended on the retired public wordmark and cannot stand under the Promptly-only identity (§14.7). Whether a replacement master phrase is adopted, and in what wording, is undecided. The headline formula at §15.8 stands; no replacement is proposed here.
**26.16 — Wordmark production specification.** The single-word wordmark's typeface, weights, spacing, optical sizing, small-size behaviour and lockup construction are unresolved (§1.5, §6.6). Any change of typeface is an identity decision under FD-06 and Charter Art. IV.4, not a production refinement.
**26.17 — Migration of live surfaces to the Promptly wordmark.** Templates, embeds, alert furniture, outreach material and exported records carrying the retired wordmark require a scheduled, logged migration; its sequence is operational (UOS §24.9). Historic published records are not rewritten.

---

## Schedule A — Disposition of Brand World v1.1, v2.0 and v2.1

Brand World v1.1's strategy, benchmark research, roadmap, launch grid, creative routes, gates and defensibility map were not carried into a design manual; its expressive content was retained, condensed and traced. That disposition, made at v2.0, stands unchanged. Brand World v2.0's statement that the system has no illustration language was corrected at v2.1 and remains corrected at §11.4: the Bible permits functional, explanatory illustration and governs the point. Brand World v2.1 is carried forward in full; this version changes naming, foundational language, status clarity and cross-references, and reopens no visual-design decision.

## Schedule B — Expression-to-authority map

Every section §§1–25 states its authority in its heading line: Charter Article, Operating System section, Founder Decision, and Bible origin where migrated. The map is the headings themselves, maintained in place so it cannot drift from the text it describes.

## Schedule D — Archived construction record

The incumbent mark's construction — eight-point starburst on an 8×8 unit grid; point length three units; central core two units; vertical and horizontal points at full length, diagonals at 90%; 0.5-unit negative space between points; circular inner core of two units' diameter; and the Bible's own requirement for a simplified 16px variant — is preserved as the construction record of the mark in service. It is superseded as a forward standard pending FD-06 and is retained because the mark it describes remains in use.

## Schedule E — How to use this document

*For designers* — §§4, 6, 7, 9 first; §§2.7–2.9 as the final tone check. *For developers* — §§7, 13 hold the tokens and floors; §§9, 20 hold component behaviour; the Pillar Card is the single most important component, so build it well. *For writers* — §14 is the primary reference; §§15, 16 hold the standards. *For those writing instructions for automated surfaces* — §20.6 and §23.2 hold the voice standards and never-lists. *For social* — §22, with §14 governing captions and §19.6 the publication format. *For external designers and agencies* — §§1–13 and §25 form the brief; §25.1 is mandatory reading; §0.8 tells you which statements are settled and which are open; ask for the component library before starting. *For new colleagues* — read the Founding Charter, then the Constitutional Charter, then §§1–4 here. That is what Promptly is.

---

## Annex F — Identity traceability audit

*Performed 28 July 2026, before presentation of this version.*

**F.1 — Traceability.** Every section §§1–25 states its Charter Article, and where applicable its Operating System section, Founder Decision and Bible origin. Undecided matters are quarantined at §26 and are marked as such in the text that refers to them.

**F.2 — No constitutional authority created.** The document states no principle of its own. Where it states a rule, the rule is the expressive application of a cited Article. Section 2.1 quotes the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath as the Charter states them and adds nothing.

**F.3 — No operational or governance authority created.** Every gate, threshold, approval, rate limit and cadence is referred to the Operating System (Charter Art. III.4). The migrated pre-publication checklist and the vendor-request handling table sit in the Operating System and are not restated here.

**F.4 — Naming alignment.** Promptly is used as the institution throughout. GetPromptly Ltd appears at §1.4 and §21.8 only, in legal-entity and colophon contexts. Historical titles — Brand Bible v1.1, Brand World v2.1, the Migration Report, Governance Log GL-2026-07-26-01 — retain their recorded wording and are identified as historical. One inherited phrase that depended on the retired wordmark has been withdrawn from use and referred at §26.15 rather than silently replaced.

**F.5 — Founder decisions respected.** FD-01 at §9.2 and §15.5, with the prohibition on implying approval preserved. FD-02 at §4.3, §9.4, §16, §22.5. FD-03 at §17, with stamp meaning kept separate from mark selection. FD-04 as the expression doctrine at §2. FD-05A at §18.3. FD-05B at §21. FD-06 at §1.6, §6.6 and §25.4, with no territory selected and no mark adopted.

**F.6 — Nothing pending converted.** Seventeen matters stand at §26, three of them added by this version and none of them decided by it. The amendment register remains unapplied. Section 0.8 separates settled language from current direction and from open production matters, so that a reader cannot mistake one for another.

**F.7 — Beneficiary language.** Safeguarding, consent and pupil-data provisions name children and young people (§§11.6, 20.6, 14.9). Learners appear where the beneficiaries of educational decisions are meant (§§1.9, 2.1, 15.9). Future generations appear only in stewardship contexts (§2.6, §21.1) and state no operational rule.

**F.8 — Institutional memory preserved.** The Manifesto, the version history, the mark's construction record, the disposition index and the rationale of every omitted provision are retained. The Bible is superseded, not erased; v2.1 is superseded, not erased.
