# SOURCE FILE: `PROMPTLY-CONSTITUTIONAL-CHARTER-v1.1.md`

# The Constitutional Charter of Promptly

**Version 1.1 · 28 July 2026**

**Status:** Harmonised Draft for Founder Ratification. This Charter enters into force upon ratification by the Founder.

**Supersedes:** GetPromptly Constitutional Charter v1.0 (26 July 2026) — preserved intact and marked historic under Article I.13. The ratification block of v1.0, as held in the institution's records, is unsigned; nothing in this version asserts otherwise.

**Version Note.** This version harmonises the Charter with the Founding Charter, the Unified Operating System and the identity constitution, so that the four instruments carry one vocabulary and one meaning. The principal changes are: **institutional naming alignment** — the institution is Promptly throughout, with GetPromptly Ltd retained only for legal-entity purposes; **foundational-language alignment** — the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath are recorded at the Foundations and bind the interpretation of every Article; **beneficiary-language alignment** — learners, children and young people, and future generations are used deliberately rather than interchangeably, and safeguarding provisions continue to name children and young people; **hierarchy alignment** — the Founding Charter is recorded at Article III as a moral and interpretive foundation which creates no operational authority; **cross-reference correction and terminology harmonisation** throughout; and **removal of duplication**. No constitutional principle has been created, and no recorded decision has been altered in substance.

**Names.** *Promptly* is the institution: its public identity, its work, its methodology, its judgements and its public presence. *GetPromptly Ltd* is the legal entity, used only where a legal, corporate, contractual, statutory or colophon purpose requires it. Historical document titles and governance records retain the wording they bore when made, and are identified as historical.

---

## Preamble

Institutions earn trust through conduct, not claims.

Promptly exists to help educators and education leaders make responsible decisions about artificial intelligence, through independent evidence, transparent methodology, accountable judgement and preserved institutional memory.

This Charter establishes the constitutional principles by which Promptly governs itself. Every methodology, review, publication, product and visual expression must be capable of being traced back to these principles.

The purpose of this Charter is to preserve institutional integrity over time, so that future growth, innovation and change strengthen — rather than dilute — the standards upon which the institution was founded.

---

## Foundations

*Recorded from the Founding Charter of Promptly, the institution's moral and interpretive foundation (Article III.1(a-iii)). These provisions bind the interpretation of every Article of this Charter. Their enumeration is confirmed by the Founder at ratification, and thereafter they may be restated or struck only by amendment under Article V.*

**F.1 — The First Principle.**

> Every learner deserves decisions worthy of the future they will inherit.

This is the constitutional compass of the institution. Where competing interests conflict, it prevails, and the Articles below are construed to give it effect.

**F.2 — The Constitutional Oath.**

> Promptly shall place the interests of learners and future generations above commercial interests.

This duty is binding and is not an aspiration. It governs commercial decisions, partnerships, methodology, judgements, publication, product development, identity, automation, the artificial-intelligence systems the institution operates, and the institution's growth. It is not suspended by opportunity, financial pressure or institutional convenience.

**F.3 — The present duty is not deferred.** The stewardship owed to future generations does not stand in place of the protection owed now. The precedence of safeguarding at Article I.10 operates immediately and is unaffected by any longer horizon.

**F.4 — Standing of the Foundations.** These provisions state purpose and precedence. They confer no power, create no process and authorise no activity. Powers are conferred by Article IV, and processes by the governance instruments made under Article III.

---

## Our Belief

*Recorded from the Declaration of Belief v1.0 (Founder Approved), which guides constitutional interpretation and does not displace this Charter. The institutional name is rendered as Promptly under the naming alignment recorded in the Version Note; no other wording is altered.*

We believe artificial intelligence will play an increasingly important role in the future of education.

We founded Promptly because we believe educators and artificial intelligence achieve more when they work together than when they work apart.

We believe that the future of education should combine the professional judgement, care and experience of educators with the capabilities that responsible artificial intelligence can provide.

We also believe that progress must be accompanied by responsibility.

As artificial intelligence becomes more influential within schools, educators deserve clear evidence, transparent evaluation and accountable judgement to help them make informed decisions with confidence.

Innovation should never come at the expense of trust, safeguarding or educational integrity.

Promptly exists to support the responsible adoption of artificial intelligence in education by encouraging innovation while helping safeguard the interests of learners, educators, schools and the generations that follow.

Our role is neither to promote nor to oppose artificial intelligence.

Our responsibility is to help ensure that its adoption is informed, transparent, accountable and evidence-led.

We believe that institutions earn trust through conduct, not claims.

This belief will guide every methodology, review, publication and decision made by Promptly.

---

## Article I — Constitutional Principles

**I.1 — Standing of this Article.** The principles below are formalised from the constitutional source documents and from the institution's ratified governance instruments and standing doctrine. Each carries a note of source. No principle is new; each is the formal expression of an approved position. This Article is read subject to the Foundations. The enumeration is confirmed by the Founder at ratification; thereafter a principle may be added, restated or struck only by amendment under Article V.

**I.2 — Independence.** Promptly accepts no payment for placement, ranking or favourable judgement. Judgements are not for sale. Commercial arrangements, where they exist, are structurally separated from the formation and publication of judgements, and are disclosed. Commercial interests do not determine methodology, evidence, publication or conclusions. *(Source: the published independence commitment; the separation and disclosure doctrine of the identity constitution; Foundations F.2.)*

**I.3 — Non-advocacy.** The institution's role is neither to promote nor to oppose artificial intelligence. Its responsibility is to help ensure that adoption is informed, transparent, accountable and evidence-led. The institution serves the decision, not the outcome of the decision. It strengthens professional judgement; it does not replace it. *(Source: Declaration of Belief v1.0.)*

**I.4 — Evidence before assertion.** No claim is published that cannot be traced to its evidence. Figures are published only where they can be itemised; where they cannot, they are not published. The provenance of evidence is distinguished and stated — what was attested, what was documented, what was tested, what was corroborated. *(Source: the ratified evidence-provenance framework; the standing itemisation rule; FD-02.)*

**I.5 — Resolvability.** Every published evidence-bearing claim resolves to the record from which it derives. Evidence remains traceable, transparent and independently verifiable wherever reasonably practicable. A claim that cannot be resolved to its supporting record is not institutional evidence and carries no institutional authority. *(Source: FD-02, Resolvability Doctrine.)*

**I.6 — The dated record.** Judgements are dated. Methodologies are versioned, and their versions are public. Changes are recorded as changes, with reasons; corrections are published, including corrections against the institution itself. The record is never silently altered. *(Source: the ratified versioning and change-record doctrine; the published correction practice.)*

**I.7 — Named human accountability.** Every substantive review has an identifiable accountable reviewer. The institution maintains a public Reviewer Register recording reviewer identity or approved professional identity, institutional role, responsibilities, declared conflicts where applicable, review history and methodological accountability. No favourable movement of a published judgement takes effect without the recorded approval of a named person. Automated systems may assist and may gate; accountability rests with a responsible human reviewer and cannot be inherited by a system. *(Source: FD-05A; the ratified approval rule for favourable movements.)*

**I.8 — Honest uncertainty.** Insufficient evidence is a legitimate published finding, stated as such. Where evidence is sufficient to conclude that one or more material requirements are not currently satisfied, and a defined reassessment pathway remains available, the institution may record a verdict of "Not Yet". Such a verdict communicates present evidence, not future expectation; it does not imply eventual approval and is not a prediction of success. Uncertainty is displayed, not concealed by confidence nor confessed under pressure. *(Source: FD-01, Verdict Doctrine; the ratified recognition of insufficient evidence as a publishable finding.)*

**I.9 — Review, not approval.** Promptly reviews; it does not certify, accredit, endorse or approve. The institution's stamp signifies custody of a published record — provenance, publication authority and the existence of an accountable review record — and signifies nothing further. No output may state or imply institutional approval of any thing reviewed, and no third party may be permitted to imply it. Statutory and professional duties rest where law places them and are neither transferred nor discharged by any review. *(Source: FD-03, Custody Doctrine; the standing non-certification doctrine and defence protocol.)*

**I.10 — The precedence of safeguarding.** Where interests conflict, the protection of children and young people takes precedence. Innovation is not pursued at the expense of trust, safeguarding or educational integrity. No commercial, reputational or operational consideration outranks this precedence, and no longer-horizon consideration defers it (Foundations F.3). *(Source: Declaration of Belief v1.0; the safeguarding-first structure of the adopted assessment model.)*

**I.11 — Publication by process.** Findings are published when the governing process is satisfied, and not before, and not otherwise. Publication is neither accelerated by pressure, opportunity or embarrassment, nor withheld for them. No party is granted preview of, or influence over, a judgement. Exceptions are not made, because exceptions become precedents. *(Source: the ratified publication gate sequence; the standing no-preview and no-exception rules.)*

**I.12 — Information held in trust.** Information gathered in the course of the institution's work is held for the purposes of that work. The institution's records and calibration materials are not commercialised as instruments of surveillance. *(Source: the standing doctrine on calibration and validity data.)*

**I.13 — Institutional memory.** Superseded versions, retired instruments and past judgements are preserved as part of the record and marked historic; they are not deleted to flatter the present. Where reasonably practicable, significant institutional publications receive independent external timestamping or equivalent historical anchoring, so that the institution is not the sole custodian of its own history. The record is kept because future generations are entitled to examine how the present decided. *(Source: FD-05B, External Anchoring; the standing historic-state and record-preservation practice; Preamble.)*

**I.14 — Evidence before preference.** Permanent institutional identity decisions are determined by evidence rather than preference. Identity systems, institutional marks and constitutional visual assets are adopted only after structured evaluation against criteria set in advance, including recognition, comprehension, accessibility, distinctiveness, legal clearance and operational suitability. Where the evidence does not support adoption, no permanent identity is approved. *(Source: FD-06, Evidence Before Preference.)*

**I.15 — Restraint in expression.** Public presentation favours clarity, restraint, documentary readability and institutional memory rather than technological novelty. Editorial surfaces communicate evidence, permanence and institutional trust. Visual expression supports understanding rather than decoration. Trust is shown through conduct; it is not claimed through language or styling. *(Source: FD-04, Surface Doctrine.)*

---

## Article II — Founder Decisions

**II.1 — Nature of this Article.** This Article is the constitutional record of decisions taken by the Founder that bind the institution's instruments. It records decisions; it does not make them. An entry appears here only when transcribed from the Founder Decision Record. This Charter may clarify a recorded decision; it may not alter its intent except by amendment under Article V.

**II.2 — Requirements of an entry.** Each entry states the decision as approved, its constitutional effect, the instruments it binds, and its source record. An entry lacking any of these is not valid.

**II.3 — The decisions.** *All entries below are transcribed from the Founder Decision Record v1.0 (Ratified Constitutional Source Document), status Approved. The institutional name is rendered as Promptly under the naming alignment; the substance and the qualifying words of each decision are transcribed unaltered.*

**II.3.1 — FD-01 · Verdict Doctrine.** *Decision:* the constitutional review framework shall recognise "Not Yet" as a legitimate verdict where sufficient evidence exists to conclude that one or more material requirements are not currently satisfied, while a defined reassessment pathway remains available. A "Not Yet" verdict shall not imply future approval and shall not be interpreted as a prediction of eventual success; its purpose is to communicate present evidence rather than future expectation. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.8. *Instruments bound:* the methodology in force, which shall define the verdict's evidential threshold, its reassessment pathway and its published expression; and all publication instruments, which shall not present the verdict as provisional approval. *Source:* Founder Decision Record v1.0, FD-01.

**II.3.2 — FD-02 · Resolvability Doctrine.** *Decision:* every published evidence-bearing claim made by the institution shall resolve to the underlying record from which that claim is derived. Evidence must remain traceable, transparent and independently verifiable wherever reasonably practicable. If a published claim cannot be resolved to its supporting record, that claim shall not be treated as institutional evidence. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.5. *Instruments bound:* all publication, product and communication instruments, which shall provide the means of resolution appropriate to each surface; and the pre-publication gate, which shall test resolution before publication. *Source:* FD-02.

**II.3.3 — FD-03 · Custody Doctrine.** *Decision:* the Audit Stamp signifies institutional custody of a published record. It does not represent approval, endorsement, accreditation or certification. Its purpose is to identify provenance, publication authority and the existence of an accountable review record. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.9. *Instruments bound:* the identity constitution and every instrument bearing or licensing the stamp; the wording of all records, packs and third-party embeds. *Reservation:* this decision fixes the meaning of the stamp; it does not itself determine the institution's identifying mark, which is governed by FD-06 and Article I.14. *Source:* FD-03.

**II.3.4 — FD-04 · Surface Doctrine.** *Decision:* the public presentation of the institution shall favour clarity, restraint and documentary readability. Editorial surfaces should communicate evidence, permanence and institutional trust rather than technological novelty. Visual expression shall support understanding rather than decoration. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.15. *Instruments bound:* the identity constitution and all strategic, programme and operational instruments governing public expression. *Reservation:* this decision states the standard against which surface instruments are judged; it does not settle any particular instrument-level specification, which remains subject to Article IV. *Source:* FD-04.

**II.3.5 — FD-05A · Reviewer Register.** *Decision:* every substantive review shall have an identifiable accountable reviewer. The institution shall maintain a public Reviewer Register recording reviewer identity or approved professional identity, institutional role, responsibilities, declared conflicts where applicable, review history and methodological accountability. Institutional accountability shall always be attributable to a responsible human reviewer. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.7. *Instruments bound:* the governance instruments establishing review roles and sign-off; every published review and record bearing reviewer attribution. *Source:* FD-05A.

**II.3.6 — FD-05B · External Anchoring.** *Decision:* where reasonably practicable, significant institutional publications shall receive independent external timestamping or equivalent historical anchoring. The purpose is to strengthen institutional memory, preserve public confidence and reduce reliance upon the institution as the sole custodian of its own historical record. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.13. *Instruments bound:* the publication and record-keeping instruments, which shall define what constitutes a significant publication and the anchoring method in use. *Source:* FD-05B.

**II.3.7 — FD-06 · Evidence Before Preference.** *Decision:* permanent institutional identity decisions shall be determined through evidence rather than preference. Identity systems, institutional marks and constitutional visual assets shall be adopted only after structured evaluation against predefined criteria including recognition, comprehension, accessibility, distinctiveness, legal clearance and operational suitability. Where evidence does not support adoption, no permanent identity shall be approved. *Constitutional effect:* enacted at Article I.14; qualifies the exercise of the reserved matter at Article IV.4. *Instruments bound:* all identity, prototyping and approval instruments, which shall record the criteria in advance and the evidence against them. *Source:* FD-06.

**II.4 — Reconciliation.** The institution maintains a ledger of matters awaiting the Founder. At ratification, and upon each entry made under this Article, the identifiers here are reconciled against that ledger so that no matter is recorded in two numbering schemes and no open matter is mistaken for a decided one. The reconciliation current at ratification is recorded at Schedule B.

---

## Article III — Constitutional Hierarchy

**III.1 — Constitutional instruments.** Four instruments are constitutional:

- **(a) this Charter** — the highest governing instrument, which controls constitutional meaning and institutional authority and prevails over every other instrument of the institution;
- **(a-i) the Founder Decision Record** — the originating authority, whose recorded decisions this Charter may clarify but not alter, and whose intent may be changed only by amendment under Article V;
- **(a-ii) the Declaration of Belief** — an interpretive authority, to which recourse is had where the meaning of this Charter is unclear, and which does not displace or override it;
- **(a-iii) the Founding Charter** — the moral and interpretive foundation, which supplies the institution's conscience and the wording of the Foundations above, to which recourse is had where the meaning of this Charter is unclear, and which does not displace or override it.

**III.2 — Classes of instrument.** Below the constitutional instruments, documents are ordered by function, not by product or technology, so that instruments may be renewed without disturbing this Article: **(b) ratified governance instruments** — those by which the institution's judgement processes are constituted and controlled, including the Unified Operating System, which implements this Charter through governance and operations; **(c) the methodology in force** — those defining how assessment is conducted and scored; **(d) the identity constitution** — that governing identity, expression, voice and public presentation; **(e) strategic and programme instruments** — plans, programme documents and their amendment registers; **(f) operational outputs** — reviews, publications, communications and products.

**III.3 — Precedence.** Each class yields to every class above it. Within a class, the later ratified version prevails, and the instrument itself states its version. The current instance of each class is listed at Schedule A, which may be updated by erratum under Article V.2 without amendment to this Charter.

**III.4 — Limits of the lower instruments.** The Founding Charter creates no operational authority. A governance instrument implements this Charter and invents no constitutional principle. The identity constitution governs expression and creates no governance power, no approval right and no methodology. An instrument that purports to exceed these limits is defective in that respect and is corrected.

**III.5 — Conflict.** A conflict between instruments is a defect to be recorded, not a discretion to be enjoyed. On discovery, the lower instrument yields at once; the conflict is logged; and the lower instrument is corrected by erratum or amendment. A conflict is never preserved by vague drafting. A conflict with a constitutional instrument is resolved only by correcting the other instrument or by amendment under Article V — never by construction that quietly reads this Charter down.

**III.6 — Records are not authorities.** Registers, ledgers, evidence files, archives, retired instruments and historical source documents record what occurred. They bind by their accuracy, not by their authority, and rank outside the order above, unless an instrument in force expressly gives one of them standing. No record may be altered to resolve a conflict of instruments.

---

## Article IV — Reserved Matters

The following require the Founder's recorded approval and may not be exercised by delegation, automation or usage:

**IV.1** — Amendment of this Charter, of the Founding Charter, of the Declaration of Belief, or of the Founder Decision Record, including any entry under Article II.
**IV.2** — Ratification of a new version of any instrument in classes (b) to (d) of Article III, including any change to the methodology's version.
**IV.3** — Any favourable movement of a published judgement, per Article I.7.
**IV.4** — Adoption or retirement of the institution's identifying mark, name or master identity, exercised only in the manner required by Article I.14.
**IV.5** — Any partnership, co-branding, endorsement arrangement, or licence of the institution's marks, stamp or instruments.
**IV.6** — Any change to the independence commitments of Article I.2, including any new category of revenue.
**IV.7** — Any public commitment made in the institution's name that binds its future conduct, including provision for succession of the guardianship under Article VI.3.
**IV.8** — Disposition of any matter on the open-decisions ledger.
**IV.9** — Entry into, and settlement of, any matter affecting the institution's ability to maintain a published judgement under pressure.

Approvals under this Article are dated, recorded and preserved. Silence approves nothing.

---

## Article V — Amendment Process

**V.1 — The pipeline.** Amendment of a constitutional instrument follows the institution's ratified change process, applied at its strictest: proposal in writing; review; the Founder's decision under Article IV.1; versioning; and public disclosure of the change with its reasons. No step may be omitted, and no amendment takes effect before disclosure.

**V.2 — Errata.** An erratum corrects an error of transcription, reference or arithmetic without altering meaning. Errata are logged, dated and disclosed, and increment the minor version. Anything that alters meaning is an amendment, whatever it is called.

**V.3 — Amendment touching a recorded decision.** Where an amendment would alter the substance of a decision recorded at Article II, the Founder Decision Record is amended first, or in the same instrument, so that the two never diverge. A Charter provision may not be used to narrow a recorded decision by interpretation.

**V.4 — Editorial harmonisation is not amendment.** Correction of naming, cross-references, duplication and terminology across the document family is editorial, is disclosed in the Version Note of the instrument corrected, and does not alter constitutional meaning. Where harmonisation would require a change of meaning, it is an amendment and follows V.1. No editorial correction is silently treated as an amendment, and no amendment is presented as an editorial correction.

**V.5 — Version history principles.** Every version is numbered and dated. Superseded versions are preserved intact and marked historic; they are never edited, deleted or overwritten. Each version states what changed from the last, and why. A reader of any future version must be able to reconstruct the document's entire history from the document family alone. No silent change is possible in a compliant instrument.

**V.6 — Continuity.** Amendment does not interrupt the institution. Instruments made under a prior version remain valid unless the amendment states otherwise and provides for transition.

---

## Article VI — Stewardship

**VI.1 — The duty of traceability.** Every future methodology, review, publication, product and visual identity must be capable of being traced to this Charter through the hierarchy of Article III. An output that cannot be so traced is defective and is corrected or withdrawn. Traceability is demonstrated, not presumed: instruments cite the authority under which they act.

**VI.2 — The instruments of stewardship.** The institution maintains, as standing practice: a pre-publication gate through which every output passes; a periodic audit of instruments against this Charter and of outputs against their instruments; and the record-keeping by which both are evidenced. The audits are themselves recorded, and their findings acted upon or answered in writing.

**VI.3 — The guardian.** The Founder is guardian of this Charter. The guardian's duty is to the Charter's principles before any other interest, including growth. Provision for succession of the guardianship is a reserved matter and is made while it is not needed.

**VI.4 — The standard of inheritance.** Those who inherit this institution inherit its record whole — its principles, its versions, its corrections and its refusals. Growth, innovation and change are welcome where they can show, in writing, that they strengthen what this Charter protects; and are declined where they cannot.

**VI.5 — The horizon.** The institution's decisions are taken in the knowledge that learners and future generations will live with their consequences, and that the record will be read by people who were not present when it was made.

---

**Erratum 01 · 26 July 2026 (GL-2026-07-26-01) — historical record.** The class (d) and (e) entries were updated on the Founder's ratification of Brand World v2.1 and the retirement of Brand Bible v1.1. Schedule A is erratum-updatable and that correction required no amendment to the Charter.

---

## Schedule B — Reconciliation of recorded decisions with the open-decisions ledger

*A record made under Article II.4, current at ratification. Records bind by accuracy, not authority.*

**Closed by a recorded decision.** The verdict question, by FD-01 (the verdict is constitutional; its evidential threshold, reassessment pathway and published wording remain instrument-level work). Resolvability, by FD-02 (the principle is constitutional; the means of resolution on each surface, including surfaces that cannot carry a direct link, remain instrument-level work). The stamp's meaning, by FD-03. The reviewer register, by FD-05A (the option of approved professional identity is part of the decision and is preserved in implementation). External anchoring, by FD-05B (what constitutes a significant publication remains instrument-level work).

**Settled by ratification of this version.** The institution's public name is Promptly, and GetPromptly Ltd is the legal entity. The name is a reserved matter under Article IV.4; the Founder's direction to align the document family to it is given effect by ratifying this version, and no earlier adoption is asserted. Nothing in this settlement determines the identifying mark.

**Governed but not decided.** The identifying mark, including the selection or elimination of any territory and the approval of any mark, is governed by FD-06 and Article I.14 and remains reserved under Article IV.4. Surface and expression specifications are governed by FD-04 and Article I.15 and remain reserved under Article IV.2.

**Remaining open before the Founder.** Every other matter on the ledger, without exception, including: the institution's programme naming and category language; the primary published metric and its cadence; the colour scarcity rule; identity prototyping budget and sequence; the pending errata to the identity constitution; the timing of the first periodic report; commissioning of a bespoke typeface; the wording of school-facing evidence packs; the public checking guide; the concern-intake path; the audience-table amendment; ratification of the plain-language gloss set; the format rename and phrase replacement; panel sizes for validation; approval of the surface operating rules; and provision for succession of the guardianship. Nothing in this Charter decides any of them.

---

## Annex C — Constitutional consistency audit

*Performed 28 July 2026, before presentation of this version.*

**C.1 — Fidelity to source.** The Preamble is carried from v1.0. *Our Belief* is reproduced from the Declaration of Belief v1.0 with the institutional name rendered as Promptly and no other alteration. Every entry at Article II preserves the substance and the qualifying words of the Founder Decision Record — including "shall not imply future approval", "wherever reasonably practicable", "where applicable" and "predefined criteria" — none of which has been strengthened or weakened. The Foundations are transcribed from the Founding Charter.

**C.2 — No invention.** Each principle at Article I traces to a stated source: FD-01 to FD-06, the Declaration, the Founding Charter, or the institution's ratified instruments and standing doctrine. The Foundations state the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath in the wording directed by the Founder; they are the formal expression of an approved position, not an addition to it. Articles III to VI formalise existing practice and create no principle.

**C.3 — No open decision enshrined.** Checked against the ledger. Where a recorded decision closes a matter, Schedule B says so and identifies what remains instrument-level. The naming settlement is recorded as an exercise of Article IV.4 effected by ratification, not as a pre-existing approval. Where a decision governs without deciding — the identifying mark under FD-06, surface specification under FD-04 — the Charter states the standard and reserves the decision. No pending proposal has been converted to policy.

**C.4 — Internal consistency.** The Foundations and Article I.10 are consistent: F.3 states expressly that stewardship for future generations does not defer the immediate protection of children and young people. FD-03 (custody) and FD-06 (evidence before preference) are consistent: the first fixes what the stamp means, the second governs whether any mark is adopted; Article II.3.3 records the reservation expressly. FD-01 ("Not Yet") and I.9 (review, not approval) are consistent: the decision's own words exclude any implication of future approval. FD-02 (resolvability) and I.4 (evidence before assertion) are distinct limbs — one requires evidence to exist, the other requires it to be reachable — and neither is stated more strictly than its source. FD-05A (accountable reviewer) and I.11 (publication by process) operate together: process governs when, a named person governs who.

**C.5 — Consistency with the instruments below.** Checked against the ratified gate sequence (I.11 restates it and does not reorder it), the approval rule for favourable movements (I.7, IV.3), the evidence-provenance framework (I.4 names its four modes without altering them), and the identity constitution's separation and defence doctrines (I.2, I.9). Article III.4 records the limits of each lower instrument so that no operating manual creates a principle and no identity instrument creates a power.

**C.6 — Technology neutrality.** The Charter body names no platform, vendor, format or technology. Anchoring is expressed as "independent external timestamping or equivalent", resolution as "the means appropriate to each surface". Named instances are confined to Schedule A, which changes by erratum.

**C.7 — Hierarchy integrity.** The four constitutional instruments are ranked without circularity: this Charter governs; the Founder Decision Record originates and constrains amendment of what it records; the Declaration of Belief and the Founding Charter interpret and cannot override. Article V.3 prevents divergence between Charter and Record.

**C.8 — Vocabulary.** Beneficiary language is used deliberately: children and young people for safeguarding, regulatory duty, age-related risk and immediate protection; learners for the primary beneficiaries of education and for the First Principle; future generations for stewardship, legacy and long-term consequence; educators and education leaders for the professional users who carry responsibility for adoption. Review is not interchanged with approval, judgement with certification, evidence with information, historic with obsolete, provisional with "Not Yet", reviewer with approver, or the institutional name with the legal entity.

**C.9 — Style.** No marketing, startup or investor language. Adjectives retained only where load-bearing. Terms of art defined at first use. British English throughout.

**C.10 — Completeness.** No reserved insertion remains. The document is complete for ratification. Ratification requires: the Founder's confirmation of the Foundations and of the Article I enumeration; confirmation of Schedule A; and the dated signature below.

---

**Ratification**

Approved and adopted as the Constitutional Charter of Promptly.

*— End of the Constitutional Charter of Promptly · v1.1 · 28 July 2026 · GetPromptly Ltd*

# SOURCE FILE: `PROMPTLY-FOUNDING-CHARTER-v1.1.md`

# The Founding Charter of Promptly

**Founder's Edition · Version 1.1 · 28 July 2026**

**Status:** Harmonised Draft for Founder Ratification

**Supersedes:** The Founding Charter of GetPromptly, Founder's Edition v1.0 — retained intact and marked historic, and not edited or overwritten.

**Version Note.** This edition aligns the Founding Charter with the institution's other governing instruments. The principal changes are: the institutional name is now Promptly throughout; the First Principle is restated in its canonical form; the Constitutional Oath is restated to name learners and future generations; beneficiary language is used deliberately rather than uniformly, so that safeguarding passages continue to name children and young people; duplicated lines and repeated headings inherited from the source file are removed; and the standing of this Charter within the constitutional hierarchy is stated so that it cannot be mistaken for an operational instrument. No principle has been added, weakened or withdrawn.

---

## Standing of this Charter

This Charter is the conscience of the institution. It supplies the moral and interpretive foundation from which the Constitutional Charter of Promptly takes its bearings, and to which future custodians may return when the meaning of an instrument is unclear.

It creates no operational authority. It establishes no gate, no approval, no process and no power. Where its language and the Constitutional Charter's language differ, the Constitutional Charter governs, and this Charter is read consistently with it.

It is written to be read in a hundred years by people the Founder will never meet.

---

## A Note to Future Custodians

If you are reading this many years after its publication, then Promptly has become something greater than those who first imagined it.

This Charter was never written to preserve a company. It was written to preserve a way of thinking.

Technology will evolve. Artificial intelligence will become more capable. Leaders will change. Products will change. Methods will improve.

None of those things should require this institution to abandon the principles upon which it was founded.

If ever uncertainty arises, return to these pages. Do not ask what is easiest. Do not ask what is most profitable.

Ask only:

> What decision is worthy of the future that learners will inherit from it?

If this Charter continues to answer that question, it has fulfilled its purpose.

---

## The Founder's Charge

Promptly was not founded to tell schools what to think.

It was founded so that those who lead and teach never have to make important decisions without trustworthy evidence.

Artificial intelligence will continue to change. The responsibility carried by those who educate children and young people will remain.

This institution exists so that the responsibility is never carried alone.

---

## The First Principle

> **Every learner deserves decisions worthy of the future they will inherit.**

This principle is the constitutional foundation of Promptly.

Every publication, review, judgement, partnership, technological advance and institutional decision shall be judged against this standard.

Where competing interests conflict, this principle prevails.

---

## The Constitutional Oath

> **Promptly has a duty to place the interests of learners and future generations above commercial interests.**

This duty shall not be suspended by opportunity, financial pressure or institutional convenience.

It governs commercial decisions, partnerships, methodology, judgements, publication, product development, identity, automation, the artificial-intelligence systems the institution operates, and the institution's own growth.

It exists because trust cannot survive where commercial influence outweighs educational responsibility.

The duty owed to future generations does not stand in place of the duty owed now. The protection of children and young people in the present is immediate, and it is not deferred by any longer horizon.

---

## The Preamble

There are moments in history when a new technology changes not only what humanity can do, but how humanity must decide.

Education is living through one of those moments.

Artificial intelligence presents real opportunities to improve learning, reduce workload and extend human capability. It also introduces new responsibilities, new risks and new ethical questions, and those cannot be answered by marketing, opinion or speculation.

The decisions taken today will shape classrooms, the working lives of educators, and the experience of children and young people for generations.

Those decisions deserve more than confidence. They deserve evidence.

Promptly was founded upon the belief that every learner deserves decisions worthy of the future they will inherit.

This Charter exists so that the belief is never forgotten.

---

## Article I — Purpose

The purpose of Promptly is to help educators and education leaders make responsible decisions about artificial intelligence, through independent evidence, transparent methodology, accountable judgement and preserved institutional memory.

The institution serves the decision, not a predetermined outcome. It neither promotes nor opposes artificial intelligence.

It exists not to replace professional judgement, but to strengthen it.

---

## Article II — Duty

The institution recognises a permanent duty to:

- protect children and young people, whose interests take precedence where interests conflict;
- strengthen the decisions made on behalf of learners;
- support educators and education leaders in the responsibilities they already carry;
- earn trust through evidence rather than assertion;
- improve the quality of educational decision-making; and
- act in stewardship for future generations, who will live with the decisions of this one.

---

## Article III — Character

Promptly chooses to be an institution defined by:

- truth before convenience;
- learners before commerce;
- evidence before opinion;
- transparency before certainty;- responsibility before reputation;
- independence before influence; and
- stewardship before ownership.

These characteristics are expected of every colleague, governor, reviewer and contributor, and of every automated system acting on the institution's behalf. A system may act; it may not be trusted in place of the person accountable for it.

---

## Article IV — The Covenant

Promptly accepts that some principles become meaningful only when they are costly to uphold.

Accordingly, the institution enters into this covenant:

- Commercial partnerships shall never determine methodology.
- Evidence shall never be altered to satisfy a commercial interest.
- Every judgement shall remain capable of being explained through the evidence that produced it.
- Integrity shall be valued above growth.
- Where uncertainty exists, it shall be stated honestly rather than concealed.
- Where mistakes occur, they shall be acknowledged openly and corrected on the record.

A covenant kept only when it is convenient is not a covenant. These undertakings are expected to cost the institution something, and they are made in that knowledge.

---

## Article V — Legacy

Artificial intelligence should extend human capability, never diminish human responsibility.

The institution therefore seeks not merely to review technology, but to ensure that decisions about it can be made with confidence, transparency and integrity — and that the reasoning behind them survives long enough to be examined.

Records are kept because future generations are entitled to know how the present decided. Superseded judgements are preserved rather than tidied away, so that the institution can be held to what it once said.

If Promptly does its work well, education will meet the age of artificial intelligence with evidence rather than speculation, and with shared understanding rather than unequal access to trustworthy knowledge.

---

## Closing Declaration

This Charter is not intended to constrain innovation. It exists so that innovation does not lose its conscience.

The success of Promptly shall not be measured by its growth, its influence or its recognition.

It shall be measured by the confidence it gives to those entrusted with the education of children and young people, and by whether the decisions it helped shape were worthy of the learners who lived with them.

Should future generations inherit an institution worthy of their trust, the purpose of this Charter will have been fulfilled.

---

**Adopted as the Founder's Edition of the Founding Charter of Promptly.**

Founder: ................................................  Date: ....................

> *For the generations whose future depends upon the decisions we make today.*

*— End of the Founding Charter of Promptly · v1.1 · 28 July 2026*
