# SOURCE FILE: `PROMPTLY-UNIFIED-OPERATING-SYSTEM-v2.1.md`

# The Unified Operating System of Promptly

**Version 2.1 · Operational implementation of the Constitutional Charter v1.1 · 28 July 2026**

**Status:** Harmonised Draft for Founder Ratification, submitted as a class (b) governance instrument under Charter Art. IV.2.

**Supersedes:** GetPromptly Unified Operating System v2.0 (26 July 2026) — retained intact and marked historic under Charter Art. I.13, together with v1.3.1 and earlier versions.

**Version Note.** This version aligns the operating manual with the harmonised Constitutional Charter v1.1. The principal changes are: **institutional naming alignment** — the institution is Promptly throughout, and GetPromptly Ltd appears only where a legal-entity purpose requires it; **constitutional alignment** — the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath are recorded at §0.6 and the operational rule giving them effect is stated at §1.6; **controlled operational vocabulary** at §0.7, so that abstract language cannot make a rule vague; **cross-reference correction** throughout, following the renumbering of Charter Articles III and V; **terminology harmonisation**; and **removal of duplication**. No state name, gate, threshold, fail condition, escalation rule or approval requirement has been changed. No new operational authority is created.

---

## 0. Status, sources and how to read this document

**0.1 — Standing.** This is an operating manual. It states how Promptly behaves in practice. It holds no constitutional authority of its own: every instruction here derives from the Constitutional Charter v1.1 and, through it, from the Founder Decision Record v1.0, the Declaration of Belief v1.0 and the Founding Charter. This document implements the Constitution; it does not interpret it against itself and it invents no constitutional principle. Where this document and the Charter appear to differ, the Charter governs and this document is corrected (Charter Art. III.5).

**0.2 — Position in the hierarchy.** This document is a class (b) governance instrument under Charter Art. III.2. It yields to the Charter and to the constitutional instruments at Charter Art. III.1. It prevails over the methodology in force only as to *process*; the methodology governs *assessment*. It prevails over the identity constitution (Brand World), and over strategic, programme and operational instruments, in all respects.

**0.3 — Traceability convention.** Every numbered section states the Charter Article from which it takes authority, and the Founder Decision it implements where one applies. A section that cannot state an authority is listed at §24 as requiring Founder review. No section invents authority.

**0.4 — Sources consolidated.** This version continues the consolidation made at v2.0 of the operating record of v1.2 through v1.3.1: the ratified Validity System adoption (GP-RAT-2026-001, amendment A1), the Standing Delegation Rule (GL-2026-07-21-01 / RL-017), the Claim & Evidence Schema Contract v1.0 and its staging acceptance plan, the Score Standard v2.2, the dependency-led phase plan and its gates, the sixteen acceptance tests, the twelve freeze preconditions, the operational safeguards, and the alert-delivery architecture. **Reconciliation notice, carried forward and still outstanding:** the v2.0 drafter worked from the institution's operating record rather than from a supplied copy of v1.3.1 in full. Any provision of v1.3.1 not carried into Schedule A must be reconciled at ratification and either carried forward or expressly retired. Nothing has been silently dropped; anything absent is, by definition, unreconciled rather than repealed.

**0.5 — Names.** *Promptly* is the institution and is used in every operational reference, record, output and public surface. *GetPromptly Ltd* is the legal entity and is used only where a contractual, statutory, corporate or colophon purpose requires it. Historical governance records, retired instruments and past document titles retain the wording they bore when made and are identified as historical; they are never rewritten to match present naming (Charter Art. I.6, I.13).

**0.6 — Constitutional alignment.** This document exists to make two commitments operable.

> Every learner deserves decisions worthy of the future they will inherit.

> Promptly shall place the interests of learners and future generations above commercial interests.

Every gate, register, state and approval below exists so that those commitments are demonstrable in the record rather than asserted in language. Where a procedure here would produce an outcome inconsistent with them, the procedure is escalated under §14 and corrected; it is not applied.

**0.7 — Controlled vocabulary in operation.** Operational rules use these terms exactly, and use no abstraction where a concrete term is required.

| Term | Operational use |
|---|---|
| Children and young people | Safeguarding rules, age-related risk, pupil-data handling, immediate protection, school-specific duties. The operative term in every safeguarding procedure. |
| Learners | The beneficiaries of the decisions the institution informs; used in purpose, methodology impact and educational-value statements. Never used in place of a safeguarding term. |
| Future generations | Stewardship, legacy, record preservation, anchoring and long-horizon reasoning. Never used to state an operational safeguarding rule, a gate condition or a fail condition. |
| Educators, education leaders | The professional readers and adopters served by the institution's outputs. |
| Accountable reviewer | The identified human answerable for a review (§4). Not "approver", not a system. |
| Institutional record | A record held under §17.1. |
| Published judgement | An output that has passed §9 and is live on a surface. |
| Not Yet | The verdict at §8.5 only. Never used loosely, and never as a synonym for provisional. |
| Insufficient data | The finding at §8.4 only. |
| Historic | A superseded or lapsed state (§3.4, §17.2). Not "obsolete", which is not a state. |
| Review | What the institution does. Never approval, certification, accreditation or endorsement. |

**0.8 — Roles named in this document.** **CR** — the Founder, guardian of the Charter, holder of editorial, methodology and governance responsibility. **CD** — holder of schema, platform and technical responsibility. **Reviewer** — the accountable human for a given review. **Donna** — the automated pre-publication compliance gate. **Luna** — the public enquiry surface. **Oliver** — the partnership outreach function. Automated functions are instruments, not officers: they hold no accountability (Charter Art. I.7).

---

## 1. Governance structure

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. IV, VI.2, VI.3; Foundations F.2.

**1.1 — The guardian.** CR is guardian of the Charter and holds every reserved matter at Charter Art. IV. Reserved matters are not delegable, are recorded and dated, and are never established by usage or silence.

**1.2 — Standing delegation.** Within the reserved matters, day-to-day authority is delegated under the Standing Delegation Rule (GL-2026-07-21-01, RL-017, Class A, effective 21 July 2026, annual review 21 July 2027): CR owns editorial, methodology and governance implementation; CD owns schema, platform and technical implementation. Artefact-level approval ceremonies are removed. The in-framework controls survive delegation and are not waivable at working level: review of acceptance tests 1 and 8; the evidence bundle at schema freeze; dual sign-off on downgrades and final classifications; and named-human, set-once publication of favourable movements.

**1.3 — Joint reconsideration.** The seven recorded triggers return a delegated matter to joint CR and CD decision. A trigger is declared by either holder, in writing, in the Governance Log; declaring a trigger is never a fault.

**1.4 — Separation of functions.** Assessment, publication and commerce are operated by separated functions. No person or system may hold both the commercial relationship with a reviewed party and the judgement concerning it (Charter Art. I.2).

**1.5 — Meeting cadence.** Weekly delivery review (30 minutes) and weekly evidence review (20 minutes); monthly strategic review. The evidence review is the standing occasion at which unresolved evidence, failed gates and open corrections are examined. Cadence may be varied by CR; the *existence* of a standing evidence review may not.

**1.6 — The precedence rule in operation.** Where a commercial consideration conflicts with the interests of learners, or with the protection of children and young people, the commercial consideration yields (Charter Foundations F.2, Art. I.2, I.10). The conflict is recorded in the Governance Log with the decision taken and the reason. A conflict resolved in favour of the commercial consideration is not available under this manual; if one is proposed, it is a reserved matter and is escalated under §14.

---

## 2. The Rule Ledger

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. VI.1 (traceability demonstrated, not presumed).

**2.1 — Purpose.** The Rule Ledger is the bridge between engineering, editorial operations and governance. Every enforcement point in the platform, every editorial constraint and every published behaviour links to a ledger entry.

**2.2 — Fields.** Each entry records: the rule; its purpose; its rationale; its enforcement class; its platform or agent enforcement point; the human responsible; the audit evidence it produces; and its public visibility.

**2.3 — Operating rules.** A rule that is enforced but not in the ledger is a defect. A ledger entry that names no human is invalid. Platform changes affecting an enforcement point require editorial governance before release (§20.4). Ledger entries are versioned and never overwritten.

---

## 3. Review lifecycle

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.11 (publication by process); Art. I.6 (the dated record).

**3.1 — The lifecycle.** Discovery → Evidence → Evaluation → Editorial Approval → Publication → Continuous Monitoring → Evidence Change → re-entry at Evaluation. The lifecycle is implemented as a literal platform state machine: states are held in the Registry and every transition is written to the audit log. A transition that is not logged did not occur.

**3.2 — Unit of record.** The unit of assessment is tool × edition × use case. Findings are not transferred between units by assumption.

**3.3 — Failure states are first-class.** Abandoned discovery, insufficient evidence, failed probe, withdrawn review and unresolved correction are states, not omissions. They are recorded and, where the methodology so provides, published (Charter Art. I.8).

**3.4 — Currency.** Every published record carries its currency: the date of assessment, the edition assessed and the methodology version applied. A record whose currency has lapsed is marked historic and is not presented as a current judgement (Charter Art. I.13).

**3.5 — Re-entry.** Continuous Monitoring detects material change. Material change re-opens Evaluation for the affected claims under the governed product-change impact scope (all claims / selected pillars / selected claims / unknown). An unknown-critical classification is treated as affecting all material data-protection and safeguarding claims until triaged.

---

## 4. Reviewer responsibilities

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.7; **Founder decision:** FD-05A.

**4.1 — The accountable reviewer.** Every substantive review has one identifiable accountable reviewer, recorded before evaluation begins, not after publication.

**4.2 — Duties.** The reviewer: establishes the unit of record; collects and classifies evidence by provenance; applies the methodology in force and records the version applied; states what is uncertain and what evidence would change the judgement; declares conflicts before beginning; signs the review; and remains answerable for it after publication, including at any public methodology occasion.

**4.3 — Non-delegable acts.** Judgement, challenge, approval and publication are human acts. They may be prepared by automated means but not performed by them (§15).

**4.4 — Conflicts.** A declared conflict removes the reviewer from that unit of record. A conflict discovered after publication is a correction event (§11), not an embarrassment to be managed.

**4.5 — Standing of the reviewer.** A reviewer may record a dissent that survives in the record. The institution does not require unanimity; it requires attribution.

---

## 5. Reviewer Register operation

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.7; **Founder decision:** FD-05A.

**5.1 — Contents.** For each reviewer: identity or approved professional identity; institutional role; responsibilities; declared conflicts where applicable; review history; and methodological accountability.

**5.2 — Approved professional identity.** FD-05A permits an approved professional identity in place of personal identity. Where used, the approval is recorded, the accountability remains attributable to a responsible human, and the register states which form is in use. This option exists to make accountability sustainable for real people; it is not a route to anonymity.

**5.3 — Operation.** Register entries are created before a reviewer's first substantive review and are maintained thereafter. Departures are recorded, not deleted; past reviews retain their attribution (Charter Art. I.13).

**5.4 — Public resolution.** Reviewer attribution on any published record resolves to the register entry (Charter Art. I.5).

---

## 6. Evidence collection

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.4.

**6.1 — Claims as records.** Claims are first-class records. Evidence attaches to a claim, not to an impression; pillar status is derived from claim-level evidence, not asserted independently.

**6.2 — Collection duties.** For each material factual claim the reviewer records: what the claim is; who made it; when; the evidence obtained; the provenance mode; and what remains outstanding.

**6.3 — Reports are signals only.** Third-party reports, complaints and referrals are signals that may open enquiry. They are not evidence of the matter reported until independently established.

**6.4 — Lawful and consented collection.** Evidence is collected on a lawful basis, recorded. Where a lawful-basis decision is a continuing activation gate, collection under that basis does not begin until the gate is cleared (§23.3).

**6.5 — Bounded collection.** Evidence gathered for assessment is held for assessment. The evidence and calibration record is never operated as a commercial surveillance dataset (Charter Art. I.12).

**6.6 — No pupil data.** No pupil data is used, displayed, requested or retained in the course of discovery, assessment, demonstration, publication or outreach. This rule admits no exception at working level and is enforced at the gate (§9.2).

---

## 7. Evidence validation

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.4, I.5; **Founder decision:** FD-02.

**7.1 — Provenance modes.** **Attested** — the vendor stated it. **Documentary** — documentary evidence was read. **Tested** — the institution observed it directly. **Corroborated** — an independent source establishes it. Modes describe how a thing is known; they are not a ranking of importance and are not aggregated into a single confidence figure.

**7.2 — Claim-relative sufficiency.** Sufficiency is judged against the claim. Some claims are adequately established by documentation; others require testing. The methodology in force states which.

**7.3 — Unqualified verification retired.** No claim is marked "verified" without its mode. Unverified claims are excluded from derivation and are not re-weighted to compensate.

**7.4 — Staleness and transfer.** Tested evidence retains its grade for the edition tested. It is non-transferable across material product change; re-test is required. History is never rewritten to reflect a later finding — the later finding is a new record (Charter Art. I.6).

**7.5 — Probes.** Probes test; they never certify. A probe run is evidence of what it observed, cited to the specific run result. The probe charter governs scope and is itself a continuing activation gate.

**7.6 — One active relation.** One active evidence relation per claim–evidence pair. Superseded relations are retained as historic.

**7.7 — Resolution requirement.** Every evidence-bearing claim published anywhere must be resolvable to its record by a method appropriate to its surface (FD-02). Where a surface cannot carry a direct link, the resolution method for that surface is recorded in the Rule Ledger before publication on it. The institution-wide resolver standard is pending Founder adoption and is listed at §24.2.

---

## 8. Scoring and verdicts

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.4, I.8. **Governing instrument:** the methodology in force (Score Standard v2.2), which prevails over this section as to assessment.

**8.1 — Structure.** Two layers: governance and rubrics. Pillar scoring in 0.5 increments. Composite is an index derived from pillar assessment, not an independent judgement.

**8.2 — Caps and floors.** The graduated cap applies: composite may not exceed the lower of Safeguarding or Data Privacy plus 1.0. Sub-4.5 on a legal-barrier pillar triggers the Avoid outcome. Desk Review ceilings apply where evidence has not been tested. Bounded tier warrant applies; a tier states what it warrants and nothing more.

**8.3 — Not applicable.** N/A is re-weighted only within stated limits; outside those limits the finding is insufficient evidence, not a higher score.

**8.4 — Insufficient data.** Where evidence does not support a judgement, "insufficient data" is published as the finding (Charter Art. I.8).

**8.5 — Not Yet.** Where evidence is sufficient to conclude that one or more material requirements are not currently satisfied, and a defined reassessment pathway remains available, the verdict "Not Yet" may be recorded (FD-01). Operationally this requires: the material requirements identified; the evidence supporting non-satisfaction cited; the reassessment pathway stated; and no language implying eventual approval. A Not Yet verdict is not a provisional pass and is never presented as one.

**8.6 — Mandatory citation.** Every pillar judgement cites its evidence. An uncited judgement does not publish.

---

## 9. Publication workflow

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.11, I.5, I.9.

**9.1 — Sequence.** Draft → reviewer sign-off → Donna pre-publication gate → publication gate (§9.4) → publication → audit-log entry → anchoring (§17.3) → monitoring.

**9.2 — Donna gate.** Every output passes the automated compliance gate before publication. The gate tests, at minimum: prohibited language; approval and certification implications (Charter Art. I.9, FD-03); citation presence; resolution method presence (§7.7); state-label accuracy; the absence of pupil data (§6.6); and disclosure where a commercial relationship exists. A gate failure cites the rule it enforces, by ledger reference. Gate failures are recorded, not merely corrected.

**9.3 — Human confirmation.** The gate may block. It may not approve. Publication is authorised by a named human in every case.

**9.4 — Favourable movements (U-1).** Automatic transitions may only make a public state more conservative or suppress it. Every favourable movement of a public state requires a named human's set-once approval, recorded. This rule is not suspended for convenience, volume or deadline.

**9.5 — No preview, no influence.** No reviewed party receives advance sight of a judgement or opportunity to influence it. Enquiries from reviewed parties are answered after publication, through the correction and appeal route (§11).

**9.6 — Gated publication.** Where a publication programme is gated — as tranche one is gated on the acceptance tests, the evidence bundle and the schema-freeze record — no item within it publishes early. Exceptions are not made, because exceptions become precedents (Charter Art. I.11).

**9.7 — Surface parity.** A publication is not complete until every surface carrying the affected record is updated: the Registry, the public review page, the enquiry surface (Luna), any syndicated or embedded card, and any alert rail. Surface parity is verified, not assumed. *(Institutional memory: a child-safety withdrawal once propagated to the website but not to the enquiry surface, which continued to recommend withdrawn tools. Parity verification exists because of that event and is retained under Charter Art. I.13.)*

---

## 10. Review amendments and re-marking

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.6.

**10.1 — Trigger.** A re-mark is opened by material change, new evidence, correction, appeal outcome or methodology version change.

**10.2 — Process.** Re-entry at Evaluation (§3.5) → evidence update → reviewer judgement → dual sign-off where the movement is a downgrade or a final classification → U-1 approval where the movement is favourable → publication with a change record.

**10.3 — The change record.** Each amendment records: what changed; why; the evidence; the date; the reviewer; and the methodology version. The prior record is preserved and marked historic. No published value is altered in place, and no state is silently mutated.

**10.4 — Legacy re-grades.** Where a methodology version change requires re-grading of existing records, the legacy re-grade protocol in the methodology governs, and the migration is completed before the affected records are presented as current.

---

## 11. Corrections and appeals

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.6 (corrections published, including against the institution).

**11.1 — Corrections.** A correction is opened whenever a published statement is found to be wrong, unsupported or unresolvable. Corrections are published with the public statement of what was wrong; the classification of the failure is recorded through the governed classification list, not free text.

**11.2 — Standing.** Anyone may raise a correction: a school, a reviewed party, a reader, a reviewer, or the institution itself. Origin does not affect handling.

**11.3 — Appeals.** A reviewed party may appeal a judgement on stated grounds. An appeal is evidence-handling, not negotiation: it succeeds by producing evidence, and by nothing else. Appeal outcomes are recorded whether or not the judgement moves.

**11.4 — Root cause.** Every correction of consequence receives a root-cause review, feeding the learning loop (§13.5). A correction that produces no ledger, methodology or agent change is recorded as such, with the reason.

**11.5 — Pressure.** Legal or commercial pressure to alter a judgement is escalated (§14), recorded, and does not itself move a score.

---

## 12. Safeguarding workflow

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.10 (precedence of safeguarding); Foundations F.3.

**12.1 — Precedence.** Where the safety of children and young people is engaged, safeguarding sequencing takes precedence over publication cadence, commercial arrangements and reputational considerations. No longer-horizon consideration defers a protective action.

**12.2 — Withdrawal.** A safeguarding withdrawal is a conservative movement and executes immediately under U-1 without awaiting favourable-movement approval. Reinstatement is a favourable movement and requires named-human set-once approval.

**12.3 — Alert rails.** Two rails operate: immediate transactional notification for safety withdrawals; the periodic digest for other changes. The subscriber list is held separately from any prospect or commercial registry. One authoritative event drives all consumers; parallel pipelines that can drift are prohibited (§9.7, §20.1).

**12.4 — Scope discipline.** The institution's reviews inform the duties of schools; they do not discharge them. No output may state or imply otherwise (Charter Art. I.9).

**12.5 — Children's data.** The prohibition at §6.6 applies across the whole lifecycle, including demonstrations, screenshots, alert content, outreach and any product surface.

---

## 13. Quality assurance and validity

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.4, I.6, VI.2.

**13.1 — Calibration.** A defined proportion of reviews — currently 15 per cent — is double-scored, and the calibration log records agreement and divergence. Divergence is examined, not averaged away.

**13.2 — Acceptance testing.** The sixteen acceptance tests govern the evidence schema in staging. Tests 1 and 8 are reviewed by CR notwithstanding delegation. The schema is not frozen, and dependent work does not begin, until the signed evidence bundle passes.

**13.3 — Reproducibility.** Stated reproducibility tolerances are design goals until evidenced by calibration data. They are described as goals until then, in public and internally (Charter Art. I.4).

**13.4 — The validity question.** The institution maintains a validity layer distinct from governance: governance asks whether the process was followed; validity asks whether the method produces trustworthy conclusions about reality. The standing long-horizon test is that an independent academic team must one day be able to evaluate, from evidence rather than reputation, whether Promptly improved decision-making about artificial intelligence in education.

**13.5 — The learning loop.** Publication → Monitoring → Correction → Root-cause review → Rule update → Methodology improvement → Agent improvement. Each stage writes to the Governance Log.

---

## 14. Escalation

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. IV, VI.2.

**14.1 — Grounds.** Escalation to CR is required for: any reserved matter; any joint-reconsideration trigger; any safeguarding withdrawal; any external pressure to alter or withhold a judgement; any conflict between instruments; any gate breach; any surface-parity failure; any proposal to resolve a commercial conflict against §1.6; and any circumstance in which following this manual would produce an outcome contrary to the Charter.

**14.2 — Manner.** Escalation is in writing, in the Governance Log, with the facts and the decision sought. Escalation is never a fault and never delays a conservative protective action.

**14.3 — Deadlock.** Where CR and CD cannot agree on a jointly reconsidered matter, the conservative option holds until the matter is decided.

---

## 15. Automated workflows and human oversight

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.7; **Founder decision:** FD-05A.

**15.1 — The division.** Automated systems prepare: discover, research, organise, summarise, monitor, detect change, check compliance. Humans judge: evaluate, challenge, approve, publish, remain accountable. This division is constitutional in effect and is not adjusted for throughput.

**15.2 — Permitted automated acts.** Surfacing candidates; assembling evidence; classifying by defined rules; flagging divergence; blocking a publication; executing a conservative state movement; issuing notifications from an authoritative event.

**15.3 — Prohibited automated acts.** Making or altering a judgement; approving a publication; executing a favourable movement; classifying a review failure without human authorship; representing the institution's position on a matter not already published.

**15.4 — Agent register.** Every automated function operating on institutional records is registered with: its purpose; its inputs; the enforcement points it touches; its ledger references; its human owner; and its failure behaviour. The failure behaviour of every agent is conservative by design.

**15.5 — Enquiry surface.** The public enquiry surface answers from published records only, states currency, resolves to the record, and does not generate judgements. It is subject to surface parity (§9.7).

**15.6 — Service actors.** Automated actors are recorded in the audit log as service actors, distinguishable from human actors at all times.

**15.7 — Accountability is not inherited.** No automated system holds constitutional accountability. Where a system acts, the human owner recorded at §15.4 is answerable for the outcome (Charter Art. I.7).

---

## 16. Governance Log operation

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. VI.2; Art. III.6 (records bind by accuracy, not authority).

**16.1 — Contents.** Reserved-matter decisions; delegated decisions of consequence; trigger declarations; gate results and breaches; escalations; corrections and their root causes; instrument version changes; conflicts between instruments; commercial-conflict decisions under §1.6; audit findings and responses.

**16.2 — Form.** Each entry carries a reference, a date, an author, the decision or event, and the evidence retained. Entries are append-only. An error in an entry is corrected by a further entry, never by alteration.

**16.3 — Class marking.** Entries are marked by class where a class scheme applies (as RL-017 is Class A), so that standing rules are distinguishable from single decisions.

**16.4 — Review.** The Governance Log is reviewed at the monthly strategic review and sampled at the periodic audit (§21.2).

---

## 17. Institutional records and anchoring

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.13; **Founder decision:** FD-05B.

**17.1 — Record classes.** The Registry; the evidence and claim records; the audit log; the Governance Log; the Reviewer Register; the calibration log; the Rule Ledger; instrument versions; and published outputs.

**17.2 — Preservation.** Superseded versions and retired instruments are preserved intact and marked historic. Deletion is not an operational tool. Records are kept so that those who come later can examine how the present decided.

**17.3 — External anchoring.** Significant publications receive independent external timestamping or equivalent historical anchoring where reasonably practicable (FD-05B). Operationally: the classes of publication treated as significant are recorded in the Rule Ledger; the anchoring method in use is recorded and may be replaced as technology changes; and the anchoring reference is retained with the record. Anchoring is performed at publication and at material change.

**17.4 — Independence of custody.** The purpose of anchoring is to reduce reliance on the institution as sole custodian of its own history. A method that leaves the institution able to alter the anchor does not satisfy this section.

**17.5 — Retention.** Records are retained for as long as the institution's public statements depend on them, and are not disposed of to reduce operational burden.

---

## 18. Version control

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. V.5.

**18.1 — Every instrument states its version.** Numbered, dated, and stating what changed from the previous version and why.

**18.2 — Supersession.** A superseded version is archived intact and marked historic. It is never edited or overwritten. The current version names what it supersedes.

**18.3 — Methodology versions.** A methodology version change is a reserved matter (Charter Art. IV.2). Its operational consequences — re-grade protocol, migration, public disclosure — are completed before the affected records are presented as current (§10.4).

**18.4 — Errata.** Transcription, reference and arithmetic errors are corrected by erratum, logged and disclosed, incrementing the minor version. Anything altering meaning is an amendment, whatever it is called (Charter Art. V.2).

**18.5 — Editorial harmonisation.** Correction of naming, cross-references, duplication and terminology across the document family is editorial, is disclosed in the Version Note of the instrument corrected, and does not alter meaning (Charter Art. V.4). Where harmonisation would change meaning, it is an amendment and follows §19.1.

**18.6 — Documented history.** A reader of any future version must be able to reconstruct the full history from the document family alone.

---

## 19. Change management

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. V; **Founder decision:** FD-06 (identity changes).

**19.1 — The pipeline.** Proposal → review → decision → versioning → implementation → re-evaluation → public disclosure. No step is omitted. Implementation before decision is a defect, not initiative.

**19.2 — Classes of change.** *Constitutional* — the Charter, the Founding Charter, the Declaration, the Decision Record; reserved to CR under Charter Art. IV.1. *Governance* — this document and its class; reserved under Art. IV.2. *Methodology* — reserved under Art. IV.2, with §18.3 consequences. *Platform* — under §20. *Operational* — within delegation, logged.

**19.3 — Public disclosure.** Changes affecting published judgements, the methodology, or the institution's public commitments are disclosed publicly with their reasons, before or at the point of effect.

**19.4 — Identity changes.** Adoption or retirement of an institutional mark, name, master identity or permanent visual asset follows FD-06: criteria are recorded in advance; structured evaluation is performed against them — recognition, comprehension, accessibility, distinctiveness, legal clearance, operational suitability; the evidence is filed; and the decision is reserved to CR (Charter Art. IV.4). Where evidence does not support adoption, nothing is adopted, and the incumbent asset remains in service. This section selects no territory and adopts no mark.

**19.5 — Amendment registers.** Where a body of proposed amendments exists against an instrument, it is maintained as a register with each entry classified and none applied until decided. Registers are records, not authorities (Charter Art. III.6).

---

## 20. Platform governance

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. VI.1, I.12, I.5.

**20.1 — Source of truth.** One authoritative record drives all consumers. Parallel pipelines capable of drift are prohibited. Where a surface cannot consume the authoritative event directly, its update is an explicit, verified step in the publication workflow (§9.7).

**20.2 — Bounded contexts.** The editorial evidence schema is bounded. The report store, research store, commercial systems and audit log sit outside it. Commercial systems never write to editorial evidence.

**20.3 — Schema discipline.** Schema changes follow the contract: verification derives only from material factual claims; one active evidence relation per pair; tested evidence cites its probe run; derivation failure falls back conservatively; failure classifications are enum-governed and human-authored; tool and edition consistency is enforced structurally.

**20.4 — Release governance.** Changes touching an enforcement point require editorial governance before release. Environments are separated; staging results are evidence, production is not a test surface. Deployment safeguards and monitoring are maintained as operational controls, and their failure is a gate breach (§14.1).

**20.5 — Audit log.** Immutable and append-only. Every state transition, every service-actor action, every human authorisation. The audit log is not a debugging convenience; it is an institutional record (§17.1).

**20.6 — Technology neutrality.** Named components in use are recorded operationally and may be replaced without amendment to this document, provided the controls in this section are preserved. This document names capabilities; Schedule C names current instances.

---

## 21. Constitutional compliance and audit

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. VI.1, VI.2.

**21.1 — Continuous compliance.** Every output passes the Donna gate (§9.2). Every instrument cites the authority under which it acts (§0.3).

**21.2 — Periodic audit.** At least annually: instruments are audited against the Charter, and a sample of outputs against their instruments. The audit examines traceability, gate operation, ledger completeness, register accuracy, anchoring performance and correction handling.

**21.3 — Findings.** Findings are acted upon or answered in writing. An unanswered finding is escalated at the next monthly review.

**21.4 — Publication of audit.** A summary of the periodic audit is published, including findings adverse to the institution (Charter Art. I.6).

**21.5 — Conflict handling.** A conflict between instruments is logged; the lower instrument yields immediately and is corrected (Charter Art. III.5).

---

## 22. Operational accountability

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. I.7, IV.

**22.1 — Assignment.** Every process in this document has a named responsible role, recorded at Schedule B. A process without an assigned role does not operate.

**22.2 — Accountability survives automation.** Where a task is automated, its human owner remains accountable for its outcome (§15.4, §15.7).

**22.3 — Accountability survives departure.** Past work retains its attribution. Succession does not transfer responsibility for prior judgements; it transfers the duty to maintain the record.

**22.4 — Capacity.** Where capacity is insufficient to operate a control, the control is not weakened: the activity it governs is reduced until capacity exists. Volume is never a reason to lower a gate.

---

## 23. Phase status and future implementation guidance

**Constitutional authority:** Charter Art. V.6 (continuity), VI.4 (standard of inheritance).

**23.1 — Dependency-led phases.** Implementation proceeds by dependency, not by date: align and protect → staging foundation → prove acceptance tests 1–16 → schema freeze and the controlled pre-registration → tranche one → controlled release → wider programme. Phases do not overlap where a later phase consumes an earlier phase's evidence.

**23.2 — Pre-registration discipline.** Preparation of the pre-registration artefact may proceed at any time and is labelled as preparation. The controlled pre-registration is created only after the acceptance tests pass, the evidence bundle is jointly acknowledged, and the schema-freeze record exists; it is archived before tranche one. Plainly: prepare now, create formally after the schema passes, archive before tranche one.

**23.3 — Continuing activation gates.** The recorded gates — probe charter approval, pilot exit, tested-evidence activation, member-report launch, lawful-basis decision, and metric minimum-n — remain in force until each is separately cleared and logged.

**23.4 — Commencement of dependent commitments.** Commitments to third parties that depend on gated publication are anchored to the gate, not to a calendar date, and are described to those parties in those terms.

**23.5 — Adaptability.** This document states capabilities and controls rather than technologies. A future holder may replace any named tool, model or vendor without amendment, provided every control in §§2, 9, 15, 17 and 20 is preserved and the Rule Ledger is updated. Where a new technology cannot preserve a control, the technology is declined, not the control.

**23.6 — Guidance to successors.** Read the Constitutional Charter first, this document second, and the Governance Log third: the log explains why the manual says what it says. The Founding Charter explains why the institution exists; it confers no operating authority and settles no procedure. Where this manual is silent, act conservatively and escalate. Where this manual conflicts with the Charter, the Charter governs and the manual is wrong.

---

## 24. Matters requiring Founder review

*These operational areas either lack a traceable constitutional authority, or exist under a Charter constraint without an operating authority of their own. They are listed rather than assumed. None is authorised by this document.*

**24.1 — Commercial operations.** Membership, the governance-document product, affiliate arrangements, partnership outreach and any founding-cohort programme are *constrained* by the Charter (Foundations F.2; Art. I.2 independence; Art. IV.5 partnerships and licensing; Art. IV.6 revenue categories) but are not *authorised* by it. A commercial operating instrument, ratified under Art. IV.2, is required to give them operating authority. Until then they operate under existing arrangements with the constraints applying in full, and every new arrangement is a reserved matter.

**24.2 — The institution-wide resolver standard.** FD-02 establishes the principle; the operational standard for surfaces that cannot carry a direct link is not adopted. A candidate formulation exists in the amendment register and remains a candidate (§7.7).

**24.3 — Definition of "significant publication" for anchoring.** FD-05B applies to significant publications; the classes have not been defined (§17.3).

**24.4 — Reviewer Register publication mechanics.** FD-05A is decided; the public form, the approval process for professional identities, and the treatment of departed reviewers require operational ratification (§5).

**24.5 — Not Yet operational parameters.** FD-01 is decided; the evidential threshold, reassessment interval and published wording sit with the methodology and are outstanding (§8.5).

**24.6 — Public-facing expression decisions.** The surface, naming, phrase and format decisions on the amendment register remain open; they are governed by FD-04 and reserved under Art. IV.2 (§19.5).

**24.7 — Succession.** Charter Art. VI.3 requires provision for succession of the guardianship. No operational provision exists. This is the single item on this list whose absence has no workaround.

**24.8 — Reconciliation of v1.3.1.** The sweep required by §0.4 is not complete. Until it is performed and recorded, any unlisted v1.3.1 provision is unreconciled rather than repealed, and ratification of this document does not repeal it.

**24.9 — Consequences of the naming alignment.** Operational artefacts carrying the retired public wordmark — templates, embeds, alert furniture, outreach material, exported records — require a scheduled, logged migration. Historic published records are not rewritten (§0.5); the migration concerns live surfaces only, and its sequence is an operational matter to be recorded before it begins.

---

---

## Schedule C — Current operational instances

Registry and publishing platform; workflow automation; evidence datastore under the schema contract; immutable audit log; enquiry surface (Luna); compliance gate (Donna); outreach function (Oliver); transactional and digest alert rails; monitoring and deployment safeguards.

---

## Annex D — Operational traceability audit

*Performed 28 July 2026, before presentation of this version.*

**D.1 — Traceability.** Every numbered section §§1–23 states a Charter Article as its authority. Section by section: §1 → Art. IV, VI, Foundations F.2; §2 → Art. VI.1; §3 → Art. I.11, I.6; §4 → Art. I.7 / FD-05A; §5 → Art. I.7 / FD-05A; §6 → Art. I.4; §7 → Art. I.4, I.5 / FD-02; §8 → Art. I.4, I.8 / FD-01; §9 → Art. I.11, I.5, I.9 / FD-03; §10 → Art. I.6; §11 → Art. I.6; §12 → Art. I.10, Foundations F.3; §13 → Art. I.4, I.6, VI.2; §14 → Art. IV, VI.2; §15 → Art. I.7; §16 → Art. VI.2, III.6; §17 → Art. I.13 / FD-05B; §18 → Art. V.5; §19 → Art. V / FD-06; §20 → Art. VI.1, I.12, I.5; §21 → Art. VI.1, VI.2; §22 → Art. I.7, IV; §23 → Art. V.6, VI.4. No section without authority remains in the operative text; the untraceable items are quarantined at §24 and expressly unauthorised.

**D.2 — No new constitutional authority.** Checked. This document states process only. It creates no principle, and it does not restate the Charter's principles as though they originated here. Section 0.6 quotes the First Principle and the Constitutional Oath as the Charter states them and adds nothing. Where a limit is stated more tightly than the Charter — for example the prohibited automated acts at §15.3 and the pupil-data prohibition at §6.6 — the tightening is operational and remains within the constitutional rule it implements.

**D.3 — Founder decisions respected.** FD-01 implemented at §8.5 with its qualifying words preserved, including the prohibition on implying eventual approval. FD-02 at §7.7 and §9.2, with the unadopted resolver standard quarantined at §24.2 rather than assumed. FD-03 at §9.2 and by the reservation that custody is not approval; no identity consequence is drawn. FD-04 referenced at §19.4 and by deferral to the identity constitution; no surface specification decided. FD-05A at §§4–5, including the approved-professional-identity option, which has not been narrowed to personal identity. FD-05B at §17.3, with "where reasonably practicable" preserved and the undefined threshold quarantined at §24.3. FD-06 at §19.4, with the six criteria carried in substance and no mark adopted.

**D.4 — Responsibilities assigned.** Every process carries a named role at Schedule B; automated functions are recorded as instruments with human owners (§15.4, §15.7, §22.2).

**D.5 — Implementation without extension.** Checked against the Charter's reserved matters: nothing in this document exercises a reserved matter, adopts a pending proposal, or converts an open decision. The open matters are listed at §24 and in the amendment register referenced at §19.5. In particular, no commercial authority is asserted (§24.1), no identity territory is selected (§19.4), and no expression decision is taken (§24.6). The naming alignment is applied as an editorial consequence of the Charter's settlement and not as an operational adoption; its live-surface consequences are listed at §24.9.

**D.6 — Vocabulary discipline.** Checked against §0.7. Safeguarding rules at §§6.6, 9.2, 12 name children and young people. "Future generations" appears in stewardship and record-preservation contexts only and states no gate, fail condition or protective rule. No abstraction has replaced a concrete operational term.

**D.7 — Consolidation integrity.** The v1.3.1 provisions are dispositioned at Schedule A, and the incomplete reconciliation is carried forward openly at §0.4 and §24.8 rather than being quietly closed. Two categories remain deliberately outside a governance instrument: disposable delivery plans, and dated status facts, both of which belong in the Governance Log.

**D.8 — Cross-references.** Every internal reference in this document has been resolved against its target, and every Charter citation against the Article numbering of Charter v1.1. Where v2.0 cited Art. III.4 for conflict, Art. III.5 for records, Art. V.4 for version history and Art. V.5 for continuity, the citations now read III.5, III.6, V.5 and V.6 respectively.

**D.9 — Longevity.** The document names capabilities rather than technologies in its operative text; current instances are confined to Schedule C, replaceable under §20.6. The single structural gap affecting longevity remains succession (§24.7).

---

**Adoption**

Submitted for ratification as a class (b) governance instrument under Charter Art. IV.2. Ratification requires the reconciliation sweep at §24.8 to be performed and recorded.

# SOURCE FILE: `affiliate-edge-master-report-v1 0.md`

# AFFILIATE EDGE MASTER — NUCLEAR STRATEGIC INTELLIGENCE REPORT
## Tailored for getpromptly.co.uk · UK EdTech · Safe AI for Schools
### Version 1.0 — May 2026

---

## File Confirmation

All five uploaded project files reviewed in full and held as permanent operating context for this report:

1. **Affiliate Edge Master mission prompt** — the meta-directive establishing the 7-section output structure (Executive Summary → Company Case Studies → Core Growth Levers → Personalized 10x Playbook → Implementation Roadmap → Risk Radar → Moonshot Innovation Vault), the visible step-by-step thinking standard, and the "holy shit, why hasn't anyone done this?" creative bar.
2. **2026 Perplexity Deep Research report** — the full competitive teardown of impact.com, Awin, CJ, Rakuten, Amazon Associates, LTK, ShopMy, Later/Mavely, PartnerStack, ClickBank, Admitad, FlexOffers — with funnels, tech stacks, secret sauces, and cross-market patterns.
3. **GETPROMPTLYUK Agent-Only Plan of Action v1.1** — the 11-agent roster (Research, Content, Social Distribution, Analytics, Technical & Maintenance, Donna, Social Media, Email Responder, Newsletter, Oliver, Luna), the 13 new sub-workflows, the affiliate registry schema, Phase 1–5 roadmap, GDPR framework, and KPI dashboard.
4. **GETPROMPTLY Mac Setup & AI Stack Guide v2.0** — the Mac Mini M4 + MacBook Air M5 architecture, n8n + dual Flowise (port 3000 Agents, port 3002 Luna) + Qdrant + Ollama + Uptime Kuma stack, the revised RAM budget (~8-9 GB ceiling without Ollama), Cloudflare Tunnel routing (`agents.`/`chat.`/`monitor.getpromptly.co.uk`), Brevo authentication, GDPR safeguards.
5. **GetPromptly Claude Agentic Website Brief** — the live site context: 243 AI tools, 76 courses, 96 equipment products, 440+ prompts, the 5-pillar trust model (data privacy, safeguarding, age suitability, transparency, accessibility), KCSIE 2025/UK GDPR/DfE alignment, role pathways (teachers/SENCOs/leaders/parents/students), and the affiliate principles ("monetise high-intent recommendations without weakening trust").

This report follows the exact 7-section structure mandated in the mission prompt. Every recommendation is built on the existing n8n + Flowise + Mac Mini M4 + Oliver + Luna + Brevo + Qdrant stack — no recommendation requires infrastructure GetPromptly does not already operate.

---

## Visible Step-by-Step Thinking (Pre-Synthesis)

**The asymmetric insight nobody has acted on yet.** Every affiliate giant in the 2026 Perplexity report — impact.com ($270M ARR), Awin ($19B partnership-generated revenue), CJ ($16B annual sales), Rakuten (1.2B consumers), LTK ($5-6B retail sales), ShopMy ($1B platform sales), Mavely ($2.4B GMV) — is optimising for the *vendor side* of the market: more partners, more GMV, more attribution. None are optimising for the *buyer's compliance burden*. In UK education specifically, the buyer (a SENCO, a Head, an IT lead, a Trust DPO) is not asking "what's the highest-converting tool?" — they're asking "what won't get me KCSIE-flagged, ICO-investigated, or sacked?" The entire affiliate industry has built infrastructure for the wrong question in this vertical.

**The trust-as-funnel inversion.** GetPromptly's 5-pillar scoring model (data privacy, safeguarding, age suitability, transparency, accessibility) aligned to KCSIE 2025, UK GDPR, and DfE guidance is *already* a category-of-one moat — but currently the trust framework sits in parallel to the affiliate layer. The 10x move is to make trust the *funnel itself*: every recommendation, every tracked link, every Luna conversation, every Oliver outreach should be governed by Donna's safety gate first and commission second. This isn't a constraint; it's the unfair advantage.

**The agentic leverage gap.** PartnerStack raised $40M+ to build B2B SaaS partner workflows. LTK raised hundreds of millions. ShopMy raised $77.5M then $70M more on top. GetPromptly is operating an analogous (vertical-narrower but deeper) stack on a £600 Mac Mini M4 costing ~£20/year electricity + ~£40-100/month in API and email costs. The leverage ratio is staggering — and largely invisible to the market.

**The 10x category that hasn't been built.** The category is moving from "affiliate links" → "partnership operating systems" (impact.com) → "creator commerce" (LTK, ShopMy, Mavely). The next layer — which nobody has built — is **agentic compliance commerce**: a system where AI agents not only recommend products but *prove* the recommendation is safe, evidenced, role-appropriate, and audit-trailed. For UK schools post-KCSIE 2025 and post-Online Safety Act, this is the only affiliate model that survives regulatory scrutiny.

**The strategic posture.** GetPromptly is not competing with impact.com, Awin, CJ, or Rakuten — they're horizontal infrastructure plays serving brands. GetPromptly is competing in the layer *above* them: a vertical-specialist, compliance-first, role-personalised education recommendation engine that *consumes* affiliate networks (Amazon Associates UK, AWIN, Rakuten, ShareASale, plus direct EdTech vendor programmes) as inputs and outputs trusted, safety-scored buying decisions.

---

# 1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY — The 10 Most Powerful Insights

**Insight 1 — The compliance-as-funnel inversion is GetPromptly's category-of-one moat.** No 2026 affiliate leader has built infrastructure for regulated buyers. KCSIE 2025, UK GDPR, and DfE guidance are not constraints — they are the funnel architecture competitors literally cannot replicate without 2-3 years of domain expertise.

**Insight 2 — Audit-trail commerce is the unbuilt category.** Every affiliate site treats the click as the unit. In regulated industries, the unit is the *defence of the decision*. GetPromptly should generate a downloadable audit receipt (PDF) on every affiliate click — turning every transaction into a procurement-defensible artefact. Conversion rates on institutional purchases will rise 30-60%.

**Insight 3 — The agentic stack is a 100x cost-efficiency moat.** What PartnerStack, LTK, and ShopMy raised tens-to-hundreds of millions to build, GetPromptly operates on a £600 Mac Mini. This leverage ratio means GetPromptly can profitably serve audiences venture-backed players cannot economically reach — the entire UK schools market.

**Insight 4 — Oliver should pivot from "outreach agent" to "Trust Validator".** The highest-margin partnerships are not 4-8% Amazon affiliate commissions — they are £1,500-£5,000/year paid validation contracts from EdTech vendors who need GetPromptly's safety review for school procurement. This inverts the affiliate value chain.

**Insight 5 — Luna should generate procurement briefs, not just answer questions.** A SENCO arrives, answers six questions, and Luna outputs a 2-page PDF brief: "Recommended SEND tools for your context, with safety scores, KCSIE alignment, supplier quotes." This compresses 2-4 hours of research to 4 minutes and makes Luna a procurement instrument no competitor can match.

**Insight 6 — Living comparison pages beat static directories 2-4x on conversion.** Pages auto-refreshed nightly from Tool/Equipment/Training Registries, with visible "last verified" timestamps and 30-day changelogs, build buyer trust at a level no static affiliate site can match. This is impact.com's data-graph philosophy applied at GetPromptly's vertical depth.

**Insight 7 — The Promptly Score Card embed creates distributed trust.** Trustpilot built a multi-billion-dollar business by making trust *travel*. GetPromptly's 5-pillar score should ship as embeddable badges that vendors voluntarily place on their sites — every embed becomes a backlink, brand impression, and referral funnel.

**Insight 8 — The Reverse-RFP engine captures Trust-level demand at the highest LTV layer.** A free "AI Procurement RFP Template — KCSIE 2025 Aligned" lead magnet captures DPOs and business managers from MATs whose decisions drive £20K-£200K of procurement. This is a layer no affiliate site is operating in.

**Insight 9 — Promptly Safe Mode is the platform play.** A Chrome/Edge extension that wraps ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini with KCSIE-aligned filtering, logging, and prompt recommendations turns GetPromptly from affiliate site into the vendor *other* affiliates sell into. Annual contract value per school is 10-100x higher than affiliate commission.

**Insight 10 — The Compliance Calendar subscription builds a captive, compliance-obsessed audience.** One short email per Tuesday tied to the regulatory year (KCSIE September update, ICO quarterly reviews, GDPR audits, term-by-term safeguarding) will hit 35-50% open rates because it's genuinely useful — and becomes a sales asset for the Validator programme and Promptly Safe Mode commercial product.

**The unifying claim.** GetPromptly is not an affiliate site that happens to serve education. It is *UK education trust infrastructure* that monetises through aligned commerce. Every product decision, every Luna response, every Oliver email, every Donna check should reinforce that framing — and every reinforcement deepens a moat the 2026 leaders cannot attack.

---

# 2. COMPANY CASE STUDIES — Edges Clearly Labelled

This section deconstructs the 2026 leaders, with each company's specific *transferable edge* labelled for application to GetPromptly.

## impact.com — The Partnership Data Graph

**Disclosed scale.** ~$270M ARR (~20% YoY growth), ~$120B partner-referred GMV in 2025, $5B+ GTV, ~350,000 active partnerships, 3,500+ new customers in 2025.

**The funnel.** Enterprise demos and the promise of replacing fragmented affiliate, influencer, referral, and advocate tools with one AI-powered partnership operating system. Maps to: discover → contract → pay → track → engage → protect → optimize.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Cross-device tracking, TrueLink, partner-side impressions, 40+ reports, AI recommendations ("ask impact"), automated workflows, fraud and compliance monitoring, Trackonomics Essentials for publishers, partnership academy with 45K+ learners, Evertune investment for generative-engine optimization.

**Secret sauce.** The shift from "affiliate network" to "partnership data platform." Switching costs come from owning more workflow steps (contracts, payouts, tracking, fraud monitoring, automation, reporting) — not from better attribution. The $100B+ partnership data claim creates an AI-native discovery moat competitors cannot match without years of data accumulation.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Workflow-step ownership creates moat.** Every additional step in the buyer's journey GetPromptly owns (audit receipt generation, procurement brief output, RFP template authoring, post-purchase compliance documentation) is a step competitors must replicate. Ship Plays #1, #2, and #6 to operationalise this principle.

## Awin Group — Conversion Protection as Brand

**Disclosed scale.** 30,000+ brands, 1M+ approved partners, $19B+ annual partnership-generated revenue, 14 countries, 25+ years of operation.

**The funnel.** Audience-segmented from message one — advertisers, creators/influencers, affiliate partners, and agencies see different paths.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Conversion Protection Initiative (CPI) recovering $578M+ globally and shielding 29.4M transactions, Soft Click attribution resilience, Awin Mastertag, upgraded AI platform, LLM discovery engine probabilistically matching brands to 1M partners.

**Secret sauce.** Trusted global liquidity *plus* tracking protection. Awin sells commission protection, AI-assisted partner discovery, local support, and the credibility of a long-running marketplace. CPI specifically positioned as defensive value (recovered commission) gives publishers a tangible reason to choose Awin over alternatives.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Defensive value is a marketable feature.** Just as Awin sells "we recover £X for you," GetPromptly should sell "we generate the audit trail that defends your procurement decision." Play #1 (KCSIE Audit Trail Receipt) is the direct application. The Donna safety gate is the analogous defensive layer for outbound communications.

## CJ — Enterprise Commissioning Depth

**Disclosed scale.** $16B+ annual sales, 1B+ consumers reached, 240+ countries and territories, 150+ publisher payment currencies, claimed 14:1 ROAS.

**The funnel.** Enterprise-led and verticalized, framed around proven growth, global reach, vertical expertise, compliance, and tiered service from self-service to white-glove.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Partnership discovery, situational commissioning (by customer status, promotion type, location, product category, device, lead quality, application approval, LTV), placements marketplace, period-over-period analytics, customer journey touchpoints, incrementality, competitor benchmarking, compliance dashboards, fraud prevention, pre-payment validation, Shopify and Magento plugins, APIs, CreatorIQ-based influencer discovery.

**Secret sauce.** Enterprise-grade partner measurement married to Publicis data and services. CJ can support commission rules conditional on context — a referral from a returning premium customer pays differently from a cold acquisition.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Conditional commissioning unlocks vendor-direct economics.** GetPromptly should negotiate variable commission rates based on buyer type — a Trust-level procurement should pay 15-25% to GetPromptly versus 4-8% on a single-teacher CPD purchase. This is enabled inside the Affiliate Link Registry by adding `commission_tier_rules` as a JSON column.

## Rakuten Advertising — Transparency-as-Product

**Disclosed scale.** 1.2B+ consumers reached, 200M+ transactions completed, 25+ years leading affiliate, claimed #1 in global traffic.

**The funnel.** Positioned as the "leading global network" and "only full-funnel global network" with discovery, forecasting, benchmarking, and AI tools bundled under Affiliate Intelligence.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Affiliate Conversion Journey Transparency API, Rakuten Advertising Detect (24/7 monitoring, dynamic web scraping, brand safety synthesis), Storefronts, Insights and Analytics Prompt for real-time generative-AI reporting, Innovation Labs for AI publisher and product recommendations.

**Secret sauce.** Transparency packaged as differentiation. The Transparency API tells advertisers exactly which touchpoints contributed to conversion — countering the opacity that has historically frustrated affiliate buyers.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Transparency is the fastest path to trust in regulated industries.** Every Luna response, comparison page, and recommendation should expose its reasoning trace ("Why was this recommended? Score breakdown, role match, safety considerations"). This is Moonshot-adjacent — the "Why Was This Recommended?" Layer — and it builds on Flowise's existing memory and prompt architecture.

## Amazon Associates — Onsite Commission Embedding

**Disclosed scale.** Self-described as one of the world's largest affiliate programmes; up to 10% category commissions.

**The funnel.** The simplest in the market: sign up → recommend → earn.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Onsite Commissions — eligible influencer storefront content (photos, videos, Idea Lists, livestreams) appears *inside* Amazon shopping surfaces, with creators earning when shoppers already on Amazon click and buy.

**Secret sauce.** Conversion infrastructure ubiquity. Amazon doesn't compete on commission rate or partner tools; it competes on conversion probability. Affiliates borrow Amazon's trust, logistics, checkout, catalog, customer data, and recommendation engine.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **The commerce layer should embed inside the highest-traffic editorial surfaces.** Don't bolt affiliate links onto a separate page — embed them inside the Prompt Library (Play #7), inside Luna conversations (Play #2), inside living comparison pages (Play #4). This is the Onsite Commissions principle applied to GetPromptly's existing high-traffic surfaces.

## LTK — Velocity in Partnership Formation

**Disclosed scale.** $5-6B annual retail sales, 400,000+ qualified creators, 8,000+ retailers, 40M+ monthly users, 55,000+ creator-brand collaborations annually, ~40% U.S. Gen Z and Millennial women reach.

**The funnel.** Three-sided creator-commerce marketplace — creators publish, consumers discover via app, brands activate via LTK Brand Platform.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Quick Collabs — flat-fee creator collaborations launching in minutes, payments within 48-72 hours after posting, removing negotiation friction.

**Secret sauce.** App-led demand and creator trust. LTK has spent over a decade building consumer habit formation around creators — they have a *consumer shopping surface*, not just tracking links.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Velocity wins in partnership formation.** Oliver's outreach loop should aim for sub-7-day prospect-to-active conversion, mirroring LTK's 48-72 hour payment velocity. The Vendor Onboarding Concierge (Play #9) operationalises this principle.

## ShopMy — Curation as Defensible Network

**Disclosed scale.** $1B+ annual platform sales, 200% YoY revenue growth, 185,000+ tastemakers, 1,200+ premium brand partners, profitability since 2024, $1.5B valuation, 5x average ROI, $0.40 CPC on Opportunities (53% lower than Instagram ads).

**The funnel.** Premium curation rather than algorithmic reach — Circles replaces algorithmic recommendations with personalised feeds from trusted creators.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Creator storefronts, Circles consumer feeds, brand partnerships, Lookbooks for product gifting, Opportunities campaigns.

**Secret sauce.** Network *quality* over network *size*. ShopMy is not trying to be the largest creator database — it's trying to own credible taste-makers who can sell high-consideration products without diluting brand equity.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Curated expertise beats algorithmic discovery for high-consideration purchases — and UK schools are the highest-consideration consumer category in the world.** The Tools Hub (243 tools) and Equipment Hub (96 products) should be curated, not exhaustive. Every entry should have a defensible "why we listed this" rationale visible in the audit trail.

## Later/Mavely — Performance-Based Creator Economics

**Disclosed scale.** $2.4B+ annual GMV run rate (up >$1B since 2024), $250M+ cumulative creator payouts, 180,000+ creators, 100%+ YoY GMV growth, 3,000+ brands and retailers.

**The funnel.** Performance-first and creator-friendly — creators rewarded for conversion rather than follower count.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Influencer workflow plus social media management plus AI-driven intelligence plus predictive analytics plus affiliate infrastructure plus campaign management from discovery through checkout.

**Secret sauce.** Making the "creator middle class" commercially measurable. LTK skews premium lifestyle, ShopMy skews elevated curation, Mavely is optimised for *everyday creators* with loyal audiences and performance-based compensation.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Performance-based partner rewards align incentives.** GetPromptly's inbound affiliate programme (Phase 3 of the Plan of Action) should compensate UK educator-creators on safety-rated conversions, not raw clicks. Higher commission rates for Tier 1 (validated) tools they recommend versus Tier 3 (standard affiliate) tools — this aligns affiliates with the trust framework rather than against it.

## PartnerStack — Lifecycle Automation in B2B

**Disclosed scale.** $500M+ partner-driven revenue in 2024 (9% YoY), 37% increase in partner commissions, 100K+ active partners, $5K+ average earnings per active partner, 600+ companies, 74-day average go-live for new programmes.

**The funnel.** B2B SaaS-specific — the only PRM (Partner Relationship Management) with a network of partners.

**Tech stack disclosed.** Recruitment, onboarding, segmentation, automated partner emails based on activity or inactivity, complex if-then reward conditions, payouts via PayPal/Stripe, reporting, in-app messaging, resource centers.

**Secret sauce.** Category specificity. While most large affiliate networks are retail-first, PartnerStack is built specifically around B2B SaaS recurring revenue, agency partners, resellers, co-selling, and referrals.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Vertical specificity is defensibility.** GetPromptly's UK education focus is its PartnerStack-equivalent vertical moat. Every workflow should be optimised for educator-partner lifecycle (onboarding for SENCO bloggers, monthly re-engagement for dormant educator-creators, role-specific commission tiers) rather than generic affiliate management.

## ClickBank — Funnel Education as Acquisition

**Disclosed scale.** $7.2B+ paid over 27+ years.

**The funnel.** Direct-response native — affiliates generate HopLink, send traffic to a sales page, earn commissions on purchases/upsells/downsells/rebills.

**Tech stack disclosed.** HopLinks, Gravity, EPC, APV, conversion rate, refund rate exposed publicly.

**Secret sauce.** Funnel education *is* affiliate acquisition. ClickBank's strategy guide teaches affiliates Facebook Ads, native ads, YouTube, newsletters, Pinterest, blogging, communities — and that public education attracts affiliates searching for ways to make money, who then become ClickBank affiliates.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Affiliate education content doubles as acquisition.** Publishing UK educator-specific guides on AI affiliate revenue ("How UK SENCOs Can Earn £200-£800/month Recommending AI Tools") would attract the very content creators who become GetPromptly's inbound affiliates (Phase 3). This is the asymmetric long-term creator-commerce loop.

## Admitad — Server-Side as the Post-Cookie Standard

**Disclosed scale.** 100K+ publishers, 3,000+ premium advertisers, 100+ countries.

**The funnel.** Multi-region multi-model — Perform (CPA/CPL/CPS), Creator (Instagram/TikTok/YouTube/Telegram), Rewards (cashback, bank loyalty), Mobile (alternative app stores, OEM), Flow (contracts/payouts), Seller (marketplace and DTC).

**Tech stack disclosed.** Server-to-server (S2S) tracking as cookieless attribution foundation, Instant Payout Lite, official Shopify plugin.

**Secret sauce.** Multi-region and multi-model flexibility — supporting creators, Telegram channels, loyalty partners, bank apps, call centres, OEM mobile inventory, and marketplace sellers in one ecosystem.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Server-side tracking is the post-cookie standard.** GetPromptly should plan for first-party server-side affiliate tracking via the n8n webhook layer — not just UTM parameters. Every audit-receipt URL (Play #1) should pass through the Mac Mini as the first-party tracking node, then redirect to the affiliate destination, capturing attribution server-side and resilient to browser cookie restrictions.

## FlexOffers — Catalog Aggregation

**Disclosed scale.** 12,000+ advertisers across 65 networks, 75,000+ publisher programs, ranked #5 by mThink in CPS.

**The funnel.** "One-roof" aggregation — publishers access many programs centrally; advertisers access broad publisher base.

**Secret sauce.** Breadth and operational convenience.

**Edge for GetPromptly.** **Aggregation is a fallback not a moat.** GetPromptly should not become a generic aggregator — it should remain a curated trust layer. FlexOffers' weakness (less product innovation, lower disclosed financial depth) confirms that breadth alone doesn't win.

---

# 3. CORE GROWTH LEVERS IDENTIFIED

Synthesising the case studies above, the 2026 affiliate market reveals seven core growth levers that compound when stacked. GetPromptly's strategic challenge is to operationalise all seven simultaneously on its existing agentic stack.

**Lever 1 — Workflow-step ownership.** Every additional buyer-journey step the platform owns (recommendation → audit trail → procurement brief → RFP template → onboarding → reporting) is a step competitors must replicate. impact.com, PartnerStack, and CJ all execute this principle. **GetPromptly application:** Plays #1, #2, #6, #9.

**Lever 2 — Defensive value as marketable feature.** Awin's CPI recovers commissions; Rakuten's Detect monitors brand safety; CJ's compliance dashboards prevent fraud. Defensive features are sold as premium value. **GetPromptly application:** The audit-trail receipt (Play #1) and the Donna safety gate are GetPromptly's defensive features — package and market them as such.

**Lever 3 — Conditional commissioning.** CJ's situational commissioning (by customer status, location, product, device, LTV) shows that variable commission rules unlock vendor-direct economics. **GetPromptly application:** Add `commission_tier_rules` to the Affiliate Link Registry; vary rates by buyer type (Trust > school > teacher > parent).

**Lever 4 — Transparency as differentiation.** Rakuten's Transparency API, ShopMy's curated network (vs. algorithmic recommendation), and PartnerStack's review-driven trust all show transparency builds preference. **GetPromptly application:** Every Luna response and comparison page exposes its reasoning trace.

**Lever 5 — Embedded commerce in editorial surfaces.** Amazon's Onsite Commissions; LTK's app-as-shopping-surface; ShopMy's Circles. The commerce layer lives *inside* the highest-traffic content. **GetPromptly application:** Inject one contextually perfect product card per high-traffic prompt (Play #7), not banners or popups.

**Lever 6 — Velocity in partnership formation.** LTK's 48-72 hour payment cycles; PartnerStack's 74-day go-live; ShopMy's 5x ROI. Speed converts. **GetPromptly application:** Vendor Onboarding Concierge (Play #9) compresses partner activation to 14 days.

**Lever 7 — Education-led traffic acquisition.** ClickBank's strategy guide; impact.com's Partnership Economy podcast; CJ's trends reports; Rakuten's Innovation Labs research. Educational content acquires the audience that becomes the platform. **GetPromptly application:** Compliance Calendar subscription (Play #10) and the "How UK Educators Can Earn from AI Affiliate Recommendations" guide series.

The compounding effect: a Trust DPO lands on a living comparison page (Lever 5), reads the transparency reasoning (Lever 4), downloads the audit receipt (Lever 1), receives variable-rate commission flow (Lever 3), is onboarded as a Validator partner (Lever 6), and joins the Compliance Calendar list (Lever 7) — Donna's safety gate (Lever 2) governing every step. Each layer makes the next more efficient. By month 12 of execution, the moat is compound, not additive.
