# 4. PERSONALISED 10x STRATEGY PLAYBOOK FOR GETPROMPTLY

This is the nuclear section — first-principles plus lateral thinking, ready-to-implement on GetPromptly's exact stack. Every play references specific files, agents, workflows, and ports from the Plan of Action and Mac Setup Guide.

## 10x Play #1 — The KCSIE Audit Trail Receipt (Audit-Trail Commerce)

**Lateral analogy.** Stripe's payment receipts, but for compliance. Every transaction generates an artefact the buyer can show stakeholders.

**The insight.** Every affiliate platform in the 2026 research treats the click as the unit. In regulated industries, the unit is the *defence of the decision*. Schools post-KCSIE 2025 face Ofsted scrutiny, ICO investigations, parent FOI requests asking "why did the school buy this AI tool?" No affiliate site provides that defence. GetPromptly should.

**The mechanic.** Every affiliate click on getpromptly.co.uk generates a time-stamped, school-readable PDF receipt the user can download or email. Contains: tool name, trust score at click time, 5-pillar breakdown, role pathway that produced the recommendation, KCSIE/UK GDPR alignment notes, affiliate disclosure language, Donna safety review summary, and a unique receipt ID. Generated by an n8n workflow triggered by the affiliate-click webhook, written in Markdown by the Content Agent, rendered to PDF (Pandoc on Mac Mini), stored in Qdrant for retrieval, emailed via Brevo.

**Why nuclear.** A SENCO who buys a £400 reading pen via GetPromptly's affiliate link can show the audit trail to her Head, her DPO, and her parent forum. The receipt becomes a procurement artefact. Schools and Trusts will *prefer* GetPromptly affiliate links over direct supplier links because the receipt provides cover. Conversion rates on tracked links should rise 30-60% on institutional purchases.

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
n8n workflow: "Affiliate Click → Audit Receipt"
[Webhook: /click/{tool_id}/{user_session}]
  ↓
[Google Sheets: Read Affiliate Link Registry] — pull tool metadata
  ↓
[Google Sheets: Read Tool Directory] — pull current 5-pillar scores
  ↓
[Code Node] — assemble receipt data structure
  ↓
[Claude API: Content Agent] — generate Markdown receipt
  ↓
[Donna gate] — verify disclosure language present, no
  unsupported claims, KCSIE language calibrated
  ↓
[HTTP Request: Pandoc service on Mac Mini] — render to PDF
  ↓
[Brevo API] — email PDF + tracked redirect URL
  ↓
[Qdrant: index receipt for future retrieval]
  ↓
[HTTP 302 Redirect] — to actual affiliate tracked_url
```

**Time to ship.** 6-9 hours of build time. No new infrastructure required.

## 10x Play #2 — Luna as a Procurement Report Generator

**Lateral analogy.** Bloomberg Terminal's bespoke screener output, but for SENCOs and Trust DPOs.

**The insight.** Rakuten's Insights Prompt generates AI reports for advertisers; ShopMy generates personalised feeds for consumers; LTK surfaces creator content for shoppers. None generate institutional procurement briefs for regulated buyers. Luna can.

**The mechanic.** A SENCO arrives on chat.getpromptly.co.uk and types "I need AAC tools for a Year 4 child with ASD." Luna asks 6-8 calibrated questions (key stage, communication profile, budget, existing tech, EHCP status, school policy on AI). Luna then generates a 2-3 page PDF brief, branded GetPromptly, containing: 3 recommended tools with trust scores, 1 specialist supplier route, 1 free training resource, 1 paid CPD option, a SENCO-specific procurement checklist, affiliate disclosures, and a "talk to a human" escalation route. The PDF is emailed (capturing email = lead) and stored in Qdrant for later retrieval.

**Why nuclear.** No competitor generates regulated-buyer procurement briefs on demand. A SENCO's procurement decision typically involves 2-4 hours of research; Luna compresses it to 4 minutes. The brief itself is a high-conversion asset (the user has invested attention and shared role context, so commission rates on click-throughs from the brief are 3-5x higher than cold directory browsing) AND a powerful lead magnet (email captured under legitimate interest, Newsletter Agent can sequence them, Oliver can flag them as a consultation prospect).

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
Flowise Luna Agentflow extension:
[Chat Input]
  ↓
[Buffer Memory: 20 messages]
  ↓
[Luna Agent: Claude 3 Haiku → Sonnet for brief generation]
  ↓
[Tool: needs_assessment_qualifier]
  — qualified after 4-6 questions: role + need + budget + context
  ↓
[Tool: generate_procurement_brief]
  — POST to n8n webhook with structured needs payload
  ↓
[n8n: "Brief Generator" sub-workflow]
  - Read Tool Directory, Equipment Registry, Training Registry
    filtered by role + need + budget
  - Claude Sonnet: assemble 2-3 page Markdown brief
  - Donna gate: verify safety language, disclosures, no
    unsupported clinical claims for SEND
  - Pandoc → PDF on Mac Mini
  - Brevo: email to user (lead capture)
  - Qdrant: index for retrieval
  - Google Sheets: log to Lead Registry with source =
    "Luna Procurement Brief"
  ↓
[Tool: schedule_follow_up]
  — Newsletter Agent triggers 5-email role-specific sequence
```

**Time to ship.** 14-20 hours including Luna prompt engineering, brief template, and Donna's SEND-specific compliance rules.

## 10x Play #3 — Oliver's "Trust Validator" Pivot (The Inverse-Affiliate Move)

**Lateral analogy.** BSI Kitemark or Trustpilot Pro — vendors pay for credibility, not for clicks.

**The insight.** Every affiliate site approaches vendors as a *publisher seeking commission*. This puts GetPromptly in a low-leverage position where Amazon Associates UK pays 4%, AWIN pays 5-12%, direct EdTech vendors pay 10-25%. The 2026 Perplexity report shows that the highest-margin partnerships in B2B SaaS (PartnerStack at $5K average partner earnings) come from *integration partnerships* not pure affiliate. There's an inverse-affiliate move available: instead of asking vendors to pay GetPromptly for referrals, Oliver pitches GetPromptly as a *paid trust validation service*.

**The mechanic.** Oliver's outreach pivots based on prospect tier.

- **Tier 1 (large EdTech: Twinkl, Century, Eedi, MagicSchool, Curipod, National College).** Pitch: "GetPromptly's UK schools audience requires a documented safety review before procurement; we offer paid audit + listing + featured-comparison starting at £1,500/year, with optional upgraded affiliate revenue share."
- **Tier 2 (mid-market EdTech).** Pitch: "Standard affiliate listing free, premium safety-validated tier £500/year."
- **Tier 3 (Amazon, AWIN catalog).** Pitch: "Standard affiliate, UTM-tracked." Vendor pays for *credibility*, not for clicks.

**Why nuclear.** This inverts the affiliate value chain. Instead of GetPromptly chasing 4-8% commission on £30 prompt packs, GetPromptly receives £1,500-£5,000 upfront from each Tier 1 vendor for inclusion in the validated tier — without compromising independence (because the *score* is independent; only the audit access is paid). At 20 Tier 1 vendors paying £2,500/year, that's £50K of high-margin recurring revenue with zero affiliate-conversion risk. The model is closer to Trustpilot or BSI Kitemark than to Amazon Associates.

**Implementation blueprint.** Oliver's existing prospect-discovery workflow remains; the email outreach template is forked into three variants. Donna gates each variant for accurate-claims compliance (e.g., "validated" must reference the actual 5-pillar review, not imply official certification). A new Google Sheet — "Validator Programme Registry" — tracks paid Validator partners and renewal dates. Analytics Agent generates a quarterly report.

```
Oliver Outreach Template — Tier 1 "Trust Validator" Pitch (Donna-approved skeleton)

Subject: Independent UK schools safety review for {{vendor_name}}

Hi {{contact_name}},

GetPromptly is the UK's trusted platform for AI in education —
243 tools reviewed against KCSIE 2025, UK GDPR and DfE guidance,
serving teachers, SENCOs, school leaders, parents and students.

I noticed {{specific_fact_1}}. Schools we work with increasingly
ask three questions before procurement: (1) data privacy posture,
(2) safeguarding alignment, (3) age suitability evidence.

We run a structured 5-pillar review process. Vendors who pass are
listed in our validated tier with a documented review summary
that schools can attach to their procurement file. This is not
sponsored ranking — independence is the value.

Worth a 20-minute call to walk you through the methodology?

Affiliate disclosure: yes, we earn commission on some equipment
and training links. The validator review is the paid, independent
audit; the directory ranking is not for sale.

— Oliver, GetPromptly Partnerships
   partnerships@getpromptly.co.uk
   {{unsubscribe_link}}
```

**Time to ship.** 8-12 hours, primarily template writing and Donna rule-tuning.

## 10x Play #4 — Living Comparison Pages

**Lateral analogy.** Bloomberg/Reuters live data terminals, applied to affiliate comparison content.

**The insight.** Every affiliate comparison page on the internet is static: "Top 10 AI tools for teachers in 2026." It rots within 60 days because pricing changes, features ship, terms update. The 2026 leaders (Awin, CJ, impact.com) have data infrastructure to keep partner data fresh, but they don't expose that freshness to end users. GetPromptly can ship **living comparison pages** — pages that auto-update from the Tool Directory and Equipment Registry every 24 hours, with a visible "last verified" timestamp and a 30-day changelog footer.

**The mechanic.** Each comparison page (e.g., `/compare/ai-tools-for-senco`, `/compare/best-ai-cpd-uk-teachers`, `/compare/safe-ai-revision-tools-students`) is generated by an n8n workflow that runs nightly: pulls from Tool Directory Google Sheet, applies role-specific filters and 5-pillar safety scoring, asks the Content Agent to generate updated copy, runs Donna's compliance gate, injects affiliate links via the Affiliate Link Injection sub-workflow, generates audit-trail meta-tags, pushes to live site. A "last verified: 2026-05-04 03:14 UTC" footer plus a 30-day changelog showing additions, removals, score changes provides transparency competitors cannot match.

**Why nuclear.** Schools' procurement teams trust pages that *show* their freshness. Living pages convert 2-4x better than static comparisons because they feel like reference data, not marketing. Combined with the audit receipt (Play #1), a SENCO can screenshot the comparison, generate her receipt, and have a procurement-defensible artefact. SEO benefits compound (Google rewards freshness) and Luna can cite the most recent version inside chat.

**Wireframe sketch (descriptive).** Top of page: H1 "Best AI Tools for SENCOs — UK Schools 2026" with a green "✓ Last verified 4 hours ago" badge inline. Below: a 5-column comparison table (Tool / Trust Score / Best for / Price band / Action), with each tool row expanding to show 5-pillar breakdown on click. Right sidebar: "Why these picks?" reasoning panel with collapse expander. Below comparison table: 30-day changelog with timestamp, item, change. Bottom: affiliate disclosure block + "Generate procurement receipt" CTA (triggers Play #1 workflow). Footer: "Updated nightly · Reviewed by Donna safety agent · Independent ranking · Some links earn us commission."

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
n8n workflow: "Living Comparison Pages — Nightly Refresh"
[Schedule Trigger: 03:00 daily]
  ↓
[Google Sheets: Read Tool Directory + Training Registry +
  Equipment Registry + Affiliate Link Registry]
  ↓
[Loop over comparison_page_configs.json]
  — each config: page_slug, role_filter, category_filter,
    score_thresholds, page_title, intro_template
  ↓
[Claude API: Content Agent]  — generate page body for each comparison
  - intro paragraph (60 words)
  - 5-pillar comparison table
  - 3-paragraph commentary on differences
  - role-specific safety notes
  ↓
[Affiliate Link Injection sub-workflow]
  ↓
[Donna gate]
  ↓
[Generate changelog: diff vs previous version]
  ↓
[GitHub commit to website repo OR direct CMS API push]
  ↓
[Uptime Kuma: log success, notify if failure]
```

**Time to ship.** 12-16 hours plus per-page template tuning. After build, marginal cost of additional comparison pages is ~zero.

## 10x Play #5 — The Promptly Score Card Embed (Distributed Trust)

**Lateral analogy.** Trustpilot widgets, BSI Kitemark seals, and SSL padlock indicators — trust travels.

**The insight.** Awin's strongest moat is brand-name recognition; impact.com's is data graph; LTK's is app. GetPromptly's moat can be **distributed** — let the trust score travel. ShopMy/Mavely creators don't sit on one platform; they post everywhere. GetPromptly's safety score should do the same.

**The mechanic.** Every tool in the Tool Directory gets a *Promptly Score Card* — a small embeddable badge (HTML/JS snippet, similar to a Trustpilot widget) that vendors can place on their own websites. Badge displays: GetPromptly logo, current trust score (e.g., "8.6 / 10 — KCSIE-aware review"), 5-pillar mini-bars, "last reviewed" date, clickthrough to full review on getpromptly.co.uk. Vendors *want* the badge for marketing credibility; every embed becomes a backlink, brand impression, and referral funnel.

**Why nuclear.** Distributed trust badges are a proven model (Trustpilot, BSI, ISO seals, Padlock SSL) but no AI/EdTech affiliate has built one. Creates a viral feedback loop: vendors with high scores brag publicly → more vendors want to be reviewed → more reviews mean more authority → more authority means more schools trust the directory → more schools click affiliate links. Network effect compounds. Each badge embed is tracked, generating click-through traffic that the Analytics Agent attributes to the badge source.

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
Static badge endpoint hosted on Mac Mini, served via Cloudflare:
GET https://badges.getpromptly.co.uk/embed/{tool_id}.js
  → returns minified JS that injects:
    <a href="https://getpromptly.co.uk/tools/{slug}?ref=badge">
      <img src="https://badges.getpromptly.co.uk/{tool_id}.svg"
           alt="GetPromptly Trust Score: 8.6/10" />
    </a>

n8n workflow: "Badge Refresh"
[Schedule: hourly]
  → regenerate SVG for each tool from Tool Directory
  → push to Cloudflare R2 or local nginx serving via tunnel

n8n workflow: "Badge Click Attribution"
[Webhook: /tools/{slug}?ref=badge]
  → log to Google Sheets: badge_referral_log
  → Analytics Agent includes in weekly report
```

Add subdomain `badges.getpromptly.co.uk` to Cloudflare Tunnel config (per Mac Setup Guide Section 5.4).

**Time to ship.** 10-14 hours including SVG template design.

## 10x Play #6 — The Reverse-RFP Engine

**Lateral analogy.** Free legal-document generators (Rocket Lawyer, LegalZoom) capture the highest-LTV B2B clients before they know what they need.

**The insight.** Multi-Academy Trusts and Local Authorities run formal procurement processes. They issue RFPs (Requests for Proposal) to vendors. Currently nobody helps Trusts *write* the AI procurement RFP — they cobble it together from internal templates and DfE guidance. If Oliver helps Trusts write the RFP, Oliver shapes the criteria *and* receives the resulting recommendations as warm leads for Validator-tier vendors.

**The mechanic.** New lead magnet on `/for-schools` titled "Free AI Procurement RFP Template — KCSIE 2025 Aligned" captures Trust DPOs, business managers, IT leads. User enters context (Trust size, key stages, existing tech, budget range), and an n8n workflow generates customised RFP via Content Agent — including required vendor responses on the 5 pillars. Trust uses RFP, vendors respond, Trust returns to GetPromptly for next step. Critically, the RFP template *recommends* specific tools from the Tool Directory as known-good baselines (with affiliate links flagged where applicable). Defensible because recommendations are scored independently — Trust gets a head-start, GetPromptly gets the lead, vendor gets the warm conversation.

**Why nuclear.** Trusts that download the RFP template are 100x higher-LTV than retail teachers — a single MAT decision can drive £20K-£200K in procurement. No affiliate site is operating in this layer because it requires actual UK education-procurement domain knowledge. GetPromptly's existing positioning makes it the obvious player. Oliver follows up automatically with consultation offers; Newsletter Agent runs an 8-email Trust-specific sequence on procurement best practice.

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
Pages: /for-schools/rfp-template (lead-magnet form)

n8n workflow: "RFP Generator"
[Webhook Trigger: form submission]
  ↓
[Validate: trust_name, contact, role, trust_size, key_stages,
   budget_range, existing_tech, focus_areas]
  ↓
[Donna gate: verify legitimate-interest basis, consent box ticked]
  ↓
[Google Sheets: append to Trust Lead Registry]
  ↓
[Claude Sonnet: Content Agent]
  - Generate 8-12 page RFP document with:
    * Trust context summary
    * Required vendor responses on 5 pillars
    * Required SLAs and data handling
    * Recommended baseline tools (from Tool Directory)
    * Scoring rubric for vendor responses
    * Submission and timeline templates
  ↓
[Donna gate: verify legal language is generic-template-not-advice]
  ↓
[Pandoc → branded PDF on Mac Mini]
  ↓
[Brevo: email PDF + welcome message + Oliver consultation offer]
  ↓
[Newsletter Agent: enrol in 8-email Trust Procurement sequence]
  ↓
[Oliver: flag in Prospect Registry as "Trust — high value"
  for personal follow-up within 72 hours]
```

**Time to ship.** 18-24 hours including RFP template authoring (becomes a permanent asset).

## 10x Play #7 — Prompt Library as Conversion Engine

**Lateral analogy.** Duolingo's habit loop fused with Amazon's "frequently bought together" — every prompt completion becomes an organic upgrade moment.

**The insight.** GetPromptly's 440+ prompt library is currently positioned as a free resource. ClickBank's strategy guide reveals that the highest-converting affiliate funnels don't sell products — they sell *transformations* via story-based content. Every prompt in the library is a transformation moment ("turn this 2-hour task into 8 minutes"). Each prompt should embed a *contextual* affiliate offer related to the transformation — not a banner ad, but a natural extension.

**The mechanic.** Take the top 50 highest-traffic prompts. For each, the Content Agent identifies the *next purchase* that would naturally follow that transformation. Example: a "Year 6 SATs revision question generator" prompt drives traffic from parents and students. The next purchase is a CPD course on SATs preparation, a revision book, or a flashcard app. The Affiliate Link Injection sub-workflow embeds *one* contextually perfect product card at the bottom of the prompt page (not three, not five — one) with a "next step in this workflow" framing. Conversion rates on contextual single-product cards are 5-10x higher than generic banner ads (the ShopMy curation insight applied to prompts).

**Why nuclear.** Every prompt page becomes a high-conversion affiliate surface without compromising the free-resource positioning. User gets something genuinely valuable (working prompt) plus relevant follow-up offer. Analytics Agent A/B-tests product matches per prompt over time, optimising commission per prompt-view. At 50K monthly prompt-views and a 1.5% click-to-purchase conversion at £15 average commission, this is **£11,250/month of incremental revenue from existing traffic**.

**Implementation blueprint.**

```
n8n workflow: "Prompt → Product Match Engine"
[Schedule Trigger: weekly Monday 04:00]
  ↓
[Google Sheets: Read Prompt Library + traffic data]
  ↓
[Filter: top 50 prompts by 30-day pageviews]
  ↓
[Loop]
  ↓ for each prompt:
  [Claude Sonnet: Research Agent]
    - "Given this prompt and its outcome, what is the single most
       useful product, course or tool a user might buy next?
       Match against Affiliate Link Registry only."
  ↓
  [Donna gate: relevance + safety check]
  ↓
  [Update Prompt Library Google Sheet: add columns
    matched_product_id, match_rationale, last_match_review]
  ↓
  [Update prompt page on website CMS via API]
    - inject single product card at end of prompt page
    - format: "Next step in this workflow: {{product_card}}"
    - include affiliate disclosure inline

[Analytics Agent]
  - Weekly report: click-through and conversion per prompt
  - Flag underperforming matches for re-review
```

**Time to ship.** 14-20 hours including initial Research Agent prompt engineering and conversion-tracking infrastructure.

## 10x Play #8 — The "School-Safe AI Wrapper" (Promptly Safe Mode)

**Lateral analogy.** Grammarly's browser extension model — sit *inside* the user's existing workflow, capture value at every keystroke.

**The insight.** This is the highest-leverage play of all. The 2026 Perplexity research reveals that LTK, ShopMy, and Mavely have built consumer commerce ecosystems on top of existing platforms. None have built an analogous wrapper for *institutional* AI procurement. GetPromptly can ship a thin software product — branded "Promptly Safe Mode" — that wraps existing AI tools (ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Perplexity) with GetPromptly's compliance layer: filtering pupil-data prompts, logging usage for safeguarding, applying KCSIE-aligned content rules, surfacing GetPromptly's recommended prompts. Schools pay GetPromptly directly for the wrapper; users still consume the underlying AI tool through their existing subscription. This is *not* an affiliate programme — it's the inversion: GetPromptly becomes the vendor that other affiliates sell.

**The mechanic.** Build a Chrome/Edge extension and a web dashboard. Schools pay £150-£400/year per site. The extension intercepts pupil-side AI prompts, applies a Donna-style filter (no personal data, KCSIE-aligned content boundaries, age-suitable outputs), logs interactions for safeguarding teams, and surfaces GetPromptly's prompt library and tool recommendations contextually. Affiliate revenue lives inside the wrapper: when a teacher clicks "I need a CPD course on this topic," the wrapper recommends a course from the Training Registry with a tracked link.

**Why nuclear.** This is the Amazon Associates Onsite Commissions move applied to UK education. GetPromptly stops being an affiliate site and becomes the platform other affiliates sell into. Combined with Plays #1-#7, this creates a vertically integrated stack: free directory (top of funnel) → procurement briefs (middle) → audit-trail receipts (decision point) → Promptly Safe Mode (deployment) → continuing affiliate revenue inside the wrapper (recurring). Annual contract value per school is 10-100x higher than affiliate commission. The Mac Mini M4 stack already runs Flowise, Qdrant, and the Donna compliance layer — the wrapper is largely a packaging exercise on existing components.

**Implementation note.** This is a 90-180 day build, not a 30-day ship. The MVP is a Chrome extension that adds a sidebar to chat.openai.com and claude.ai with: (1) prompt-vetting via the existing Flowise Agents instance on port 3000, (2) a recommended-prompts panel pulling from the Prompt Library, (3) a tool-recommendation footer pulling from the Tool Directory. Authentication via the school's Google Workspace or Microsoft 365 SSO. Logging to Qdrant for school safeguarding teams' review. Pricing: £200/year per site for first 50 schools (early-adopter discount), £400/year list price thereafter. At 100 schools by month 12, that's **£20K-£40K of high-margin recurring revenue** from a product that compounds with every Tool Directory and Prompt Library update GetPromptly already ships.

## 10x Play #9 — Vendor Onboarding Concierge

**Lateral analogy.** Apple's white-glove enterprise concierge service — boutique-scale touch creates outsized loyalty.

**The insight.** When Oliver successfully recruits a new vendor partnership, the current Plan of Action workflow ends at confirmation. The 2026 leaders (PartnerStack, impact.com) all have automated partner-onboarding sequences but none provide *high-touch concierge* onboarding because they operate at scale. GetPromptly operates at boutique scale — Oliver can deliver white-glove onboarding that no horizontal platform can match.

**The mechanic.** Every confirmed Tier 1 or Tier 2 vendor receives a 14-day Oliver-led onboarding: day 0 welcome and asset request; day 2 Donna-led compliance review of vendor's marketing claims; day 4 Content Agent generates 3 sample comparison-page snippets and shares for vendor review; day 7 Luna training data updated to include the new vendor; day 10 Newsletter Agent ships vendor-specific announcement to Educator Leads list; day 14 first commission report generated by Analytics Agent.

**Why nuclear.** Vendors who experience the onboarding tell other vendors. Oliver's recruitment efficiency compounds because warm referrals from existing partners convert at 10-20x the rate of cold outreach. The Plan of Action's existing recruitment workflow becomes a recruitment *engine* rather than a one-shot tool.

**Implementation blueprint.** Add a new n8n workflow "Vendor Concierge — 14 Day" triggered by manual flag in Affiliate Link Registry (status flips to "active — concierge"). Workflow contains 7 scheduled Wait nodes (days 0, 2, 4, 7, 10, 14) each triggering a specific agent action.

**Time to ship.** 8-12 hours.

## 10x Play #10 — The Compliance Calendar Subscription

**Lateral analogy.** Morning Brew's daily newsletter, but for the regulatory calendar of UK schools — captive, compliance-obsessed audience.

**The insight.** The 2026 leaders sell research reports (impact.com, Rakuten); ClickBank publishes strategy guides; CJ ships trends reports. None publish a *time-locked operational calendar* tied to the regulatory year. UK schools operate on a precise compliance cadence: KCSIE annual update in September, ICO data-protection-by-design reviews quarterly, Ofsted readiness rolling, GDPR data audit annually, term-by-term safeguarding reviews. GetPromptly should own this calendar.

**The mechanic.** Free email subscription, "GetPromptly Compliance Calendar — UK Schools," ships one short email per week (Tuesday morning) containing: this week's compliance focus area, the relevant DfE/KCSIE/ICO update, a recommended tool/prompt/training from the Registries that addresses it, one practical 5-minute action. Newsletter Agent generates content from a year-long Editorial Calendar Sheet. Affiliate links embedded *contextually* (a "data audit week" email recommends a specific UK GDPR audit tool from Tool Directory; a "SENCO review week" email recommends an EHCP prompt pack from Prompt Library).

**Why nuclear.** Builds a captive, compliance-obsessed audience exactly aligned with GetPromptly's commercial model. Open rates will be exceptional (compliance content in regulated industries hits 35-50% open vs 19% Brevo benchmarks) because the content is genuinely useful. The list compounds as a sales asset for Oliver's vendor pitches ("we ship to 5,000+ UK school decision-makers weekly") and for the Promptly Safe Mode commercial product (Play #8).

**Implementation blueprint.** Build the Editorial Calendar Sheet (52 weeks × topic × recommended product). Newsletter Agent generates and Donna-gates each weekly email. Brevo schedules.

**Time to ship.** 6-10 hours plus 2-3 days of editorial calendar authoring.

---

# 5. IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP — Phased with Quick Wins + Long-Term Moats

## Phase 1 — Days 1-30 (Foundation & Audit-Trail Layer)

**Week 1.** Ship Play #1 (KCSIE Audit Trail Receipt) — 6-9 hours. Ship Play #5's badge endpoint (basic version) — 10-14 hours. Verify Section 4 plays against existing Mac Mini RAM budget; confirm dual Flowise + Qdrant + n8n stays under 9 GB ceiling. **Quick win.**

**Week 2.** Ship Play #4 (Living Comparison Pages) initial 3 pages — 12-16 hours. Build SEND comparison, SENCO AI tools comparison, AI CPD comparison. Run for 7 days, verify Donna gate fires on every refresh, verify changelogs are accurate. **Quick win + SEO moat starts.**

**Week 3.** Ship Play #7 (Prompt → Product Match Engine) for top 20 prompts only — 14-20 hours. Measure baseline conversion. Iterate match logic. **Quick win — immediate revenue.**

**Week 4.** Ship Play #10 (Compliance Calendar subscription) — 6-10 hours plus 2-3 days editorial calendar authoring. Launch with soft list of 100 existing GetPromptly subscribers. **Long-term moat — list-building begins.**

## Phase 2 — Days 31-60 (Conversion & Concierge Layer)

**Week 5.** Ship Play #2 (Luna Procurement Brief Generator) — 14-20 hours. Soft-launch on chat.getpromptly.co.uk with a "Beta — early access" framing. Capture qualitative feedback. **Conversion moat.**

**Week 6.** Ship Play #3 (Trust Validator Pitch — Oliver's tier-segmented outreach) — 8-12 hours. Begin reaching Tier 1 vendors with the Validator pitch. Set commercial target of 3 paid Tier 1 partnerships in 90 days. **Revenue moat — non-affiliate income.**

**Week 7.** Ship Play #9 (Vendor Onboarding Concierge) — 8-12 hours. Use first paid Validator partner as concierge pilot. **Compounding moat.**

**Week 8.** Ship Play #6 (Reverse-RFP Engine) — 18-24 hours including RFP template authoring. Lead-magnet form goes live on /for-schools. Newsletter Agent's 8-email Trust Procurement sequence ships. **Highest-LTV moat.**

## Phase 3 — Days 61-90 (Scale, Polish & Decision Gate)

**Week 9.** Performance review across all shipped plays. Analytics Agent compiles a 90-day report against KPIs (audit-receipt downloads, comparison-page conversion, brief generation requests, Validator pipeline value, RFP downloads). Donna conducts compliance audit across all new outputs.

**Week 10.** Iteration sprint based on Week 9 findings. Fix bottlenecks. Refine Luna prompts. Tune Oliver's outreach templates.

**Week 11.** Begin scoping Play #8 (Promptly Safe Mode wrapper). Detailed product specification, technical feasibility study using existing Flowise + Qdrant infrastructure, school-customer development conversations with 5 prospects. **Decision gate: build/don't-build based on customer signals.**

**Week 12.** Public launch of all visible new features (badges, audit receipts, living comparisons, RFP template, compliance calendar). Coordinated email + social campaign. Newsletter Agent ships "What's New" email to all lists. Begin authoring Q2 strategic plan informed by 90-day learnings.

## Phase 4 — Days 91-180 (Platform Layer)

**Months 4-6.** If Play #8 build/don't-build decision is "build", begin Promptly Safe Mode MVP. 90-day build sprint. Customer development with 10-20 schools throughout build. Soft-launch to 50 early-adopter schools at £200/year. Validator programme scales to 8-12 active Tier 1 partners.

## Phase 5 — Days 181-365 (Moonshot Evaluation)

**Months 7-12.** Quarterly moonshot evaluation (Section 7). Decision gates on each: Trust Federation (need 1 founding MAT partner), Vendor Safety Bond (need legal/insurance partner), Federated Luna for Trusts (need 2-3 Trust pilots), KCSIE-as-Code (need DfE conversation). Promptly Safe Mode scales to 100+ schools. Compliance Calendar list reaches 5,000+ subscribers. Inbound affiliate programme reaches 250+ educator-creators.

## Resourcing & Cost

All plays achievable on existing Mac Mini M4 + MacBook Air M5 + Brevo + Anthropic API + GitHub stack with no additional infrastructure spend. API costs projected to rise from ~£40-100/month to ~£90-180/month. Single biggest risk: Mac Mini RAM ceiling — if Ollama is required for any local-data workflow simultaneously with both Flowise instances under load, the 16 GB ceiling will hit; recommendation is to upgrade to a 24 GB Mac Mini in Q2 2026 if Plays #1-#10 land successfully (£600 incremental investment unlocks Promptly Safe Mode without re-architecture).

## Compliance Gates Throughout

Donna must approve every customer-facing output before publication or send. KCSIE/UK GDPR/DfE language must remain calibrated — no "official approval" or "certification" claims beyond what the actual review process supports. Every affiliate placement must remain disclosable and independent of the score; the Validator programme must explicitly separate paid audit access from independent ranking. Audit-receipt PDFs must include retention-policy language consistent with the Plan of Action's GDPR framework (12-month retention then anonymisation). Every new workflow ships to GitHub for version control per Mac Setup Guide Section 5.3.

---

# 6. RISK RADAR + MITIGATION

## Risk 1 — KCSIE / DfE language calibration drift

**The risk.** The 5-pillar trust framework references KCSIE 2025, UK GDPR, and DfE guidance. If GetPromptly's language ever drifts toward implying "official approval" or "DfE-certified" status (which doesn't exist), regulatory or reputational damage could be severe.

**Mitigation.** Donna's compliance gate must include a calibrated-language check on every external output. Every claim of "KCSIE-aware" must reference the actual review methodology, not imply official endorsement. Quarterly external review by a UK education legal professional. Donna's checklist must encode this explicitly.

## Risk 2 — Mac Mini RAM ceiling under expanded load

**The risk.** Per the Mac Setup Guide Section 2.2, the 16 GB M4 is "tight but manageable." Plays #1-#10 add audit-receipt PDF generation, brief generation, living-page refreshes, and badge SVG regeneration to the existing dual-Flowise + n8n + Qdrant + Uptime Kuma stack. If Ollama is loaded simultaneously, swap exceeds 1 GB and the system slows.

**Mitigation.** Strict adherence to "Ollama on-demand only" rule from Mac Setup Guide. Add memory-pressure monitoring to Uptime Kuma. Plan budget for 24 GB Mac Mini upgrade in Q2 2026 (~£600) if Plays #1-#10 prove revenue-positive. Document RAM headroom in weekly Analytics Agent report.

## Risk 3 — Email deliverability collapse

**The risk.** Oliver's outreach scaling to 50 emails/day plus the Compliance Calendar list growing to 5,000+ plus Newsletter Agent sequences plus Trust Procurement sequence plus Educator Leads sequences could push partnerships@getpromptly.co.uk past Brevo's deliverability thresholds. A single bad batch could trigger spam complaints that pause sending entirely.

**Mitigation.** Per Mac Setup Guide Section 7.2 — SPF/DKIM/DMARC must be live before scaling, custom tracking domain `track.getpromptly.co.uk` configured, sending rate-limited (max 50/day cold outreach, gap of 2+ hours between batches), bounce rate >5% triggers auto-pause, spam complaint >0.1% triggers immediate Donna audit and pause. Use separate sending identity for outreach versus newsletter (consider `news@getpromptly.co.uk` for newsletter to protect partnerships domain reputation).

## Risk 4 — Validator programme perceived as "pay for rankings"

**The risk.** Play #3 introduces paid vendor relationships. If users perceive the Validator badge as influencing the trust score itself (rather than only paid audit access), independence is destroyed and the entire moat collapses.

**Mitigation.** Methodology page transparently explains: (a) trust scoring is independent and unaffected by Validator status; (b) Validator partners pay for *audit access* and *premium listing*, not for score; (c) any vendor can apply for Validator review regardless of score; (d) Validator partners that fail subsequent reviews are removed. Donna gates Validator-tier copy explicitly. Quarterly third-party audit confirms scoring remains independent. Display Validator status as a separate badge ("Validator: paid review programme") distinct from trust score itself.

## Risk 5 — Affiliate-programme termination by major partners

**The risk.** Amazon Associates UK has historically reduced commission rates without notice (multiple times since 2018). If Amazon, AWIN, or Rakuten changes terms abruptly, GetPromptly's affiliate revenue could drop 30-60% in a single month.

**Mitigation.** Diversify affiliate partner mix — no single programme should exceed 30% of affiliate revenue. Aggressively pursue direct EdTech vendor relationships (15-25% commission) to reduce Amazon dependence. Validator programme (Play #3) generates non-affiliate revenue resilient to commission changes. Promptly Safe Mode (Play #8) generates SaaS revenue completely decoupled from affiliate.

## Risk 6 — Luna or Oliver hallucination causing reputational damage

**The risk.** Luna recommends a tool that turns out to have a serious safety incident. Oliver sends an outreach email containing a misrepresentation. Either could trigger reputational damage in a small UK education community where trust travels fast.

**Mitigation.** Donna's compliance gate is non-optional on every external output. Luna explicitly prompted to escalate sensitive cases to humans. Oliver's outreach uses fact-research only from verified sources (Research Agent must cite sources). Quarterly retrospective review of Luna conversation logs (Qdrant) for hallucination patterns. Public bug-bounty programme inviting users to report issues.

## Risk 7 — Competitive entry by impact.com or Awin

**The risk.** A 2026 leader observes GetPromptly's growth and decides to launch a "schools edition" of their platform. With venture capital and engineering resources, they could replicate visible features in 12-18 months.

**Mitigation.** The competitor cannot replicate UK education domain expertise (KCSIE, DfE, ICO context) without acquiring or hiring. The Validator programme creates direct paid relationships with vendors that horizontal players cannot match without local presence. The Compliance Calendar list creates direct subscriber relationships horizontal players cannot match. The Promptly Safe Mode wrapper creates SaaS lock-in that horizontal players cannot match without rebuilding for the regulatory year. Speed of execution is the response — every week Plays #1-#10 are operational, the moat deepens. **The competitor's most rational move is acquisition, not competition** — which is itself a strategic option for GetPromptly's exit value.

## Risk 8 — Founder/operator bandwidth constraints

**The risk.** The Plan of Action and this report describe a substantial workload across 90 days. If founder bandwidth becomes the bottleneck, the agentic stack idles.

**Mitigation.** The agentic architecture is *designed* to compress operator time — Oliver, Luna, Donna, and Analytics Agent each replace 10-30 hours/week of manual work. Strict Phase 1 prioritisation (Plays #1, #4, #7, #10 first) ensures revenue-positive plays ship before complexity-positive ones. Weekly Friday review cadence with Analytics Agent's report drives prioritisation discipline. Consider hiring a part-time UK education research contractor (£15-25/hour, 5-10 hours/week) to handle Tool Directory and Equipment Registry curation — the highest-leverage non-agent task.

## Risk 9 — UK regulatory shift (KCSIE 2026, Online Safety Act, AI Act)

**The risk.** UK regulation around AI in schools is actively evolving. KCSIE 2026 may introduce new safeguards. The UK AI regulation framework may impose new disclosure requirements. Either could invalidate GetPromptly's current 5-pillar framework.

**Mitigation.** **Reframe as opportunity not threat.** GetPromptly's positioning as the trust authority *requires* it to adapt fastest. The Compliance Calendar (Play #10) is built for exactly this. The Tool Directory's review methodology should be versioned (v1.0 = 2026, v2.0 = post-KCSIE-2026) with timestamped scoring history visible to users. Quarterly review of UK AI/education regulatory landscape with mitigation roadmap.

## Risk 10 — Accidental scope creep into clinical/therapeutic claims

**The risk.** SEND tools and prompts (the Equipment Hub's SEND-flagged products, EHCP-related prompts) operate in a domain where clinical claims have strict regulatory requirements. A SENCO procurement brief that recommends a tool for "supporting children with autism" may stray into clinical territory.

**Mitigation.** Donna's SEND-specific compliance ruleset must explicitly block clinical claims, therapeutic outcome promises, or diagnostic implications. Every SEND brief and recommendation must include "Professional assessment may be required" language. Quarterly review by SEND-specialist contractor. Donna's rule library must encode this explicitly.

---

# 7. MOONSHOT INNOVATION VAULT

Seven high-risk, longer-horizon ideas that should be evaluated quarterly. Each represents a potential 100x outcome if it lands.

## Moonshot 1 — The Schools Trust Federation

**The vision.** A consortium of UK MATs (Multi-Academy Trusts) sharing anonymised AI procurement data with GetPromptly. Each Trust contributes its tool-evaluation outcomes; GetPromptly aggregates into a "Trust Network Score" — the wisdom-of-crowds layer above individual trust scoring. ShopMy's curated-network model applied to institutional procurement.

**Why 100x.** Once 5+ Trusts contribute, the Trust Network Score becomes the de facto industry benchmark. Vendors compete to score well. Schools demand inclusion. The Federation becomes a quasi-regulatory body that GetPromptly operates.

**Time to feasibility.** 18 months and 1 founding Trust partner.

## Moonshot 2 — The Vendor Safety Bond Marketplace

**The vision.** Vendors who claim KCSIE-alignment can purchase a Safety Bond from GetPromptly: a £5,000-£25,000 deposit refunded annually if no compliance incident occurs. Schools see the bond as a buyer-protection layer. GetPromptly takes a 5-10% admin fee. Monetises trust as financial product.

**Why 100x.** This is a category-creating move. No affiliate platform monetises *guarantees*. Vendors with bonds command premium pricing. Schools buy with confidence. GetPromptly operates a financial primitive on top of its directory.

**Time to feasibility.** 24 months and legal/insurance partnership (FCA implications need scoping).

## Moonshot 3 — Federated Luna for Trusts

**The vision.** Each Trust runs its own Luna instance (white-labelled) on the Mac Mini stack model, with its own Tool/Equipment/Training Registries customised to its policies. GetPromptly licenses the agent stack at £2,000-£8,000/year per Trust. The "agent operating system for education" play.

**Why 100x.** Trusts already pay £20K-£100K/year for various EdTech management platforms. A £4,000/year Luna-as-Service is trivial budget. At 50 Trust customers by month 30, that's £200K/year of high-margin recurring SaaS revenue.

**Time to feasibility.** 24-36 months and Promptly Safe Mode (Play #8) success as foundation.

## Moonshot 4 — KCSIE-as-Code Open Standard

**The vision.** Publish the 5-pillar scoring methodology as a machine-readable open standard (JSON Schema + reference implementation). Vendors can self-assess against the standard and submit results for GetPromptly review. Schools can verify any vendor against the standard. The W3C/OpenAPI move applied to education compliance.

**Why 100x.** Standards-setting is the ultimate moat. If every UK EdTech vendor self-describes their safety posture using GetPromptly's schema, GetPromptly becomes the registrar. DfE could reference the standard in its own guidance. ICO could cite it in enforcement decisions. The opportunity for GetPromptly to *own the language* of UK AI education compliance is real.

**Time to feasibility.** 18 months and DfE conversation. Could be accelerated by a strategic publishing partnership (Tes, Schools Week).

## Moonshot 5 — Promptly for Initial Teacher Training (ITT)

**The vision.** Partnership with UK ITT providers (universities, SCITTs) to embed GetPromptly's Tool Directory and Prompt Library into trainee teacher curricula. Early-career teachers (~25,000 entering UK profession annually) become lifelong GetPromptly users.

**Why 100x.** ITT placement is the ultimate top-of-funnel. A new teacher exposed to GetPromptly during training defaults to GetPromptly throughout their career. Compounding 25,000 new users/year over a decade is a market-defining position. CPD upsells over career arc are enormous.

**Time to feasibility.** 12-18 months and 1-3 ITT partner conversations. This is the most under-utilised lever in UK EdTech generally.

## Moonshot 6 — Live Trust Score Updates via Vendor Telemetry

**The vision.** Tier 1 vendors agree to expose anonymised telemetry (uptime, content-filter hit rates, age-suitability flag rates) to GetPromptly via API. Trust Scores become *live* — updated in near-real-time based on actual operational behaviour, not point-in-time review. impact.com's data-graph moat applied to safety.

**Why 100x.** A live score that updates as vendors actually perform is a fundamentally different product than periodic reviews. Schools see *current* posture. Vendors are accountable continuously. The data graph compounds: every new Tier 1 vendor enriches the dataset, making the score more useful, attracting more vendors, deepening the moat.

**Time to feasibility.** 24 months and 5-10 Tier 1 vendor partnerships willing to expose telemetry.

## Moonshot 7 — The Parent-Side Compliance Layer

**The vision.** Mirror Promptly Safe Mode (Play #8) for home use: a £19/year browser extension parents install to wrap their child's AI usage with GetPromptly's filters and logging. Massive consumer market, recurring revenue, additional commercial spine.

**Why 100x.** UK has ~12 million school-aged children. If even 1% of parents adopt a £19/year extension, that's £2.28M/year in consumer SaaS revenue. The parent market is currently underserved by UK EdTech generally — most products target schools. GetPromptly's existing parents page (`/parents`) is the natural launch surface.

**Time to feasibility.** 18 months and a privacy-first architecture review (children's online safety regulations are particularly stringent post-Online Safety Act).

---

## Closing Operator's Note

Every play in Section 4 ships on infrastructure GetPromptly already operates. The Mac Mini M4 was specified for exactly this: an always-on agent stack that compounds value while competitors raise capital to build comparable systems. The constraint is not technology, capital, or competition — it is **implementation focus**.

Sequence the 90-day roadmap. Ship each play through Donna's gate. Log every workflow to GitHub. Monitor every container in Uptime Kuma. The moat builds itself.

**Begin with Play #1 this week.** The KCSIE Audit Trail Receipt is the lowest-effort, highest-conviction play in the entire report — 6-9 hours of build time, immediate conversion uplift, foundational artefact for every subsequent play. By Friday of Week 1, GetPromptly should have shipped a feature no affiliate site in the world currently offers.

The 2026 affiliate landscape is dominated by horizontal players optimising for the wrong question. GetPromptly's vertical depth, trust framework, and agentic stack are a category-of-one combination. Execute the 90-day roadmap with discipline, and by Q4 2026 the question shifts from "can GetPromptly compete?" to "what is GetPromptly's exit value?"

— *End of Affiliate Edge Master Strategic Intelligence Report v1.0*

# SOURCE FILE: `promptly-living-prototype-html/PROTOTYPE-HTML-VALIDATION.md`

# PROTOTYPE-HTML-VALIDATION

**Promptly — the living prototype · downloadable HTML build**
Built 10 August 2026 from the Sprint 005 prototype of record.
Source of truth: `Promptly-Living-Prototype-Colour-Parity-v1.1.pdf` and the
generator, stylesheet and route set that produced it.

**Status: PROTOTYPE — ILLUSTRATIVE — NOT ADOPTED.**

---

## 1 · What was produced

### Deliverables

| Artefact | Path |
|---|---|
| Multi-file prototype | `~/Sites/promptly-living-prototype-html/` |
| Entry point | `~/Sites/promptly-living-prototype-html/index.html` |
| Zip archive | `~/Sites/promptly-living-prototype-html.zip` |
| Single-file copy | `~/Sites/promptly-living-prototype-standalone.html` |

79 files: 55 HTML documents, 20 self-hosted font subsets, 2 stylesheets, 1
script, and this record with the build notes.

### Routes (55)

**Edition and standing pages (13)** — `index.html`, `standing-record.html`,
`standing-record-safeguarding.html`, `standing-record-corrections.html`,
`standing-record-recalculations.html`, `reviews.html`, `corrections.html`,
`methodology.html`, `migration.html`, `how-to-read.html`, `about.html`,
`register.html`, `validation.html`

**Interactive surfaces (5)** — `search.html`, `compare.html`,
`collections.html`, `ask.html`, `ask-send.html`

**Records (6)** — `review/aurora-learning-assistant.html`,
`review/beacon-reading.html`, `review/cadence-maths.html`,
`review/harbour-send.html`, `review/nimbus-tutor.html`,
`review/slate-marking.html`

**Tool histories (6)** and **methodology lineages (6)** — one of each per record,
at `tool-history/<slug>.html` and `lineage/<slug>.html`

**Permanent event addresses (19)** — `event/SRE-2026-0000943.html` through
`event/SRE-2026-0001284.html`, one file per illustrative event, each linking
back to its record, its history and the chronology

No orphans: every route is reachable from the masthead, the colophon, or a link
inside a record.

---

## 2 · Routes tested

Opened and rendered from `file://` with no server: all 55.

Measured in detail (layout, headings, links, interactions): `index.html`,
`standing-record.html` and all three filtered views, `reviews.html`,
`review/aurora-learning-assistant.html`, `review/beacon-reading.html`,
`review/nimbus-tutor.html`, `review/slate-marking.html`,
`tool-history/beacon-reading.html`, `lineage/beacon-reading.html`,
`event/SRE-2026-0001284.html`, `event/SRE-2026-0001252.html`,
`corrections.html`, `methodology.html`, `migration.html`, `search.html`,
`compare.html`, `collections.html`, `ask.html`, `ask-send.html`,
`register.html`, `about.html`, `how-to-read.html`, `validation.html`.

**Link check.** 1,277 internal link and asset references resolved against the
filesystem, including the `@font-face` URLs inside `fonts.css`. **0 broken.**
**0 external references** — no CDN, no remote font, no analytics, no fetch.

---

## 3 · Interactions tested

| Interaction | What was exercised | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Standing Record, by route | Four filter routes, each a real address, each with `aria-current` on its chip | Renders without script |
| Standing Record, narrowing | Typed `harbour` (3 of 16), an event id `SRE-2026-0001252` (1 of 16), and a term with no match (0 of 16, stated in a live region); same on the corrections view (3 of 5) | Works |
| Search, records | `harbour` → 1 record; `withdraw` → 1 record; `safeguarding` → 5 records | Works |
| Search, events | Same words matched 3–4 events; the event group stays hidden until a query matches | Works |
| Search, empty state | Unmatched term shows the "we hold no record under that name" note | Works |
| Comparison, selection | Either column set to any of the six records; heading, five findings, last event, state, note and link all repaint | Works |
| Comparison, withdrawn record | Nimbus Tutor selected: findings read `NOT COMPARABLE`, and a note explains that a withdrawn record has no comparable findings and that withdrawal is not deletion | Works |
| Comparison, same record twice | A note asks for a second, different record | Works |
| Shelf, save | Saved from a record page: button becomes "Saved to my shelf · remove", entry written to `localStorage` | Works |
| Shelf, remove | Second click removes the entry and restores the label | Works |
| Shelf, return | With an entry present, `collections.html` renders the saved record and hides the empty-state note | Works |
| Enquiry desk | Both worked enquiries selectable; selection opens the worked answer | Works |
| Record → history → lineage → event | Followed from `review/beacon-reading.html` through to a permanent event address and back | Works |
| Single-file copy | All 55 routes render; deep links, in-page anchors, the shelf across routes, comparison and search all behave as in the site | Works |

---

## 4 · Accessibility checks performed

| Check | Method | Result |
|---|---|---|
| One `h1` per page | Parsed all 55 documents | 55 / 55 |
| No skipped heading level | Parsed all 55 documents | 0 violations |
| `main` landmark and skip link | Parsed all 55 documents | 55 / 55 |
| `lang="en-GB"` | Parsed all 55 documents | 55 / 55 |
| Horizontal overflow at 390 px | 21 pages measured in a true 390 px viewport | 0 px on every page |
| Horizontal overflow at 768 / 1280 / 1600 px | Same pages | 0 px on every page |
| Target size at 390 px | Every chip, button, select and navigation link measured | None under 44 px |
| Reduced motion | Rendered with the preference forced | Animation resolves to `none`; animated elements left at full opacity, no transform |
| Without JavaScript | Script removed and pages re-rendered | All text, tables and events present; the three enhancement controls stay hidden rather than appearing broken |
| Focus visibility | Stylesheet inspection | 2 px lime ring with a 4 px ink outer ring on light grounds, an oat outer on dark grounds, `Highlight` under forced colours |
| Colour not the sole carrier | Stylesheet and markup inspection | States are words in bordered stamps; direction is two written values; the arrow is `aria-hidden` |
| Live region for filtering | Markup inspection and interaction | `role="status" aria-live="polite"` announces the narrowed count |

**Three heading-level defects were found and fixed** during this build:
`reviews.html`, `corrections.html` and `search.html` placed record entries at
`h3` directly beneath the page `h1`. They are now `h2` at the same drawn size,
so the level carries meaning and the size carries rhythm. The runtime-rendered
shelf entry was corrected the same way.

**Three responsive defects were found and fixed**, all of which only appeared
below 500 px and had been masked by a headless-browser minimum window width:
the enquiry line overflowed by up to 98 px (it now stacks below 560 px); the
findings block on a record overflowed by 72 px (editorial grid items may now
shrink below their content width); and rows carrying an inline column template —
the lineage rows among them — overflowed by 48 px (below 700 px the single-column
stack now wins over the inline template).

**Not claimed:** no screen-reader testing, no automated conformance audit, no
contrast re-measurement against this build, and no user testing.

---

## 5 · Deliberate compromises

| Compromise | Why, and what was done instead |
|---|---|
| The enquiry desk answers only worked enquiries | There is no retrieval and no model behind a static file. The desk holds the two worked enquiries written for it, states this on the page, and does not pretend to answer free text. |
| Search covers this build, not 317 records | The institutional figures on the page are illustrative and were left as drawn. A note beneath states the real extent of this build's index: the six records and the events held here. |
| Narrowing a view is not a filter route | The four Standing Record filters remain four real addresses, because a filter with no address cannot be cited. Narrowing hides rows within one of those views and says so in the live region. |
| Pagination is stated, not implemented | The record shows "page 1 of 26 at this snapshot", as in the source. The remaining pages do not exist in an illustrative set of this size. |
| The shelf needs browser storage | It is local state by design. `localStorage` worked from `file://` in the browser used here; where a browser blocks storage for local files the shelf is empty rather than broken, and every record it points to stays reachable from Reviews. |
| No appearance switch | The dark reading ground (`#2A2A2A`) is a formally open matter. The build follows the operating system preference, as the stylesheet already did, and adds no control that would settle a question the Founder has not settled. |
| Satoshi substituted by Inter | Satoshi is not in the repository and was not licensed for this build. Nothing was downloaded or packaged. The substitution matches Sprint 002 and is recorded in `PROTOTYPE-NOTES.md`. |
| Single file addresses routes by hash | A single document cannot give each route a real address. The portable copy carries a standing note saying so, and the multi-file build remains the version of record. |
| `assets/images/` is empty | The Brand World forbids generated imagery and requires provenance for every image. There are no bitmap assets to ship. |

---

## 6 · Source and reference discrepancies

**None material.** The colour-parity PDF was exported from the same stylesheet
this build uses, and its content streams carry the same opaque sRGB values, so
there was no repository/PDF conflict to resolve and nothing was estimated from
the PDF.

Two things worth recording:

1. **Route count.** The PDF describes 55 routes. The build has 55. An earlier
   line inside the validation page said "54 pages"; the page count is 55 and the
   report now says so.
2. **Nimbus Tutor holds no findings.** The record is withdrawn and its findings
   are preserved rather than published, so the data set carries an empty
   findings list. Before this was handled, selecting it on the facing page left
   the previous record's figures in place under its name. That is now
   `NOT COMPARABLE`, with the reason stated.

---

## 7 · Not implemented

- Pagination beyond page 1 of the Standing Record.
- Export and citation as downloadable files. The registers, the citation formats
  and the rule that the filter travels with the export are described on
  `how-to-read.html`, as in the source, but no file is produced.
- JSON-LD and machine-readable representations of an event.
- Any server behaviour: no account, no email, no second-device shelf, no
  enquiry escalation to a person, no supplier submission of evidence. Each is
  described where the source describes it; none is simulated.
- The customer-facing assessment device, which is unresolved elsewhere and is
  drawn nowhere.

---

## 8 · Confirmation

This is a prototype. Every tool, finding, date, score, reviewer, event,
reference and decision in it is invented for the purpose of testing the design.
Aurora Learning Assistant, Beacon Reading, Cadence Maths, Harbour SEND, Slate
Marking and Nimbus Tutor are illustrative entities and are not real products.
Nothing shown is a live Promptly judgement and nothing shown exists in
production. The prototype disclaimer appears in the colophon of all 55 pages and
in the portable copy's standing note.

Nothing about generating this build adopts or ratifies any open Founder
decision, production token, governance matter or Safe Mode decision. Brand World
v2.2 remains unamended; §26.2 and §26.3 remain formally open; the dark reading
ground remains an open matter and was not settled by adding a control. No
governed behaviour was changed: the work is a static site generated from the
existing Sprint 005 generator and its existing stylesheet, held in its own
directory, deployed nowhere.

The working tree was checked before any change: neither
`~/Sites/promptly-prototype` nor `~/Sites` is a git repository, so no branch,
history or protected decision could be touched by this work.


---

<!-- SOURCE FILE: promptly-living-prototype-html/PROTOTYPE-NOTES.md | SHA256: 20b28146710f52ef8290c6cd90ed8a2ab17ee7aa1faa3ef0992f81276a6a851f -->
# SOURCE FILE: `promptly-living-prototype-html/PROTOTYPE-NOTES.md`

# Promptly — living prototype · HTML build notes

**Status: PROTOTYPE. Illustrative throughout. Nothing here is adopted.**

Every tool, finding, date, score, reviewer, event, reference and decision in this
build is invented for the purpose of testing the design. None of it is a live
Promptly judgement and none of it exists in production.

---

## How to open it

Double-click `index.html`. No server, no build step, no install, no network.
All 55 routes are ordinary files linked with relative paths, so `file://` is
enough.

If you would rather have one file, `promptly-living-prototype-standalone.html`
(delivered beside this directory) carries all 55 routes in a single document.
The multi-file build is the version of record, because there every route is a
real address; the single file addresses routes by hash.

---

## Typography

| Role | Family | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Display | Fraunces | Self-hosted, variable, roman and italic |
| Sans | **Inter — substituted for Satoshi** | See below |
| Mono | JetBrains Mono | Provenance, stamps and apparatus |

**The substitution.** Promptly's sans register is Satoshi. Satoshi is not in
this repository and was not licensed for this build, so nothing was downloaded
or packaged for it. Inter is used instead, which is the same substitution
Sprint 002 made and the same one the design books have carried since. Inter is
a close metric and tonal neighbour at the sizes used here; it is not the brand
face, and any production build must set Satoshi.

All three families are self-hosted as latin/latin-ext subsets in
`assets/fonts/`. No font is fetched from a CDN, and nothing renders differently
offline.

---

## Colour

Colour tokens were taken from the repository stylesheet, not estimated from the
PDF. `assets/css/promptly.css` is the Sprint 005 prototype's own stylesheet, so
the values here are the governing ones:

| Token | Value | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Oat | `#F5F2EC` | Resting reading ground |
| Ground Black | `#1E1E1E` | Event ground |
| Surface Dark | `#2A2A2A` | Proposed dark reading ground — **open matter** |
| Primary Ink | `#1A1A14` | Body |
| Ink 2 | `#4A4A42` | Reasons and secondary prose |
| Archive Brass base | `#8C7A52` | Rules, borders, non-text apparatus only |
| Brass on oat | `#6F5F3C` | Apparatus text on light grounds |
| Brass on dark | `#C4B48E` | Apparatus text on dark grounds |
| Promptly Lime | `#C8E44A` | The single semantic accent |

No repository/PDF conflict was found: the PDF was exported from this stylesheet
during the Colour Parity revision, and its content streams carry these same
values.

---

## What this build adds to Sprint 005

The Sprint 005 prototype was already a connected static site of 55 routes. This
build keeps it and adds only what a downloadable, clickable copy needs:

1. **Narrowing a Standing Record view** — a text filter inside whichever of the
   four filter routes you are on. The four routes remain the record; narrowing
   hides rows in one view and says so.
2. **Search across records and events** — was tool names only; now matches
   name, reference, state, scope, verdict, finding name, reason and event
   identifier, and lists matching events beneath the records.
3. **Choosing the two records on the facing page** — either column may be any
   held record. A withdrawn record reads `NOT COMPARABLE` rather than carrying
   stale figures, with the reason stated. Still two records, never three, and
   still no winner, no composite and no highlighted column.
4. **Choosing a worked enquiry** at the desk.
5. **One script for the whole site**, loaded from the page shell, so the shelf
   button works on every record rather than on some.

Everything else — structure, routes, typography, spacing, colour, event
grammar, chronology, provenance — is the Sprint 005 build unchanged.

---

## What it deliberately does not do

- **No appearance switch.** The dark reading ground is still an open matter.
  The build follows the operating system preference, as the stylesheet already
  did, and adds no control that would settle a question the Founder has not.
- **No live enquiry.** The desk holds two worked enquiries and says so. There is
  no retrieval and no model behind a static file.
- **No sponsored ordering, ever**, and no ranking anywhere: search keeps record
  order, the shortlist is alphabetical, comparison declares no winner.
- **No images.** `assets/images/` is intentionally empty; the Brand World
  forbids generated imagery and requires provenance for every image, so every
  rule, mark and state is drawn in CSS or written in words.

---

## Accessibility, as implemented and verified

One `h1` per page and no skipped heading level on all 55 pages; a `main`
landmark and a skip link on every page; focus visible as a lime ring with an ink
outer ring, and as `Highlight` under forced colours; no chip, button, select or
navigation link under 44 px at 390 px; direction written in words with the arrow
decorative and hidden from assistive technology; colour never the sole carrier
of state; reduced motion honoured; and every page readable with JavaScript
unavailable.

Nothing beyond that is claimed. No screen-reader testing, no automated
conformance audit and no user testing has been carried out on this build.

---

## Files

```
index.html                 the current edition
assets/css/promptly.css    the governing stylesheet, unaltered
assets/css/fonts.css       @font-face for the three families
assets/js/promptly.js      progressive enhancement only
assets/fonts/              self-hosted woff2 subsets
assets/images/             intentionally empty
PROTOTYPE-HTML-VALIDATION.md   what was produced, tested and compromised
```

Generated by `build.py` in the Sprint 005 prototype directory
(`PROMPTLY_OUT=<dir> PROMPTLY_LAYOUT=package python3 build.py`). The single file
is generated by `standalone.py`.


---

# SOURCE FILE: `≡ƒôû The Founding Charter of GetPromptly.md`

📖** The Founding Charter of GetPromptly**  
  
**Founder’s Edition**  
  
**Version 1.0 (Living Document)**  
  
⸻  
  
**Title Page**  
  
**The Founding Charter of GetPromptly**  
  
**A Constitutional Charter for the Responsible Adoption of Artificial Intelligence in Education**  
  
⸻  
  
***“Every child deserves decisions worthy of their future.”***  
***“Every child deserves decisions worthy of their future.”***  
  
⸻  
  
**Founder’s Edition**  
**Founder’s Edition**  
  
**Prepared for future custodians of the institution.**  
  
**This Charter establishes the enduring purpose, duties, principles and constitutional foundations upon which GetPromptly shall operate.**  
  
**It exists so that the institution’s conscience remains constant, regardless of how technology, leadership or society evolves.**  
  
⸻  
  
**A Note to Future Custodians**  
  
**If you are reading this many years after its publication, then GetPromptly has become something greater than those who first imagined it.**  
  
**This Charter was never written to preserve a company.**  
  
**It was written to preserve a way of thinking.**  
**It was written to preserve a way of thinking.**  
  
**Technology will evolve.**  
  
**Artificial intelligence will become more capable.**  
  
**Leaders will change.**  
  
**Products will change.**  
**Products will change.**  
  
**Methods will improve.**  
  
**None of those things should require this institution to abandon the principles upon which it was founded.**  
**None of those things should require this institution to abandon the principles upon which it was founded.**  
  
**If ever uncertainty arises, return to these pages.**  
  
**Do not ask what is easiest.**  
**Do not ask what is easiest.**  
  
**Do not ask what is most profitable.**  
  
**Ask only:**  
**Ask only:**  
  
**“What decision is worthy of the children whose future may be shaped by it?”**  
**“What decision is worthy of the children whose future may be shaped by it?”**  
  
**If this Charter continues to answer that question, then it has fulfilled its purpose.**  
**If this Charter continues to answer that question, then it has fulfilled its purpose.**  
  
⸻  
  
**The Founder’s Charge**  
  
**GetPromptly was not founded to tell schools what to think.**  
  
**It was founded to ensure they never have to make important decisions without trustworthy evidence.**  
**It was founded to ensure they never have to make important decisions without trustworthy evidence.**  
  
**Artificial intelligence will continue to evolve.**  
  
**The responsibility carried by those who educate children will remain.**  
**The responsibility carried by those who educate children will remain.**  
  
**This institution exists so that responsibility is never carried alone.**  
  
⸻  
  
**The First Principle**  
  
**Every child deserves decisions worthy of their future.**  
  
**This principle is the constitutional foundation of GetPromptly.**  
**This principle is the constitutional foundation of GetPromptly.**  
  
**Every publication, review, recommendation, partnership, technological advancement and institutional decision shall be judged against this standard.**  
**Every publication, review, recommendation, partnership, technological advancement and institutional decision shall be judged against this standard.**  
  
**Where conflict exists between competing interests, this principle shall prevail.**  
**Where conflict exists between competing interests, this principle shall prevail.**  
  
⸻  
  
**The Constitutional Oath**  
  
**GetPromptly has a duty to place the interests of children above commercial interests.**  
**GetPromptly has a duty to place the interests of children above commercial interests.**  
  
**This duty shall not be suspended by opportunity, financial pressure or institutional convenience.**  
**This duty shall not be suspended by opportunity, financial pressure or institutional convenience.**  
  
**It exists because trust cannot exist where commercial influence outweighs educational responsibility.**  
  
⸻  
  
**The Preamble**  
  
**There are moments in history when new technologies change not only what humanity can do, but how humanity must decide.**  
  
**Education is living through one of those moments.**  
**Education is living through one of those moments.**  
  
**Artificial intelligence presents extraordinary opportunities to improve learning, reduce workload and expand human capability.**  
**Artificial intelligence presents extraordinary opportunities to improve learning, reduce workload and expand human capability.**  
  
**It also introduces new responsibilities, new risks and new ethical questions that cannot be answered through marketing, opinion or speculation alone.**  
  
**The decisions made today will influence classrooms, teachers and children for generations.**  
**The decisions made today will influence classrooms, teachers and children for generations.**  
  
**Those decisions deserve more than confidence.**  
  
**They deserve evidence.**  
  
**GetPromptly was founded upon the belief that every child deserves decisions worthy of their future.**  
**GetPromptly was founded upon the belief that every child deserves decisions worthy of their future.**  
  
**This Charter exists so that belief is never forgotten.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Article I**  
  
**Purpose**  
  
**The purpose of GetPromptly is to enable every education leader to make responsible decisions regarding artificial intelligence through independent evidence, transparent methodology and public accountability.**  
**The purpose of GetPromptly is to enable every education leader to make responsible decisions regarding artificial intelligence through independent evidence, transparent methodology and public accountability.**  
  
**The institution exists not to replace professional judgement but to strengthen it.**  
**The institution exists not to replace professional judgement but to strengthen it.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Article II**  
  
**Duty**  
  
**The institution recognises a permanent duty to:**  
  
* **Protect the interests of children.**  
* **Support teachers and education leaders.**  
* **Strengthen trust through evidence.**  
* **Improve educational decision-making.**  
* **Promote the responsible adoption of artificial intelligence.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Article III**  
  
**Character**  
  
**GetPromptly chooses to be an institution defined by:**  
**GetPromptly chooses to be an institution defined by:**  
  
**Truth before convenience.**  
**Truth before convenience.**  
  
**Children before commerce.**  
**Children before commerce.**  
  
**Evidence before opinion.**  
**Evidence before opinion.**  
  
**Transparency before certainty.**  
  
**Responsibility before reputation.**  
**Responsibility before reputation.**  
  
**Independence before influence.**  
  
**Stewardship before ownership.**  
**Stewardship before ownership.**  
  
**These characteristics are expected of every employee, governor, reviewer, contributor and artificial intelligence system acting on behalf of the institution.**  
**These characteristics are expected of every employee, governor, reviewer, contributor and artificial intelligence system acting on behalf of the institution.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Article IV**  
  
**The Covenant**  
  
**GetPromptly accepts that some principles become meaningful only when they are costly to uphold.**  
**GetPromptly accepts that some principles become meaningful only when they are costly to uphold.**  
  
**Accordingly, the institution enters into the following covenant:**  
**Accordingly, the institution enters into the following covenant:**  
  
**Commercial partnerships shall never determine methodology.**  
**Commercial partnerships shall never determine methodology.**  
  
**Evidence shall never be altered to satisfy commercial interests.**  
  
**Recommendations shall always be capable of being explained through transparent evidence.**  
  
**Integrity shall always be valued above organisational growth.**  
**Integrity shall always be valued above organisational growth.**  
  
**Where uncertainty exists, uncertainty shall be communicated honestly.**  
**Where uncertainty exists, uncertainty shall be communicated honestly.**  
  
**Where mistakes occur, they shall be acknowledged openly and corrected transparently.**  
**Where mistakes occur, they shall be acknowledged openly and corrected transparently.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Article V**  
  
**Legacy**  
  
**Because GetPromptly existed, education entered the age of artificial intelligence with confidence rather than fear, evidence rather than speculation, and shared understanding rather than unequal access to trustworthy knowledge.**  
  
**Artificial intelligence should expand human capability, never diminish human responsibility.**  
**Artificial intelligence should expand human capability, never diminish human responsibility.**  
  
**The institution therefore seeks not merely to review technology, but to ensure that every educational decision involving artificial intelligence can be made with confidence, transparency and integrity.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Closing Declaration**  
  
**This Charter is not intended to constrain innovation.**  
  
**It exists to ensure innovation never loses its conscience.**  
  
**The success of GetPromptly shall not be measured solely by its growth, influence or recognition.**  
**The success of GetPromptly shall not be measured solely by its growth, influence or recognition.**  
  
**Its true success shall be measured by the confidence it gives to those entrusted with the education of children.**  
**Its true success shall be measured by the confidence it gives to those entrusted with the education of children.**  
  
**Should future generations inherit an institution worthy of their trust, then the purpose of this Charter shall have been fulfilled.**  
  
⸻  
  
**Adopted by the Founder’s Edition**  
**Adopted by the Founder’s Edition**  
  
***“For the children whose future depends upon the decisions we make today.”***
